Hilbert Polynomials of Noncanonical Orthogonal


Abstract
By applying Fourier transformations to the natural orthogonal oscillator representations of special linear Lie algebras, Luo and the second author (2013) obtained a large family of infinite-dimensional irreducible representations of the algebras on the homogeneous solutions of the Laplace equation. In our earlier work, we proved that the associated varieties of these irreducible representations are the intersection of determinantal varieties. In this paper, we find the Hilbert polynomial 𝔭 M ( k ) \mathfrak{p}_{{}_{M}}(k) of these associated varieties. Moreover, we show that the Hilbert polynomial 𝔭 M , M 0 ( k ) \mathfrak{p}_{{}_{M},_{M_{0}}}(k) of such an irreducible module M M with respect to any generating subspace M 0 M_{0} satisfies 𝔭 M ( k ) ≤ 𝔭 M , M 0 ( k ) \mathfrak{p}_{{}_{M}}(k)\leq\mathfrak{p}_{{}_{M},_{M_{0}}}(k) for sufficiently large positive integer k k and find a necessary and sufficient condition that the equality holds. Furthermore, we explicitly determine the leading term of 𝔭 M , M 0 ( k ) \mathfrak{p}_{{}_{M},_{M_{0}}}(k) , which is independent of the choice of M 0 M_{0} . Keywords : special linear Lie algebra; orthogonal oscillator representation; Hilbert polynomial; Hilbert series; determinantal varieties.



Oscillator Representations of
sl(n)

1
Introduction

Let
\mathscr{A}=\bigoplus^{\infty}_{k=0}\mathscr{A}_{k}
be a finitely generated graded commutative algebra over a field
\mathbb{F}
. The Hilbert function maps a nonnegative integer
k
to the dimension of the subspace
\mathscr{A}_{k}
.
The Hilbert-Poincaré series is the formal series

{\mathfrak{s}}_{{}_{\mathscr{A}}}(t)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(\dim\mathscr{A}_{k})t^{k}.

(1.1)

Hilbert
[
17
]
(1893) showed that there exists a unique polynomial
{\mathfrak{p}}_{\mathscr{A}}(k)
with rational coefficients, which coincides with the Hilbert function
\dim\mathscr{A}_{k}
for sufficiently large
k
. This polynomial is later called
Hilbert polynomial
.
Moreover, he
[
17
]
also introduced the notions of Hilbert functions and Hilbert series for a finitely generated graded module over the ring of polynomials in finitely many variables over a field.
Northcott
[
28
]
(1947) gave a direct proof of abstract Tauberian theorems with applications to power series and Hilbert series.
In 1971, Bernstein
[
3
]
defined a variety associated with a filtered module of the algebra of differential operators, in order to construct the fundamental solution of a linear partial differential equation with constant coefficients. He also showed that the dimension of the associated variety is equal to the Gelfand-Kirillov dimension of the module in
[
3
]
, which is the degree of the Hilbert polynomial plus one. Vogan
[
31
]
(1978) developed particularly refined techniques
in the description of the leading coefficient in the Hilbert-Samuel polynomial of a Harish-Chandra module for a semisimple Lie algebra and its maximal compact subalgebra.

Anic
[
1
]
(1985) modeled Diophantine equations within the category of graded Hopf algebras and used their Hilbert series to determine undecidable spaces. Enright and Willenbring
[
9
]
(2004) found a connection among Hilbert series, Howe duality and branching for classical groups. Moreover, Enright and Hunziker
[
7
]
(2004) computed (minimal) resolutions and explicit formulas for the Hilbert series of the unitary highest weight modules of exceptional groups. They also
[
8
]
calculated the Hilbert series of the Wallach representations and provided explicit formulas for the Hilbert series of determinantal varieties by using BGG resolutions of unitarizable highest weight modules. Gross and Wallach
[
13
]
(2011) studied the Hilbert polynomials and Hilbert series of homogeneous projective varieties. There are also many other interesting significant works related to Hilbert polynomials and Hilbert series (e.g., cf.
[
4
,
5
,
10
,
11
,
14
,
16
,
19
,
20
,
21
,
22
,
23
,
24
,
25
,
27
,
29
,
30
,
32
]
).

Indeed, Hilbert polynomials and Hilbert series have played important roles in physics. For instance, Hanany and Kalveks
[
15
]
(2014) found an efficient encoding and analysis of the Hilbert series of the vacuum moduli spaces of instantons. Cremonesi, Mekareeya and Zaffaroni
[
6
]
(2016) presented a formula for the Hilbert series that counts gauge invariant chiral operators in a large class of
3d\;{\cal N}\geq 2
Yang-Mills-Chern-Simons theories. Gráf, Henning, Lu, Melia and Murayama
[
12
]
(2023) calculated the Hilbert series in connection with
the Higgs mechanism and high energy field theories. Kho and Seong
[
21
]
(2024) studied the algebraic structure of mesonic moduli spaces of bipartite field theories by computing the Hilbert series.

By applying Fourier transformations to the natural orthogonal oscillator representations of special linear Lie algebras, Luo and the second author
[
26
]
(2013) obtained a large family of infinite-dimensional irreducible representations of the algebras on homogeneous solutions of the Laplace equation. In our earlier work
[
34
]
, we proved that the associated varieties of these irreducible representations are the intersections of explicitly given determinantal varieties. In this paper, we find the Hilbert polynomial
\mathfrak{p}_{{}_{M}}(k)
of these associated varieties. Moreover, we show that the Hilbert polynomial
\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)
of such an irreducible module
M
with respect to any generating subspace
M_{0}
satisfies
\mathfrak{p}_{{}_{M}}(k)\leq\mathfrak{p}_{{}_{M},_{M_{0}}}(k)
for sufficiently large positive integer
k
and find a necessary and sufficient condition that
the equality holds. Furthermore, we explicitly determine the leading term of
\mathfrak{p}_{{}_{M},_{M_{0}}}(k)
, which is independent of the choice of
M_{0}
and therefore is an invariant of
M
.

Let
\mathbb{F}
be a field with characteristic 0, and let
n>1
be an integer. Denote by
\mathscr{A}=\mathbb{F}[x_{1},x_{2},...,x_{n},y_{1},y_{2},...,y_{n}]
the polynomial algebra in
2n
variables. Let
E_{r,s}
be the square matrix with 1 as its
(r,s)
-entry and 0
as the others. The special linear Lie algebra

sl(n)=\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}(\mathbb{F}E_{i,j}+\mathbb{F}E_{j,i})+\sum_{r=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{F}(E_{r,r}-E_{r+1,r+1})

(1.2)

with the commutator as its Lie bracket.
Denote by
\mathbb{N}
the set of nonnegative integers. For any
two integers
p\leq q
, we denote
\overline{p,q}=\{p,p+1,\cdots,q\}
.
The natural orthogonal oscillator representation
\pi
of
sl(n)
on
\mathscr{A}
is given as follows:

\pi(E_{i,j})=x_{i}\partial_{x_{j}}-y_{j}\partial_{y_{i}}\qquad\mbox{for}\;\;i,j\in\overline{1,n}.

(1.3)

With respect to the above representation, the Laplace operator

\Delta=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\partial_{x_{i}}\partial_{y_{i}}.

(1.4)

For
\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
, we denote by
\mathscr{A}_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}
the subspace of homogeneous polynomials with the degree
\ell_{1}
in
\{x_{1},x_{2},...,x_{n}\}
and the degree
\ell_{2}
in
\{y_{1},y_{2},...,y_{n}\}
. Then
\mathscr{A}=\bigoplus_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\in\mathbb{N}}\mathscr{A}_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}
is an
\mathbb{N}^{2}
-graded algebra.

Set the subspace of homogeneous harmonic polynomials as follows:

{\mathscr{H}}_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}=\{f\in{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}\mid\Delta(f)=0\}.

(1.5)

Then
{\mathscr{H}}_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}}
forms an irreducible
sl(n)
-module with highest weight
\ell_{1}\lambda_{1}+\ell_{2}\lambda_{n-1}
(e.g., cf.
[
33
]
), where
\lambda_{i}
denotes the
i
th fundamental weight of
sl(n)
.

Denote by
\mathscr{F}_{z}
the Fourier transformation on
z
. Fix
n_{1},n_{2}\in\overline{1,n}
with
n_{1}\leq n_{2}
. Letting (
1.3
) act on the space

{\mathscr{A}}^{\prime}=\mathscr{F}_{x_{1}}\cdots\mathscr{F}_{x_{n_{1}}}\mathscr{F}_{x_{n_{2}+1}}\cdots\mathscr{F}_{x_{n}}(\mathscr{A})

(1.6)

of distributions, one can obtain a large family of infinite-dimensional irreducible representations of
sl(n)
on certain spaces of homogeneous solutions of the Laplace equation. However, this picture is not convenient to be dealt with. Instead, we consider another equivalent representation as follows.
In the representation formulas given in (
1.3
), applying the Fourier transformations on operators:

\partial_{x_{r}}\mapsto-x_{r},\;x_{r}\mapsto\partial_{x_{r}},\;\partial_{y_{s}}\mapsto-y_{s},\;y_{s}\mapsto\partial_{y_{s}}\qquad\mbox{for}\;r\in\overline{1,n_{1}},\;s\in\overline{n_{2}+1,n},

(1.7)

we obtain a new representation
\tilde{\pi}
on
\mathscr{A}
. The representations
(\pi,\mathscr{A}^{\prime})
and
(\tilde{\pi},\mathscr{A})
are equivalent (or isomorphic).
Under the changes in (
1.7
), the Laplace operator becomes

\tilde{\Delta}=\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}x_{i}\partial_{y_{i}}-\sum_{r=n_{1}+1}^{n_{2}}\partial_{x_{r}}\partial_{y_{r}}+\sum_{s=n_{2}+1}^{n}y_{s}\partial_{x_{s}}.

(1.8)

Take the degree of
\{x_{1},...,x_{n_{1}},y_{n_{2}+1},...,y_{n}\}
as
-1
and that of
\{x_{n_{1}+1},...,x_{n},y_{1}...,y_{n_{2}}\}

as 1. Denote by
\mathbb{Z}
the ring of integers. For
\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
, we denote by
\mathscr{A}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
the subspace of homogeneous polynomials with the degree
\ell_{1}
in
\{x_{1},x_{2},...,x_{n}\}
and the degree
\ell_{2}
in
\{y_{1},y_{2},...,y_{n}\}
.
Then
\mathscr{A}=\bigoplus_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathscr{A}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
is a
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
-graded algebra. Set

{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}=\{f\in{\mathscr{A}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}\mid\tilde{\Delta}(f)=0\}.

(1.9)

The following is a result from
[
26
]
:

Proposition 1

For

\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
, the necessary and sufficient condition for
{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
to be an infinite-dimensional irreducible
sl(n)
-module is as follows:

(1) When
n_{1}+1<n_{2}
, (a)
\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}\leq n_{1}-n_{2}+1
; (b)
n_{2}=n
,
\ell_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
and
\ell_{2}=0
; (c)
n_{2}=n
,
\ell_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
and
\ell_{1}\geq n_{1}-n+2
;

(2) If
n_{1}+1=n_{2}
,
\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}\leq 0
or
n_{2}=n
and

0\leq\ell_{2}\leq\ell_{1}
;

(3) In the case
n_{1}=n_{2}
,
\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}\leq 0
and: (a)

\ell_{2}\leq 0
,
n_{1}<n-1
and
n\geq 3
;
(b)
\ell_{1}\leq 0
,
1<n_{1}<n
and
n\geq 3
; (c)
\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\leq 0
,
n_{1}=1
and
n=2
.

The above irreducible modules are infinite-dimensional highest weight modules with distinct highest weights. For instance, if
n_{1}+1<n_{2}<n

and
m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
with
m_{1}+m_{2}\geq n_{2}-n_{1}-1
,
{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle-m_{1},-m_{2}\rangle}
has the highest-weight vector

x_{n_{1}}^{m_{1}}y_{n_{2}+1}^{m_{2}}
of weight

m_{1}\lambda_{n_{1}-1}-(m_{1}+1)\lambda_{n_{1}}-(m_{2}+1)\lambda_{n_{2}}+m_{2}(1-\delta_{n_{2},n-1})\lambda_{n_{2}+1}
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be a finite-dimensional semisimple Lie algebra and let
U(\mathfrak{g})
be the universal enveloping algebra of
\mathfrak{g}
. Denote

U_{0}(\mathfrak{g})=\mathbb{F}
and for
m>0
with
m\in\mathbb{Z}
,
U_{m}(\mathfrak{g})=\mathbb{F}+\sum_{r=1}^{m}\mathfrak{g}^{r}
. Then
\{U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})\mid k\in\mathbb{N}\}
forms a filtration of
U(\mathfrak{g})
. Suppose that
M
is a finitely generated
U(\mathfrak{g})
-module and
M_{0}
is a finite-dimensional subspace of
M
satisfying

(U(\mathfrak{g}))(M_{0})=M.

(1.10)

Setting
M_{k}=(U_{k}(\mathfrak{g}))(M_{0})
, we get a filtration
\{M_{k}\mid k\in\mathbb{N}\}
of
M
.

The associated graded module is given by

\overline{M}=\mbox{gr}(M;M_{0})=\bigoplus_{k=0}^{\infty}\overline{M_{k}}\quad\text{with}\quad\overline{M}_{k}=M_{k}/M_{k-1},

(1.11)

and it is a finitely generated graded
S(\mathfrak{g})
-module. Here we identify
S(\mathfrak{g})
with
\mathbb{F}[\mathfrak{g}^{*}]
, the polynomial ring over
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
, in the canonical way through the Killing form of
\mathfrak{g}
. The annihilator of
\overline{M}
in
S(\mathfrak{g})
is defined as

\operatorname{Ann}_{S(\mathfrak{g})}(\overline{M})=\{\xi\in S(\mathfrak{g})\mid\xi(v)=0,\;\forall\;v\in\overline{M}\},

(1.12)

which is a graded ideal of
S(\mathfrak{g})
. It defines the associated variety in the dual space
\mathfrak{g}^{*}
:

{\mathscr{V}}(M)=\{f\in\mathfrak{g}^{*}\mid u(f)=0,\;\forall\;u\in\operatorname{Ann}_{S(\mathfrak{g})}(\overline{M})\}.

(1.13)

The variety does not depend on the choice of the subspace
M_{0}
satisfying (
1.10
).
In this paper, we define the Hilbert polynomial and Hilbert series of the coordinate ring
S(\mathfrak{g})/\sqrt{\operatorname{Ann}_{S(\mathfrak{g})}(\overline{M})}
as the Hilbert polynomial
\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)
and the Hilbert series
\mathfrak{s}_{M}(t)
of the associated variety
{\mathscr{V}}(M)
.

The followings are our main theorems in this paper:

Theorem 1

If
n_{1}\leq n_{2}<n
,
\ell_{1}\leq 0
or
\ell_{2}\leq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{s}_{{{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}}}(t)=\frac{\sum\limits_{r,s=0}\limits^{\infty}\left|\begin{matrix}\binom{n_{2}-2}{r}\binom{n-n_{2}-1}{r}&\binom{n_{2}-2}{r-1}\binom{n-n_{2}}{r}\\
\binom{n_{1}-1}{s+1}\binom{n-n_{1}-2}{s}&\binom{n_{1}-1}{s}\binom{n-n_{1}-1}{s}\end{matrix}\right|t^{r+s}}{(1-t)^{2n-3}}

(1.14)

If
n_{1}<n_{2}=n
,

\mathfrak{s}_{{{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}}}(t)=\frac{\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{n-n_{2}-1}{r}\binom{n_{1}-1}{r}t^{r}}{\left(1-t\right)^{n-n_{2}+n_{1}-1}}.

(1.15)

As a consequence, we show that the arithmetic genus of
\mathscr{V}({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle})
is 1, and its degree

\deg(\mathscr{V}({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}))=\begin{cases}\left(\frac{n-2}{n_{1}-1}-\frac{n-2}{n_{2}-1}\right)\binom{n-3}{n_{1}-2}\binom{n-3}{n_{2}-2},\ \quad\text{if }n_{2}\neq n\text{ and }n_{1}\neq n_{2};\vskip 5.69046pt\\
\frac{1}{n_{1}-1}\binom{n-4}{n_{1}-2}\binom{n-3}{n_{1}-2},\ \quad\text{if }1<n_{1}=n_{2}<n-1;\vskip 5.69046pt\\
\binom{n-2}{n_{1}-1},\ \quad\text{if }n_{1}\neq n_{2}=n;\vskip 5.69046pt\\
1,\ \quad\text{if }1=n_{1}=n_{2}<n\text{ or }n_{1}=n_{2}=n-1.\end{cases}

(1.16)

The degree of the polynomial
\mathfrak{p}_{{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}}(k)
was obtained by Bai
[
2
]
in term of Gelfand-Kirillov dimension. We difine the Hilbert polynomial of
M
with respect to the generating subspace
M_{0}
as
\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}=\dim(\overline{M}_{k})
for sufficiently large
k
.

Theorem 2

Let
M_{0}
be a subspace of the irreducible module
{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
in Proposition 1 satisfying (
1.10
).
Then we have
\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)\leq\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(k)
for sufficiently large
k
. A necessary and sufficient condition for
\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)=\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(k)
is that
M_{0}
is certain one-dimensional subspace, such as:

M_{0}=\mathbb{F}y_{n}^{-\ell_{2}}
when
n_{2}=n-1
,
\ell_{1}=0
and
\ell_{2}\leq 0
;

M_{0}=\mathbb{F}x_{1}^{-\ell_{1}}y_{n_{2}}^{\ell_{2}}
when
n_{1}+1=n_{2}=2
,
\ell_{1}\leq 0
and
\ell_{2}\geq 0
;
M_{0}=\mathbb{F}(x_{n}y_{n-1}-x_{n-1}y_{n})^{\ell_{1}}
when
n_{1}=n_{2}=n-2
,
\ell_{1}=-\ell_{2}>0
etc.

For a polynomial
f(k)
, we denote by
lc(f)
the leading coefficient of
f(k)
. We find that
lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}})
does not depend on the choice of the subspace
M_{0}
satisfying (
1.10
). Thus we can simply redenote
lc(M)=lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}})
.

Theorem 3

For the irreducible module
{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
in Proposition 1, we have

\displaystyle\frac{lc({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle})}{lc(\mathfrak{p}_{{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}})}=\begin{cases}1&\text{ if }n_{1}<n_{2}<n\text{ or }\ell_{1}=\ell_{2}=0;\\
1-\ell_{1}-\ell_{2}&\text{ if }1<n_{1}=n_{2}<n-1;\\
\binom{n+\ell_{2}-2}{\ell_{2}}&\text{ if }n_{1}<n_{2}=n;\\
\binom{n-\ell_{2}-2}{-\ell_{2}}&\text{ if }1=n_{1}=n_{2}<n,\ell_{2}\leq 0\text{ and }\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}<0;\\
\binom{n-\ell_{1}-2}{-\ell_{1}}&\text{ if }1<n_{1}=n_{2}=n-1,\ell_{1}\leq 0\text{ and }\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}<0;\\
\binom{n+\ell_{1}-3}{\ell_{1}}&\text{ if }1=n_{1}=n_{2}<n-1\text{ and }\ell_{2}=-\ell_{1}<0;\\
\binom{n+\ell_{2}-3}{\ell_{2}}&\text{ if }1<n_{1}=n_{2}=n-1\text{ and }\ell_{1}=-\ell_{2}<0.\end{cases}

(1.17)

For instance, when
1<n_{1}=n_{2}<n-1
and
\ell_{1}=\ell_{2}=-1
, we have
lc({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle})=3\,lc(\mathfrak{p}_{{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}})
, and then
\mathfrak{p}_{{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle},M_{0}}>\mathfrak{p}_{{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}}
for any
M_{0}
satisfying (
1.10
).
When
n_{1}=2
,
n_{2}=3
,
n=5
and
\ell_{1}=\ell_{2}=-1
, we have
lc({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle})=lc(\mathfrak{p}_{{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}})
, but
\mathfrak{p}_{{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle},M_{0}}>\mathfrak{p}_{{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}}
holds for any
M_{0}
satisfying (
1.10
).

The structure of the paper is as follows. In
Section 2 we prove Theorem 1 and (1.16).
Section 3 is devoted to the proof of Theorem 2. Finally we present the proof of Theorem 3 in Section 4.

2
Hilbert Polynomial of
\mathscr{V}(M)

In the rest of this paper, we mainly consider the representation
\tilde{\pi}
of
sl(n)
obtained under the Fourier transformations (
1.7
), whose representation formulas are given by

\tilde{\pi}(E_{i,j})=E_{i,j}^{x}-E_{j,i}^{y}\qquad\mbox{for}\;\;i,j\in\overline{1,n}

(2.1)

with

E_{i,j}^{x}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}-x_{j}\partial_{x_{i}}-\delta_{i,j}&\mbox{if}\;i,j\in\overline{1,n_{1}};\\
\partial_{x_{i}}\partial_{x_{j}}&\mbox{if}\;i\in\overline{1,n_{1}},\;j\in\overline{n_{1}+1,n};\\
-x_{i}x_{j}&\mbox{if}\;i\in\overline{n_{1}+1,n},\;j\in\overline{1,n_{1}};\\
x_{i}\partial_{x_{j}}&\mbox{if}\;i,j\in\overline{n_{1}+1,n}\end{array}\right.

(2.2)

and

E_{i,j}^{y}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}y_{i}\partial_{y_{j}}&\mbox{if}\;i,j\in\overline{1,n_{2}};\\
-y_{i}y_{j}&\mbox{if}\;i\in\overline{1,n_{2}},\;j\in\overline{n_{2}+1,n};\\
\partial_{y_{i}}\partial_{y_{j}}&\mbox{if}\;i\in\overline{n_{2}+1,n},\;j\in\overline{1,n_{2}};\\
-y_{j}\partial_{y_{i}}-\delta_{i,j}&\mbox{if}\;i,j\in\overline{n_{2}+1,n}.\end{array}\right.

(2.3)

Moreover, we adopt the notion

\xi(f)=\tilde{\pi}(\xi)(f)\qquad\mbox{for}\;\;\xi\in sl(n),\;f\in\mathscr{A}.

(2.4)

Denote

x^{\alpha}=x_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}x_{2}^{\alpha_{2}}\cdots x_{n}^{\alpha_{n}},\;\;y^{\alpha}=y_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}y_{2}^{\alpha_{2}}\cdots y_{n}^{\alpha_{n}}\qquad\mbox{for}\;\;\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},...,\alpha_{n})\in\mathbb{N}^{n}.

(2.5)

When
n_{1}<n_{2}
, we define the linear operator
T
on
\mathscr{A}
by

\displaystyle T(x^{\alpha}y^{\beta})=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\frac{\left(x_{n_{1}+1}y_{n_{1}+1}\right)^{i}\left(\tilde{\Delta}+\partial_{x_{n_{1}+1}}\partial_{y_{n_{1}+1}}\right)^{i}}{\prod_{r=1}^{i}\left(\alpha_{n_{1}+1}+r\right)\left(\beta_{n_{1}+1}+r\right)}(x^{\alpha}y^{\beta})

(2.6)

for
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
.
Then the set

\displaystyle\left\{T\left(x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}\right)\mid\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{n},\alpha_{n_{1}+1}\beta_{n_{1}+1}=0,x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}\in{\mathscr{A}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}\right\}

(2.7)

forms a basis of the module
{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
, as shown in
[
26
]
. Furthermore, we introduce the notion

\displaystyle N\begin{pmatrix}k_{12}&k_{12}&k_{13}\\
k_{21}&k_{22}&k_{23}\end{pmatrix}

(2.8)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\left.x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}\in\mathscr{A}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}\right|\sum_{r=1}^{n_{1}}\alpha_{r}=k_{11},\sum_{r=n_{1}+1}^{n_{2}}\alpha_{r}=k_{12},\sum_{r=n_{2}+1}^{n}\alpha_{r}=k_{13},\right.

\displaystyle\left.\sum_{r=1}^{n_{1}}\beta_{r}=k_{21},\sum_{r=n_{1}+1}^{n_{2}}\beta_{r}=k_{22},\alpha_{n_{1}+1}\beta_{n_{1}+1}=0\right\}

and

\displaystyle TN\begin{pmatrix}k_{12}&k_{12}&k_{13}\\
k_{21}&k_{22}&k_{23}\end{pmatrix}=\text{Span}\left\{T(f)\mid f\in N\begin{pmatrix}k_{12}&k_{12}&k_{13}\\
k_{21}&k_{22}&k_{23}\end{pmatrix}\right\}

(2.9)

where
k_{11},k_{12},k_{13},k_{21},k_{22},k_{23}\in\mathbb{N}
.

Define the function
{\mathfrak{d}}:{\mathscr{A}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
by

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}\left(x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}\right)=2\sum_{i\in J_{3}}\alpha_{i}+\sum_{i\in J_{2}}\alpha_{i}+2\sum_{i\in J_{1}}\beta_{i}+\sum_{i\in J_{2}}\beta_{i}-\frac{\ell_{1}+|\ell_{1}|+\ell_{2}+|\ell_{2}|}{2},

(2.10)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}\left(\lambda f\right)={\mathfrak{d}}\left(f\right),\ \forall\lambda\in\mathbb{F}\setminus\{0\},

(2.11)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}f_{i}\right)=\max\{{\mathfrak{d}}\left(f_{1}\right),\cdots,{\mathfrak{d}}\left(f_{m}\right)\},\;\;\text{where $f_{i}$ are monomials.}

(2.12)

We set

N(k)=\{x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}\mid{\mathfrak{d}}\left(x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}\right)=k,x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}\in{\mathscr{A}}_{\left\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\right\rangle},\alpha_{n_{1}+1}\beta_{n_{1}+1}=0\},

(2.13)

TN(k)=\text{Span}\{T(x^{\alpha}y^{\beta})\mid{\mathfrak{d}}\left(x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}\right)=k,x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}\in{\mathscr{A}}_{\left\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\right\rangle},\alpha_{n_{1}+1}\beta_{n_{1}+1}=0\},

(2.14)

TN_{k}=\text{Span}\{T(x^{\alpha}y^{\beta})\mid{\mathfrak{d}}\left(x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}\right)\leq k,x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}\in{\mathscr{A}}_{\left\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\right\rangle},\alpha_{n_{1}+1}\beta_{n_{1}+1}=0\}.

(2.15)

Denote

Z=\{z_{j,i}\mid i\in J_{1},j\in J_{3}\}

(2.16)

as a set of
n_{1}(n-n_{2})
variables.
Set
\mathscr{B}=\mathbb{F}[Z]
,
the polynomial algebra in
Z
. Denote by
Z^{(m)}
the set of monomials with degree
m
.
Let
\mathscr{C}=\mathbb{F}\left[x_{i},y_{j},Z|i,j\in\overline{1,n}\right]
.
Define an associative algebra homomorphism
\phi:\mathscr{C}\rightarrow\mathscr{A}
by

\phi(z_{j,i})=x_{i}x_{j}-y_{i}y_{j},\;\;\phi(x_{t})=x_{t},\;\phi(y_{t})=y_{t}\qquad i\in J_{1},\;j\in J_{3},t\in\overline{1,n}.

(2.17)

Set

\displaystyle V_{0}=\begin{cases}TN\begin{pmatrix}-\ell_{1}&0&0\\
0&0&-\ell_{2}\end{pmatrix}&\text{if }\ell_{1}\leq 0,\ell_{2}\leq 0,\\
TN\begin{pmatrix}-\ell_{1}&0&0\\
0&\ell_{2}&0\end{pmatrix}&\text{if }\ell_{1}\leq 0,\ell_{2}\geq 0\text{ and }n_{1}<n_{2}<n,\\
TN\begin{pmatrix}0&\ell_{1}&0\\
0&0&-\ell_{2}\end{pmatrix}&\text{if }\ell_{1}\geq 0,\ell_{2}\leq 0\text{ and }n_{1}<n_{2}<n,\\
\text{Span}\left\{TN\begin{pmatrix}0&\ell_{1}&0\\
k_{21}&k_{22}&0\end{pmatrix}\Big|k_{21}+k_{22}=\ell_{2}\right\}&\text{if }\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\geq 0\text{ and }n_{1}<n_{2}=n,\\
\text{Span}\left\{TN\begin{pmatrix}-\ell_{1}&0&0\\
k_{21}&k_{22}&0\end{pmatrix}\Big|k_{21}+k_{22}=\ell_{2}\right\}&\text{if }\ell_{1}\leq 0,\ell_{2}>0\text{ and }n_{1}<n_{2}=n,\\
\end{cases}\quad

(2.18)

which is a finite-dimensional subspace of
M={\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
.
When
1<n_{1}=n_{2}<n
,
\ell_{1}\leq 0
and
\ell_{2}>0
, we set
\ell_{1}=-m_{1}-m_{2}
,
\ell_{2}=m_{2}
with
m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
and

\displaystyle V_{0}=

\displaystyle\mbox{Span}\Big\{\left.\left[\prod_{1\leq p<q\leq n_{1}}(x_{p}y_{q}-x_{q}y_{p})^{k_{p,q}}\right]\prod_{r=1}^{n_{1}}x_{r}^{\alpha_{r}}\right|

(2.19)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad k_{p,q}\in\mathbb{N},\sum_{1\leq p<q\leq n_{1}}k_{p,q}=m_{2},\sum_{1\leq i\leq n_{1}}\alpha_{i}=m_{1}\Big\}.

Symmetrically, when
n_{1}=n_{2}<n-1
,
\ell_{1}>0
and
\ell_{2}\leq 0
, we set
\ell_{1}=m_{2}
,
\ell_{2}=-m_{1}-m_{2}
with
m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
and

\displaystyle V_{0}=

\displaystyle\mbox{Span}\Big\{\left.\left[\prod_{n_{1}+1\leq p<q\leq n}(x_{p}y_{q}-x_{q}y_{p})^{k_{p,q}}\right]\prod_{j=n_{1}+1}^{n}y_{j}^{\beta_{j}}\right|

(2.20)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad k_{p,q}\in\mathbb{N},\sum_{n_{1}+1\leq p<q\leq n}k_{p,q}=m_{2},\sum_{n_{1}+1\leq j\leq n}\beta_{j}=m_{1}\Big\}.

When
n_{1}<n_{2}
,
\ell_{1}\leq 0
or
\ell_{2}\leq 0
, we take
M_{0}=V_{0}
, and then we have

\displaystyle M_{k}

\displaystyle=\text{Span}\{TN\left(i\right),TN\left(k-i\right)\phi(Z^{(i)})\mid i=0,1,\dots,k\}

\displaystyle=\text{Span}\{TN_{k},TN\left(k-i\right)\phi(Z^{(i)})\mid i=1,\dots,k\}.

(2.21)

according to Proposition 2.1 in
[
34
]
.

Set

J_{1}=\overline{1,n_{1}},\quad J_{2}=\overline{n_{1}+1,n_{2}},\quad J_{3}=\overline{n_{2}+1,n}.

(2.22)

In
[
34
]
, we determined the associated varieties
\mathscr{V}(\mathscr{H}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle})
as follows:

Lemma 2.1
.

If
n_{1}\leq n_{2}
,
\ell_{1}\leq 0
or
\ell_{2}\leq 0
,

\mathscr{V}({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle})\cong\mathscr{V}_{3}(J_{2}\cup J_{3},J_{1}\cup J_{2})\cap\mathscr{V}_{2}(J_{2},J_{1})\cap\mathscr{V}_{2}(J_{3},J_{2})\cap\mathscr{V}_{1}(J_{2},J_{2}).

(2.23)

If
n_{1}<n_{2}=n
and
\ell_{1},\ell_{2}>0
,

\mathscr{V}({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle})\cong\mathscr{V}_{2}(J_{2},J_{1}).

(2.24)

Let
\{(j_{1},i_{1}),(j_{2},i_{2}),...,(j_{r},i_{r})\}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{2}
.
If there exist three distinct
1\leq k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\leq r
such that

i_{k_{1}}<i_{k_{2}}<i_{k_{3}}
and
j_{k_{1}}<j_{k_{2}}<j_{k_{3}}
, we say that
\{(j_{1},i_{1}),(j_{2},i_{2}),...,(j_{r},i_{r})\}
contains a 3-chain
\left(j_{k_{1}},i_{k_{1}}\right)\prec\left(j_{k_{2}},i_{k_{2}}\right)\prec\left(j_{k_{3}},i_{k_{3}}\right)
. Similarly, we say that
\{(j_{1},i_{1}),(j_{2},i_{2}),...,(j_{r},i_{r})\}
contains a 3-chain
\left(j_{k_{1}},i_{k_{1}}\right)\prec\left(j_{k_{2}},i_{k_{2}}\right)
if
i_{k_{1}}<i_{k_{2}}
and
j_{k_{1}}<j_{k_{2}}
.

Denote
\mathscr{Z}=\mathbb{F}\left[z_{i,j}|1\leq i\leq m,1\leq j\leq n\right]
for
m,n\in\mathbb{N}
. Denote by
\mathscr{Z}^{(r)}
the set of monomials in
\mathscr{Z}
with degree
r
for
r\in\mathbb{N}
.

For
r\in\mathbb{N}
,
a\in\mathbb{F}
and
\{(j_{t},i_{t})\in\overline{1,m}\times\overline{1,n}|1\leq t\leq r\}
, we set

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}\left(a\prod_{t=1}^{r}z_{i_{t},j_{t}}\right)=\{(i_{t},j_{t})|1\leq t\leq r\}.

(2.25)

For a monomial
f\in\mathscr{Z}^{(r)}
, we say that
f
contains a 3-chain if
\mathscr{I}(f)
contains a 3-chain.

We denote the function
h_{2}\left(m,n,r\right)
as the number of monomials in
\mathscr{Z}
of degree
r
without 2-chains,
h_{3}\left(m,n,r\right)
as the number of monomials in
\mathscr{Z}
of degree
r
without 3-chains.

According to
[
5
,
25
]
, the followings hold:

h_{2}\left(m,n,r\right)=\binom{m-1+r}{m-1}\cdot\binom{n-1+r}{n-1},

(2.26)

its generating function

{\mathscr{S}}^{m,n}_{2}(t)=\frac{\sum_{r}\binom{m-1}{r}\binom{n-1}{r}t^{r}}{\left(1-t\right)^{m+n-1}},

(2.27)

and the generating function

\displaystyle{\mathscr{S}}_{3}^{m,n}(t)=\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}h_{3}\left(m,n,r\right)t^{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\det\left(\sum_{r}\binom{m-i}{r}\binom{n-j}{r+i-j}t^{r}\right)_{i,j=1,2}}{\left(1-t\right)^{2\left(m+n-2\right)}}

(2.28)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\det\left(\sum_{r}\binom{m-i}{r}\binom{n-j}{r}t^{r}\right)_{i,j=1,2}}{t\left(1-t\right)^{2\left(m+n-2\right)}}.

For convenience, we assume that
{\mathscr{S}}_{3}^{m,n}(t)={\mathscr{S}}_{2}^{m,n}(t)=0
when
m\leq 0
or
n\leq 0
.

For
m,m^{\prime},n,n^{\prime},r,\xi\in\mathbb{N}
, we define the set

\displaystyle S_{1}^{\overline{m,m^{\prime}},\overline{n,n^{\prime}}}(\xi,r)=\Big\{f=\prod_{t=1}^{r}z_{i_{t},j_{t}}\in\mathscr{Z}^{(r)}|m\leq i_{t}\leq m^{\prime},n\leq j_{t}\leq n^{\prime};

\displaystyle\text{no 3-chains in $\mathscr{I}(f)$, no 2-chains in }\{(i_{t},j_{t})|j_{t}<\xi,t\in\overline{1,r}\}\Big\}

(2.29)

when
m\leq m^{\prime}
and
n\leq n^{\prime}
. We also denote
S_{1}^{\overline{m,m^{\prime}},\overline{n,n^{\prime}}}(\xi,r)=\emptyset
when
m>m^{\prime}
or
n>n^{\prime}
.
Denote by
P_{1}(m,n,\xi,r)
the cardinality of
S_{1}^{\overline{1,m},\overline{1,n}}(\xi,r)
for
m,n,\xi,r\in\mathbb{N}
with
\xi\in\overline{1,n}
.
In particular, we have

\displaystyle P_{1}(m,n,\xi,r)=h_{3}(m,n,r),\quad\text{for}\,\,\xi\leq 2.

(2.30)

and

\displaystyle P_{1}(m,n,\xi,r)=h_{2}(m,n,r),\quad\text{for}\,\,\xi>n.

(2.31)

Set

\displaystyle{\mathscr{S}}^{m,n,\xi}(t)=\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}P_{1}(m,n,\xi,r)t^{r},

(2.32)

then we have a recursion formula as follows:

Lemma 2.2
.

For
m,n,r\in\mathbb{N}
,
\xi\in\overline{3,n}
with
m,n\geq 2
and
r>1
, we have

\displaystyle P_{1}(m,n,\xi,r)

(2.33)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{r_{1}=1}^{r}\sum_{\xi^{\prime}=1}^{m}\binom{m-\xi^{\prime}-1+r_{1}}{r_{1}-1}\binom{\xi-2+r_{1}}{r_{1}}P_{1}(n-\xi+1,m,m-\xi^{\prime}+1,r-r_{1})\Big)

\displaystyle+h_{3}(m,n-\xi+1,r).

The series form of this formula is

\displaystyle{\mathscr{S}}^{m,n,\xi}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\xi^{\prime}=1}^{m}\Big({\mathscr{S}}^{n-\xi+1,m,m-\xi^{\prime}+1}(t)({\mathscr{S}}^{m-\xi^{\prime}+1,\xi-1}_{2}(t)-{\mathscr{S}}^{m-\xi^{\prime},\xi-1}_{2}(t))\Big)

(2.34)

Proof.

For a monomial
f\in S_{1}^{\overline{1,m},\overline{1,n}}(\xi,r)
, we write

f=\prod_{1\leq i\leq m,1\leq j\leq n}z_{i,j}^{\gamma_{i,j}}=f_{1}f_{2}

(2.35)

where

f_{1}=\prod_{1\leq i\leq m,1\leq j\leq\xi-1}z_{i,j}^{\gamma_{i,j}},\quad f_{2}=\prod_{1\leq i\leq m,\xi\leq j\leq n}z_{i,j}^{\gamma_{i,j}}

(2.36)

Setting

r_{1}(f)=\deg(f_{1})=\sum_{1\leq i\leq m,1\leq j<\xi}\gamma_{i,j},

(2.37)

then we have

|\{f\in S_{1}^{\overline{1,m},\overline{1,n}}(\xi,r)|r_{1}(f)=0\}|=h_{3}(m,n-\xi+1,r).

(2.38)

For
f\in S_{1}^{\overline{1,m},\overline{1,n}}(\xi,r)
and
r_{1}(f)>0
, we set

\xi^{\prime}=\min\{i\in\overline{1,m}|\gamma_{i,j}>0\text{ for some }j\in\overline{1,\xi-1}\},

(2.39)

and then there exists
j_{0}\in\overline{1,\xi-1}
such that
\gamma_{\xi^{\prime},j_{0}}>0
.
Thus

f_{1}\in S_{1}^{\overline{\xi^{\prime},m},\overline{1,\xi-1}}(\xi,r_{1}(f))\setminus S_{1}^{\overline{\xi^{\prime}+1,m},\overline{1,\xi-1}}(\xi,r_{1}(f)).

(2.40)

In particular,
f_{1}\in S_{1}^{\overline{m,m},\overline{1,\xi-1}}(\xi,r_{1}(f))
when
\xi^{\prime}=m
.

When there exists a 2-chain
(i_{1},j_{1})\prec(i_{2},j_{2})
in
\mathscr{I}(f)\cap\{(i,j)|i>\xi^{\prime},j\geq\xi\}
,
(\xi^{\prime},j_{0})\prec(i_{1},j_{1})\prec(i_{2},j_{2})
is a 3-chain in
\mathscr{I}(f)
. Thus there are no 2-chains in
\mathscr{I}(f_{2})\cap\{(i,j)|i>\xi^{\prime}\}
and no 3-chain in
\mathscr{I}(f_{2})
.

For
f=\prod_{1\leq i\leq m,1\leq j\leq n}z_{i,j}^{\gamma_{i,j}}\in\mathscr{Z}^{(r)}
, we denote

f^{t}=\prod_{1\leq i\leq m,1\leq j\leq n}z_{j,n-i+1}^{\gamma_{i,j}}

(2.41)

in
\mathbb{F}\left[z_{j,i}|1\leq i\leq m,1\leq j\leq n\right]
. Then
(\cdot)^{t}
is a one-to-one mapping from
\mathscr{Z}^{(r)}
to the set of monomials in
\mathbb{F}\left[z_{j,i}|1\leq i\leq m,1\leq j\leq n\right]
with degree
r
.
Hence
f_{2}^{t}\in S_{1}^{\overline{\xi,n},\overline{1,m}}(m-\xi^{\prime}+1,r-r_{1}(f))
.

On the other hand, when
g_{1}\in S_{1}^{\overline{\xi^{\prime},m},\overline{1,\xi-1}}(\xi,r_{1})\setminus S_{1}^{\overline{\xi^{\prime}+1,m},\overline{1,\xi-1}}(\xi,r_{1})
and
g_{2}^{t}\in S_{1}^{\overline{\xi,n},\overline{1,m}}(m-\xi^{\prime}+1,r-r_{1})
for some
\xi^{\prime}\in\overline{1,m}
and
r_{1}\in\overline{1,r}
, we have
g_{1}g_{2}\in S_{1}^{\overline{1,m},\overline{1,n}}(\xi,r)
.

Therefore,

\displaystyle P_{1}(m,n,\xi,r)

(2.42)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{r_{1}=1}^{r}\sum_{\xi^{\prime}=1}^{m}|S_{1}^{\overline{\xi,n},\overline{1,m}}(m-\xi^{\prime}+1,r-r_{1})||S_{1}^{\overline{\xi^{\prime},m},\overline{1,\xi-1}}(\xi,r_{1})\setminus S_{1}^{\overline{\xi^{\prime}+1,m},\overline{1,\xi-1}}(\xi,r_{1})|\Big)

\displaystyle+h_{3}(m,n-\xi+1,r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{r_{1}=1}^{r}\sum_{\xi^{\prime}=1}^{m}|S_{1}^{\overline{1,n-\xi+1},\overline{1,m}}(m-\xi^{\prime}+1,r-r_{1})|\left(h_{2}(m-\xi^{\prime}+1,\xi-1,r_{1})-h_{2}(m-\xi^{\prime},\xi-1,r_{1})\right)\Big)

\displaystyle+h_{3}(m,n-\xi+1,r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{r_{1}=1}^{r}\sum_{\xi^{\prime}=1}^{m}\binom{m-\xi^{\prime}-1+r_{1}}{r_{1}-1}\binom{\xi-2+r_{1}}{r_{1}}P_{1}(n-\xi+1,m,m-\xi^{\prime}+1,r-r_{1})\Big)

\displaystyle+h_{3}(m,n-\xi+1,r).

Thus, equation (
2.33
) holds.
The above equation can be rewritten in the form of a generating function, leading to (
2.34
).

∎

Note that
\deg(h_{3}(m,n))=2m+2n-5
.
By induction on
m+n
, it is easy to prove the following corollary of Lemma
2.2
.

Corollary 2.1
.

For
m,n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\xi\in\overline{1,n}
with
m,n\geq 2
, we have

\deg_{r}(P_{1}(m,n,\xi,r))=\begin{cases}2m+2n-5,\ \quad\text{if }\xi=1;\\
2m+2n-\xi-3,\ \quad\text{if }\xi\geq 2.\end{cases}

(2.43)

For
m,m^{\prime},n,n^{\prime},r,\varrho,\xi\in\mathbb{N}
, we define the set

\displaystyle S_{2}^{\overline{m,m^{\prime}},\overline{n,n^{\prime}}}(\varrho,\xi,r)=\Big\{f=\prod_{t=1}^{r}z_{i_{t},j_{t}}\in\mathscr{Z}^{(r)}|m\leq i_{t}\leq m^{\prime},n\leq j_{t}\leq n^{\prime},

\displaystyle i_{t}\leq\varrho\text{ or }j_{t}\geq\xi\text{ for }t\in\overline{1,r};\text{ no 3-chains in }\mathscr{I}(f),\text{no 2-chains in }

\displaystyle\{(i_{t},j_{t})|j_{t}<\xi,t\in\overline{1,r}\}\text{ and }\{(i_{t},j_{t})|i_{t}>\varrho,t\in\overline{1,r}\}\Big\}

(2.44)

when
m\leq m^{\prime}
and
n\leq n^{\prime}
, and
S_{2}^{\overline{m,m^{\prime}},\overline{n,n^{\prime}}}(\varrho,\xi,r)=\emptyset
when
m>m^{\prime}
or
n>n^{\prime}
.
Denote by
P_{2}(m,n,\varrho,\xi,r)
the cardinality of
S_{2}^{\overline{1,m},\overline{1,n}}(\rho,\xi,r)
for
m,n,\varrho,\xi,r\in\mathbb{N}
with
\varrho\in\overline{1,m}
and
\xi\in\overline{1,n}
.
In particular,

P_{2}(m,n,m,\xi,r)=P_{1}(m,n,\xi,r).

(2.45)

For
f=\prod_{1\leq i\leq m,1\leq j\leq n}z_{i,j}^{\gamma_{i,j}}\in S_{2}^{\overline{m,m^{\prime}},\overline{n,n^{\prime}}}(\varrho,\xi,r)
, we set the one-to-one mapping
(\cdot)^{T^{\prime}}
from
S_{2}^{\overline{1,m},\overline{1,n}}(\varrho,\xi,r)
to
S_{2}^{\overline{1,n},\overline{1,m}}(n-\xi+1,m-\varrho+1,r)
by

f^{T^{\prime}}=\prod_{1\leq i\leq m,1\leq j\leq n}z_{n-j+1,m-i+1}^{\gamma_{i,j}}.

(2.46)

Hence

P_{2}(m,n,\varrho,\xi,r)=P_{2}(n,m,n-\xi+1,m-\varrho+1,r).

(2.47)

By (
2.45
) and (
2.47
),

P_{2}(m,n,\varrho,1,r)=P_{2}(n,m,n,m-\varrho+1,r)=P_{1}(n,m,m-\varrho+1,r).

(2.48)

Denote

\displaystyle{\mathscr{S}}^{m,n,\varrho,\xi}(t)=\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}P_{2}(m,n,\varrho,\xi,r)t^{r},

(2.49)

then we have the recursion formula as follows:

Lemma 2.3
.

For
m,n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\varrho\in\overline{1,m-1}
,
\xi\in\overline{2,n}
and
r>1
, we have

\displaystyle P_{2}(m,n,\varrho,\xi,r)

(2.50)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\varrho^{\prime}=1}^{\varrho}\sum_{\xi^{\prime}=\xi}^{n}\bigg(\sum_{r_{1},r_{2}>0;r_{1}+r_{2}\leq r}\binom{\varrho-\varrho^{\prime}-1+r_{2}}{r_{2}-1}\binom{\xi-2+r_{2}}{r_{2}}\binom{m-\varrho-1+r_{1}}{r_{1}}

\displaystyle\;\;\;\binom{\xi^{\prime}-\xi-1+r_{1}}{r_{1}-1}P_{2}(\varrho,n-\xi+1,\varrho^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}-\xi+1,r-r_{1}-r_{2})\bigg)+P_{1}(\varrho,n,\xi,r)

\displaystyle\;\;\;+P_{1}(n-\xi+1,m,m-\varrho+1,r)-h_{3}(\varrho,n-\xi+1,r).

The series form of this formula is

\displaystyle{\mathscr{S}}^{m,n,\varrho,\xi}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\varrho^{\prime}=1}^{\varrho}\sum_{\xi^{\prime}=\xi}^{n}{\mathscr{S}}^{\varrho,n-\xi+1,\varrho^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}-\xi+1}(t)({\mathscr{S}}^{\varrho-\varrho^{\prime}+1,\xi-1}_{2}(t)-{\mathscr{S}}^{\varrho-\varrho^{\prime},\xi-1}_{2}(t))

(2.51)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad({\mathscr{S}}^{m-\varrho,\xi^{\prime}-\xi+1}_{2}(t)-{\mathscr{S}}^{m-\varrho,\xi^{\prime}-\xi}_{2}(t)).

Proof.

For
f\in S_{2}^{\overline{1,m},\overline{1,n}}(\varrho,\xi,r)
, we write

f=\prod_{1\leq i\leq m,1\leq j\leq n}z_{i,j}^{\gamma_{i,j}}.

(2.52)

We set

r_{1}(f)=\sum_{\varrho<i\leq m,1\leq j\leq n}\gamma_{i,j},\;\;\;r_{2}(f)=\sum_{1\leq i\leq m,1\leq j<\xi}\gamma_{i,j}.

(2.53)

Then we have

|\{f\in S_{2}^{\overline{1,m},\overline{1,n}}(\varrho,\xi,r)|r_{1}(f)=r_{2}(f)=0\}|=h_{3}(\varrho,n-\xi+1,r).

(2.54)

Since

\{f\in S_{2}^{\overline{1,m},\overline{1,n}}(\varrho,\xi,r)|r_{1}(f)=0\}=S_{1}^{\overline{1,\varrho},\overline{1,n}}(\xi,r),

(2.55)

\displaystyle|\{f\in S_{2}^{\overline{1,m},\overline{1,n}}(\varrho,\xi,r)|r_{1}(f)=0\}|=P_{1}(\varrho,n,\xi,r).

(2.56)

Similarly,

|\{f\in S_{2}^{\overline{1,m},\overline{1,n}}(\varrho,\xi,r)|r_{2}(f)=0\}|=P_{1}(n-\xi+1,m,m-\varrho+1,r).

(2.57)

Suppose that
r_{1}(f),r_{2}(f)>0
. We denote

\xi^{\prime}=\max\{j\in\overline{\xi,n}|\gamma_{i,j}>0\text{ for some }i\in\overline{\varrho+1,m}\}

(2.58)

and

\varrho^{\prime}=\max\{i\in\overline{1,\varrho}|\gamma_{i,j}>0\text{ for some }j\in\overline{1,\xi-1}\}.

(2.59)

Then there exist
i_{0}\in\overline{\varrho+1,m}
and
j_{0}\in\overline{1,\xi-1}
such that
\gamma_{\varrho^{\prime},j_{0}}>0
and
\gamma_{i_{0},\xi^{\prime}}>0
.
We set

f_{1}=\prod_{\varrho<i\leq m,1\leq j\leq n}z_{i,j}^{\gamma_{i,j}},\quad f_{2}=\prod_{1\leq i\leq m,1\leq j<\xi}z_{i,j}^{\gamma_{i,j}},\quad f_{3}=\prod_{1\leq i\leq\varrho,\xi\leq j\leq n}z_{i,j}^{\gamma_{i,j}},

(2.60)

and then
f=f_{1}f_{2}f_{3}
.
By (
2.53
) and (
2.58
), we have

f_{1}\in S_{1}^{\overline{\varrho+1,m},\overline{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}}(n+1,r_{1}(f))\setminus S_{1}^{\overline{\varrho+1,m},\overline{\xi,\xi^{\prime}-1}}(n+1,r_{1}(f)).

(2.61)

Similarly,

f_{2}\in S_{1}^{\overline{\varrho^{\prime},\varrho},\overline{1,\xi-1}}(n+1,r_{2}(f))\setminus S_{1}^{\overline{\varrho^{\prime}+1,\varrho},\overline{1,\xi-1}}(n+1,r_{2}(f)).

(2.62)

When there exists
\gamma_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}>0
for some
i^{\prime}\in\overline{\varrho^{\prime}+1,\varrho}
and
j^{\prime}\in\overline{\xi,\xi^{\prime}-1}
,
(\varrho^{\prime},j_{0})\prec(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\prec(i_{0},\xi^{\prime})
is a 3-chain in
\mathscr{I}(f)
. Hence
\{(i,j)\in\mathscr{I}(f_{3})|i>\varrho^{\prime},j<\xi^{\prime}\}=\emptyset
.
When there exists a 2-chain
(i_{1},j_{1})\prec(i_{2},j_{2})
in
\{(i,j)\in\mathscr{I}(f_{3})|i>\varrho^{\prime}\}
,
(\varrho^{\prime},j_{0})\prec(i_{1},j_{1})\prec(i_{2},j_{2})
is a 3-chain in
\mathscr{I}(f)
. Thus there are no 2-chains in
\{(i,j)\in\mathscr{I}(f_{3})|i>\varrho^{\prime}\}
.
Symmetrically, there are no 2-chains in
\{(i,j)\in\mathscr{I}(f_{3})|j<\xi^{\prime}\}
.
Hence,

f_{3}\in S_{2}^{\overline{1,\varrho},\overline{\xi,n}}(\varrho^{\prime},\xi^{\prime},r-r_{1}(f)-r_{2}(f)).

(2.63)

On the other hand, when
g_{1}\in S_{1}^{\overline{\varrho+1,m},\overline{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}}(n+1,r_{1}(f))\setminus S_{1}^{\overline{\varrho+1,m},\overline{\xi,\xi^{\prime}-1}}(n+1,r_{1}(f))
and
g_{2}\in S_{1}^{\overline{\varrho^{\prime},\varrho},\overline{1,\xi-1}}(n+1,r_{2}(f))\setminus S_{1}^{\overline{\varrho^{\prime}+1,\varrho},\overline{1,\xi-1}}(n+1,r_{2}(f))
and
g_{3}\in S_{2}^{\overline{1,\varrho},\overline{\xi,n}}(\varrho^{\prime},\xi^{\prime},r-r_{1}(f)-r_{2}(f))
for some
\varrho^{\prime}\in\overline{1,\varrho}
,
\xi^{\prime}\in\overline{\xi,n}
;
r_{1},r_{2}\in\overline{1,r}
with
r_{1}+r_{2}\leq r
, we have
g_{1}g_{2}g_{3}\in S_{2}^{\overline{1,m},\overline{1,n}}(\varrho,\xi,r)
.

Therefore,

\displaystyle|\{f\in S_{2}^{\overline{1,m},\overline{1,n}}(\varrho,\xi,r)|r_{1}(f),r_{2}(f)>0\}|

(2.64)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{r_{1},r_{2}>0,r_{1}+r_{2}\leq r}\sum_{\xi^{\prime}=\xi}^{m}\sum_{\varrho^{\prime}=1}^{\varrho}\Big(|S_{1}^{\overline{\varrho+1,m},\overline{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}}(n+1,r_{1})\setminus S_{1}^{\overline{\varrho+1,m},\overline{\xi,\xi^{\prime}-1}}(n+1,r_{1})|

\displaystyle|S_{1}^{\overline{\varrho^{\prime},\varrho},\overline{1,\xi-1}}(n+1,r_{2})\setminus S_{1}^{\overline{\varrho^{\prime}+1,\varrho},\overline{1,\xi-1}}(n+1,r_{2})||S_{2}^{\overline{1,\varrho},\overline{\xi,n}}(\varrho^{\prime},\xi^{\prime},r-r_{1}-r_{2})|\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{r_{1},r_{2}>0;r_{1}+r_{2}\leq r}\sum_{\xi^{\prime}=\xi}^{m}\sum_{\varrho^{\prime}=1}^{\varrho}\Big((h_{2}(m-\varrho,\xi^{\prime}-\xi+1,r_{1})-h_{2}(m-\varrho,\xi^{\prime}-\xi,r_{1}))

\displaystyle(h_{2}(\varrho-\varrho^{\prime}+1,\xi-1,r_{2})-h_{2}(\varrho-\varrho^{\prime},\xi-1,r_{2}))|S_{2}^{\overline{1,\varrho},\overline{1,n-\xi+1}}(\varrho^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}-\xi+1,r-r_{1}-r_{2})|\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\varrho^{\prime}=1}^{\varrho}\sum_{\xi^{\prime}=\xi}^{n}\bigg(\sum_{r_{1},r_{2}>0;r_{1}+r_{2}\leq r}\binom{\varrho-\varrho^{\prime}-1+r_{2}}{r_{2}-1}\binom{\xi-2+r_{2}}{r_{2}}\binom{m-\varrho-1+r_{1}}{r_{1}}

\displaystyle\;\;\;\binom{\xi^{\prime}-\xi-1+r_{1}}{r_{1}-1}P_{2}(\varrho,n-\xi+1,\varrho^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}-\xi+1,r-r_{1}-r_{2})\bigg)

Since

\displaystyle P_{2}(m,n,\varrho,\xi,r)

(2.65)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\{f\in S_{2}^{\overline{1,m},\overline{1,n}}(\varrho,\xi,r)|r_{1}(f),r_{2}(f)>0\}|-|\{f\in S_{2}^{\overline{1,m},\overline{1,n}}(\varrho,\xi,r)|r_{1}(f)=r_{2}(f)=0\}|

\displaystyle+|\{f\in S_{2}^{\overline{1,m},\overline{1,n}}(\varrho,\xi,r)|r_{2}(f)=0\}|+|\{f\in S_{2}^{\overline{1,m},\overline{1,n}}(\varrho,\xi,r)|r_{1}(f)=0\}|,

(
2.50
) holds by (
2.54
),(
2.56
),(
2.57
) and (
2.64
).
Moreover, Equation (
2.50
) can be rewritten as a generating function, yielding (
2.51
).

∎

By induction on
m+n
, it is easy to prove the following corollary of Lemma
2.3
.

Corollary 2.2
.

For
m,n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\xi\in\overline{1,n}
and
\varrho\in\overline{1,m}
with
m,n\geq 2
, we have

\deg_{r}(P_{2}(m,n,\varrho,\xi,r))=\begin{cases}2m+2n-5,\ \quad&\text{if }\xi=1\text{ and }\varrho=m;\\
m+2n+\varrho-\xi-3,\ \quad&\text{if }\xi>1\text{ or }\varrho<m.\end{cases}

(2.66)

Lemma 2.4
.

For
p,q,n\in\mathbb{N}
,

(1)

Vandermonde’s identity:

\sum_{r=0}^{n}\binom{p}{r}\binom{q}{n-r}=\binom{p+q}{n}

(2.67)

(2)

\sum_{r=0}^{n}\binom{r}{p}\binom{n-r}{q}=\binom{n+1}{p+q+1}

(2.68)

(3)

\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{p}{r+n}\binom{q}{r}=\binom{p+q}{q+n}

(2.69)

(4)

\sum_{r=0}^{n}\binom{p+r}{p}\binom{n-r}{q}=\binom{p+n+1}{p+q+1}

(2.70)

(5)

\sum_{r=0}^{n}\binom{p+r}{p}\binom{n-r+q}{q}=\binom{p+q+n+1}{p+q+1}

(2.71)

Proof.

(1) is the Vandermonde’s identity which is well-known and (2) can be easily proved by induction on
n
.
When
p=1
, it is straightforward to verify that (
2.69
) holds.
Since

\binom{p+1}{r+n}=\binom{p}{r+n}-\binom{p}{r+n-1},

(2.72)

we can prove that (
2.69
) holds by induction on
p
.
Similarly, we can prove (
2.70
) and (
2.71
) by induction on
n
.

∎

Proposition 2.1
.

For
m,n,\varrho,\xi\in\mathbb{N}
with
1\leq\varrho\leq m
and
1\leq\xi\leq n
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathscr{S}}^{m,n,\varrho,\xi}(t)=\frac{\left|\begin{matrix}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{m-2}{r}\binom{n-\xi}{r}t^{r}&\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{m-2}{r-1}\binom{n-\xi+1}{r}t^{r}\\
\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{\varrho-1}{r+1}\binom{n-2}{r}t^{r}&\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{\varrho-1}{r}\binom{n-1}{r}t^{r}\end{matrix}\right|}{(1-t)^{m+2n+\varrho-\xi-2}}

(2.73)

In particular,

\displaystyle{\mathscr{S}}^{m,n,\xi}(t)=\frac{\left|\begin{matrix}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{m-2}{r}\binom{n-\xi}{r}t^{r}&\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{m-1}{r}\binom{n-2}{r-1}t^{r}\\
\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{m-2}{r}\binom{n-\xi+1}{r+1}t^{r}&\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{m-1}{r}\binom{n-1}{r}t^{r}\end{matrix}\right|}{(1-t)^{2m+2n-\xi-2}}

(2.74)

Proof.

We prove (
2.73
) by induction on
m+n
.
When
m+n=1
, we have
m=n=\varrho=\xi=1
, and then

\displaystyle{\mathscr{S}}^{1,1,1,1}(t)=\frac{1}{1-t},

(2.75)

and (
2.73
) holds.
We assume that (
2.73
) holds when
m+n<l
and consider
m+n=l
.

When
\varrho=m
and
\xi=1
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathscr{S}}^{m,n,m,1}(t)={\mathscr{S}}_{3}^{m,n}(t),

(2.76)

then (
2.73
) holds by Corollary 1 in
[
5
]
.

Suppose that
\varrho<m
or
\xi>1
, then
\varrho+n-\xi+1<m+n=l
.
By the inductional assumption, for
\xi^{\prime}\in\overline{\xi,n}
and
\varrho^{\prime}=\overline{1,\varrho}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathscr{S}}^{\varrho,n-\xi+1,\varrho^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}-\xi+1}(t)=\frac{\left|\begin{matrix}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{\varrho-2}{r}\binom{n-\xi^{\prime}}{r}t^{r}&\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{\varrho-2}{r-1}\binom{n-\xi^{\prime}+1}{r}t^{r}\\
\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{\varrho^{\prime}-1}{r+1}\binom{n-\xi-1}{r}t^{r}&\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{\varrho^{\prime}-1}{r}\binom{n-\xi}{r}t^{r}\end{matrix}\right|}{(1-t)^{2n+\varrho+\varrho^{\prime}-\xi-\xi^{\prime}-1}}

(2.77)

Denote

\mathfrak{f}_{1}^{\xi,\varrho,\varrho^{\prime}}=\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\binom{\varrho-\varrho^{\prime}-1}{r-1}\binom{\xi-1}{r}t^{r}

(2.78)

and

\mathfrak{f}_{2}^{x,\xi,\xi^{\prime}}=\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\binom{x}{r}\binom{\xi^{\prime}-\xi-1}{r-1}t^{r}

(2.79)

for
x,\xi,\xi^{\prime},\varrho,\varrho^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
.

By (
2.27
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathscr{S}}^{\varrho-\varrho^{\prime}+1,\xi-1}_{2}(t)-{\mathscr{S}}^{\varrho-\varrho^{\prime},\xi-1}_{2}(t)=\frac{\mathfrak{f}_{1}^{\xi,\varrho,\varrho^{\prime}}}{(1-t)^{\varrho-\varrho^{\prime}+\xi-1}}

(2.80)

and

\displaystyle{\mathscr{S}}^{m-\varrho,\xi^{\prime}-\xi+1}_{2}(t)-{\mathscr{S}}^{m-\varrho,\xi^{\prime}-\xi}_{2}(t)=\frac{\mathfrak{f}_{2}^{m-\varrho,\xi,\xi^{\prime}}}{(1-t)^{m-\varrho+\xi^{\prime}-\xi}}.

(2.81)

According to Lemma
2.3
, (
2.77
), (
2.80
) and (
2.81
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathscr{S}}^{m,n,\varrho,\xi}(t)(1-t)^{m+2n+\varrho-\xi-2}

(2.82)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\varrho^{\prime}=1}^{\varrho}\sum_{\xi^{\prime}=\xi}^{n}\frac{\left|\begin{matrix}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{\varrho-2}{r}\binom{n-\xi^{\prime}}{r}t^{r}&\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{\varrho-2}{r-1}\binom{n-\xi^{\prime}+1}{r}t^{r}\\
\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{\varrho^{\prime}-1}{r+1}\binom{n-\xi-1}{r}t^{r}&\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{\varrho^{\prime}-1}{r}\binom{n-\xi}{r}t^{r}\end{matrix}\right|}{(1-t)^{2n+\varrho+\varrho^{\prime}-\xi-\xi^{\prime}-1}}\cdot\frac{\mathfrak{f}_{1}^{\xi,\varrho,\varrho^{\prime}}}{(1-t)^{\varrho-\varrho^{\prime}+\xi-1}}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\frac{\mathfrak{f}_{2}^{m-\varrho,\xi,\xi^{\prime}}}{(1-t)^{m-\varrho+\xi^{\prime}-\xi}}\cdot(1-t)^{m+2n+\varrho-\xi-2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\varrho^{\prime}=1}^{\varrho}\sum_{\xi^{\prime}=\xi}^{n}\left|\begin{matrix}\mathfrak{f}_{2}^{m-\varrho,\xi,\xi^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{\varrho-2}{r}\binom{n-\xi^{\prime}}{r}t^{r}\right)&\mathfrak{f}_{2}^{m-\varrho,\xi,\xi^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{\varrho-2}{r-1}\binom{n-\xi^{\prime}+1}{r}t^{r}\right)\\
\mathfrak{f}_{1}^{\xi,\varrho,\varrho^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{\varrho^{\prime}-1}{r+1}\binom{n-\xi-1}{r}t^{r}\right)&\mathfrak{f}_{1}^{\xi,\varrho,\varrho^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{\varrho^{\prime}-1}{r}\binom{n-\xi}{r}t^{r}\right)\end{matrix}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\begin{matrix}\sum\limits_{\xi^{\prime}=\xi}\limits^{n}\mathfrak{f}_{2}^{m-\varrho,\xi,\xi^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{\varrho-2}{r}\binom{n-\xi^{\prime}}{r}t^{r}\right)&\sum\limits_{\xi^{\prime}=\xi}\limits^{n}\mathfrak{f}_{2}^{m-\varrho,\xi,\xi^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{\varrho-2}{r-1}\binom{n-\xi^{\prime}+1}{r}t^{r}\right)\\
\sum\limits_{\varrho^{\prime}=1}\limits^{\varrho}\mathfrak{f}_{1}^{\xi,\varrho,\varrho^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{\varrho^{\prime}-1}{r+1}\binom{n-\xi-1}{r}t^{r}\right)&\sum\limits_{\varrho^{\prime}=1}\limits^{\varrho}\mathfrak{f}_{1}^{\xi,\varrho,\varrho^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{\varrho^{\prime}-1}{r}\binom{n-\xi}{r}t^{r}\right)\end{matrix}\right|

By Lemma
2.4
, we have

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\xi^{\prime}=\xi}\limits^{n}\mathfrak{f}_{2}^{m-\varrho,\xi,\xi^{\prime}}\left(\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{\varrho-2}{r}\binom{n-\xi^{\prime}}{r}t^{r}\right)

(2.83)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\xi^{\prime}=\xi}^{n}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\left(\sum_{r^{\prime}=0}^{r}\binom{\varrho-2}{r-r^{\prime}}\binom{n-\xi^{\prime}}{r-r^{\prime}}\binom{m-\varrho}{r^{\prime}}\binom{\xi^{\prime}-\xi-1}{r^{\prime}-1}\right)t^{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\left[\sum_{r^{\prime}=0}^{r}\left(\sum_{\xi^{\prime}=\xi}^{n}\binom{n-\xi^{\prime}}{r-r^{\prime}}\binom{\xi^{\prime}-\xi-1}{r^{\prime}-1}\right)\binom{\varrho-2}{r-r^{\prime}}\binom{m-\varrho}{r^{\prime}}\right]t^{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\left(\sum_{r^{\prime}=0}^{r}\binom{n-\xi}{r}\binom{\varrho-2}{r-r^{\prime}}\binom{m-\varrho}{r^{\prime}}\right)t^{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{n-\xi}{r}\binom{m-2}{r}t^{r}

Similarly, we can prove other three equations.
Hence

\displaystyle(1-t)^{m+2n+\varrho-\xi-2}{\mathscr{S}}^{m,n,\varrho,\xi}(t)=\left|\begin{matrix}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{m-2}{r}\binom{n-\xi}{r}t^{r}&\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{m-2}{r-1}\binom{n-\xi+1}{r}t^{r}\\
\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{\varrho-1}{r+1}\binom{n-2}{r}t^{r}&\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{\varrho-1}{r}\binom{n-1}{r}t^{r}\end{matrix}\right|

(2.84)

and (
2.73
) holds.

∎

For
i_{1},i_{2},i_{3},j_{1},j_{2},j_{3}\in\overline{1,n}
, we denote

\Delta^{i_{1},i_{2}}_{j_{1},j_{2}}=z_{i_{1},j_{1}}z_{i_{2},j_{2}}-z_{i_{1},j_{2}}z_{i_{2},j_{1}}

(2.85)

and

\displaystyle\Delta^{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}}_{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle z_{i_{1},j_{1}}z_{i_{2},j_{2}}z_{i_{3},j_{3}}+z_{i_{2},j_{1}}z_{i_{3},j_{2}}z_{i_{1},j_{3}}+z_{i_{3},j_{1}}z_{i_{1},j_{2}}z_{i_{2},j_{3}}

(2.86)

\displaystyle-z_{i_{2},j_{1}}z_{i_{1},j_{2}}z_{i_{3},j_{3}}-z_{i_{1},j_{1}}z_{i_{3},j_{2}}z_{i_{2},j_{3}}-z_{i_{3},j_{1}}z_{i_{2},j_{2}}z_{i_{1},j_{3}}.

Denote
\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}=\mathbb{F}\left[z_{i,j}\mid 1\leq i\leq n-n_{1},1\leq j\leq n_{2}\right]
. Denote by
\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}_{m}
the subspace of its homogeneous polynomials with degree
m
.
Set the ideal of
\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\langle z_{i_{0},j_{0}},\Delta^{k_{1}^{\prime},k_{2}^{\prime}}_{k_{1},k_{2}},\Delta^{k_{3}^{\prime},k_{4}^{\prime}}_{k_{3},k_{4}},\Delta^{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}}_{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3}}\big|i_{0}\in\overline{1,n_{2}-n_{1}},j_{0}\in\overline{n_{1}+1,n_{2}};1\leq k_{1}<k_{2}\leq n_{2}-n_{1},

(2.87)

\displaystyle\quad 1\leq k_{1}^{\prime}<k_{2}^{\prime}\leq n_{1},n_{2}-n_{1}+1\leq k_{3}<k_{4}\leq n-n_{1},n_{1}+1\leq k_{3}^{\prime}<k_{4}^{\prime}\leq n_{2};

\displaystyle\quad 1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}\leq n-n_{1},1\leq j_{1}<j_{2}<j_{3}\leq n_{2}\Big\rangle.

then by Lemma
2.1
, the homogeneous ideal of
\mathscr{V}({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle})
is
I
.
Denote
I_{(i)}=I\cap\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}_{m}
, then
I=\bigoplus_{i}I_{(i)}
, and
R=\bigoplus_{i}\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}_{i}/I_{(i)}
is the homogeneous coordinate ring of the associated variety
\mathscr{V}({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle})
.

Proposition 2.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}=sl(n)
,
M={\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
. If
n_{1}\leq n_{2}<n
,
\ell_{1}\leq 0
or
\ell_{2}\leq 0
, the Hilbert polynomial of
\mathscr{V}(M)
is

\mathfrak{p}_{{M}}(k)=P_{2}(n_{2},n-n_{1},n_{1},n_{2}-n_{1}+1,k).

(2.88)

If
n_{1}<n_{2}=n
,

\mathfrak{p}_{{M}}(k)=h_{2}(n-n_{2},n_{1}).

(2.89)

Proof.

(
2.89
) holds by Lemma
2.1
and (
2.26
), we only need to prove (
2.88
).

We take
\leq
be the lexical order on
\overline{1,m}\times\overline{1,n}
. For

S_{1}=\{(j_{1},i_{1}),...,(j_{k},i_{k})\},\quad S_{2}=\{(j_{1}^{\prime},i_{1}^{\prime}),...,(j_{k}^{\prime},i_{k}^{\prime})\}

(2.90)

with
(j_{s},i_{s}),(j_{s}^{\prime},i_{s}^{\prime})\in\overline{1,m}\times\overline{1,n}
.
Since the notion of sets
\{...\}
is independent of the order of its elements, we may assume

(j_{1},i_{1})\leq\cdots\leq(j_{k},i_{k}),\qquad(j_{1}^{\prime},i_{1}^{\prime})\leq\cdots\leq(j_{k}^{\prime},i_{k}^{\prime}).

(2.91)

Take the lexical order
\leq
on
(\overline{1,m}\times\overline{1,n})^{k}
and define
S_{1}\leq S_{2}
if

((j_{1},i_{1}),\dots,(j_{k},i_{k}))\leq((j_{1}^{\prime},i_{1}^{\prime}),\dots,(j_{k}^{\prime},i_{k}^{\prime}))\;\;\mbox{in}\;\;(\overline{1,m}\times\overline{1,n})^{k}.

(2.92)

Assume the above
S_{1}\leq S_{2}
and

T_{1}=\{(m_{1},\ell_{1}),...,(m_{\iota},\ell_{\iota})\},\quad T_{2}=\{(m_{1}^{\prime},\ell_{1}^{\prime}),...,(m_{\iota}^{\prime},\ell_{\iota}^{\prime})\}

(2.93)

with
(m_{t},\ell_{t}),(m_{t}^{\prime},\ell_{t}^{\prime})\in\overline{1,m}\times\overline{1,n}

also satisfying
T_{1}\leq T_{2}
. Then

\{S_{1},T_{1}\}\leq\{S_{2},T_{2}\}.

(2.94)

Consider elements in the set of generating elements of
I
in (
2.87
).
Each
\Delta^{k_{1}^{\prime},k_{2}^{\prime}}_{k_{1},k_{2}}
corresponds to a 2-chain
(k_{1},k_{1}^{\prime})\prec(k_{2},k_{2}^{\prime})
, each
\Delta^{k_{3}^{\prime},k_{4}^{\prime}}_{k_{3},k_{4}}
corresponds to a 2-chain
(k_{3},k_{3}^{\prime})\prec(k_{4},k_{4}^{\prime})
, and each
\Delta^{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}}_{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3}}
corresponds to a 3-chain
(i_{1},j_{1})\prec(i_{2},j_{2})\prec(i_{3},j_{3})
. Thus this set of generating elements corresponds to

\displaystyle S_{I}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\{\{(i_{0},j_{0})\},\{(k_{1},k^{\prime}_{1}),(k_{2},k^{\prime}_{2})\},\{(k_{3},k^{\prime}_{3}),(k_{4},k^{\prime}_{4})\},\{(i_{1},j_{1}),(i_{2},j_{2}),(i_{3},j_{3})\}|

(2.95)

\displaystyle\quad i_{0}\in\overline{1,n_{2}-n_{1}},j_{0}\in\overline{n_{1}+1,n_{2}};1\leq k_{1}<k_{2}\leq n_{2}-n_{1},1\leq k_{1}^{\prime}<k_{2}^{\prime}\leq n_{1},

\displaystyle\quad n_{2}-n_{1}+1\leq k_{3}<k_{4}\leq n-n_{1},n_{1}+1\leq k_{3}^{\prime}<k_{4}^{\prime}\leq n_{2};

\displaystyle\quad 1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}\leq n-n_{1},1\leq j_{1}<j_{2}<j_{3}\leq n_{2}\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{w_{1},\cdots,w_{N}\}

where
N=|S_{I}|
.

We set the linear map
(\cdot)^{T}
from
\mathscr{Z}
to
\mathbb{F}\left[z_{j,i}|i\in\overline{1,m},j\in\overline{1,n}\right]
by

\left(\prod_{1\leq i\leq m,1\leq j\leq n}z_{i,j}^{\gamma_{i,j}}\right)^{T}=\prod_{1\leq i\leq m,1\leq j\leq n}z_{j,i}^{\gamma_{i,j}}.

(2.96)

Then
(f^{T})^{T}=f
for
f\in\mathscr{Z}
.

For
f\in\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}_{i}
, we write
f=\sum_{j=1}^{m_{0}}f_{j}
, where
f_{j}
are distinct monomials in
\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}_{i}
, with
\mathscr{I}(f_{1})\leq\mathscr{I}(f_{2})\leq\cdots\leq\mathscr{I}(f_{m_{0}})
according to the lexical order defined in (
2.92
).
Define
\mathscr{I}(f)=\{\mathscr{I}(f_{1}),\mathscr{I}(f_{2}),\cdots,\mathscr{I}(f_{m_{0}})\}
.
For another
g\in\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}_{i}
with
g=\sum_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}_{0}}g_{j}
where
g_{j}
are distinct monomials in
\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}_{i}
.
We define
\mathscr{I}(f)\leq\mathscr{I}(g)
if
\mathscr{I}(f_{j})\leq\mathscr{I}(g_{j})
for
j\in\overline{1,\min\{m_{0},m_{0}^{\prime}\}}
.

We want to show that there exists a
\widetilde{f}\in\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}_{i}
such that
\widetilde{f}+I_{(i)}=f+I_{(i)}
and

\widetilde{f}^{T}\in\text{Span}\left\{S_{2}^{\overline{1,n_{2}},\overline{1,n-n_{1}}}(n_{1},n_{2}-n_{1}+1,i)\right\}.

(2.97)

by induction on
\mathscr{I}(f)
, since
|\{\mathscr{I}(f)\mid f\in\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}_{i}\}|<\infty
.

When

w_{\eta}\not\subset\mathscr{I}(f_{j})\qquad\mbox{for}\,\,\eta\in\overline{1,N}\text{ and }j\in\overline{1,m_{0}},

(2.98)

we have
f_{j}^{T}\in S_{2}^{\overline{1,n_{2}},\overline{1,n-n_{1}}}(n_{1},n_{2}-n_{1}+1,i)
for
j\in\overline{1,m_{0}}
.
Then we take
\widetilde{f}=f
in this case, and (
2.97
) holds. In particular, when
\mathscr{I}(f)\geq\mathscr{I}(g)
for each
g\in\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}_{i}
, we have
f\in\mathbb{F}z_{n-n_{1},n_{2}}^{i}
. Then we take
\widetilde{f}=f
and (
2.97
) holds.

For
g\in\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}_{i}
with
\mathscr{I}(g)>\nu
, there exists a
\widetilde{g}\in\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}_{i}
such that
\widetilde{g}+I_{(i)}=g+I_{(i)}
and (
2.97
) holds. Consider
f\in\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}_{i}
with
\mathscr{I}(f)=\nu
.

Suppose that there exists some
w_{\eta}\in S_{I}
such that
w_{\eta}\subset\bigcup_{j=1}^{m_{0}}\mathscr{I}(f_{j})
.
Set

\eta_{0}(f)=\min\left\{\eta\in\overline{1,N}\Big|w_{\eta}\subset\bigcup_{j=1}^{m_{0}}\mathscr{I}(f_{j})\right\}.

(2.99)

and

J_{0}=\{j\in\overline{1,m_{0}}|w_{\eta_{0}(f)}\subset\mathscr{I}(f_{j})\}\neq\emptyset.

(2.100)

Now we want to show that there exists
\overline{f}\in\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}_{i}
such that
\overline{f}+I_{(i)}=f+I_{(i)}
,
\mathscr{I}(\overline{f})>\mathscr{I}(f)
or (
2.98
) holds for
\overline{f}
.

(1) When
w_{\eta_{0}(f)}=\{(k_{1},k^{\prime}_{1}),(k_{2},k^{\prime}_{2})\}
for some
k_{1},k_{2}\in\overline{1,n_{2}-n_{1}}
and
k_{1}^{\prime},k_{2}^{\prime}\in\overline{1,n_{1}}
with
k_{1}<k_{2}
and
k_{1}^{\prime}<k_{2}^{\prime}
.

Write
f_{j}=f_{j}^{\prime}z_{k_{1},k_{1}^{\prime}}z_{k_{2},k_{2}^{\prime}}
for
j\in J_{0}
, and
we set

\overline{f}=\sum_{j\not\in J_{0}}f_{j}+\sum_{j\in J_{0}}(f_{j}^{\prime}z_{k_{1},k_{2}^{\prime}}z_{k_{2},k_{1}^{\prime}}).

(2.101)

Then we have

\overline{f}+I_{(i)}=f+I_{(i)}

(2.102)

and

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}(f_{j}^{\prime}z_{k_{1},k_{2}^{\prime}}z_{k_{2},k_{1}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(k_{1},k_{2}^{\prime}),(k_{2},k_{1}^{\prime}),\mathscr{I}(f_{j}^{\prime})\}

(2.103)

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\{(k_{1},k_{1}^{\prime}),(k_{2},k_{2}^{\prime})\mathscr{I}(f_{j}^{\prime})\}=\mathscr{I}(f_{j}),

for
j\in J_{0}
.
Hence
\mathscr{I}(\overline{f})>\mathscr{I}(f)
.

(2) When
w_{\eta_{0}(f)}=\{(k_{3},k^{\prime}_{3}),(k_{4},k^{\prime}_{4})\}
with
n_{2}-n_{1}+1\leq k_{3}<k_{4}\leq n-n_{1}
and
n_{1}+1\leq k_{3}^{\prime}<k_{4}^{\prime}\leq n_{2}
.

Similar to the case (2), we set

\overline{f}=\sum_{j\not\in J_{0}}f_{j}+\sum_{j\in J_{0}}(f_{j}^{\prime}z_{k_{3},k_{4}^{\prime}}z_{k_{3},k_{4}^{\prime}}).

(2.104)

Then we also have
\overline{f}+I_{(i)}=f+I_{(i)}
and
\mathscr{I}(\overline{f})>\mathscr{I}(f)
.

(3) When
w_{\eta_{0}(f)}=\{(i_{1},j_{1}),(i_{2},j_{2}),(i_{3},j_{3})\}
with
1\leq i_{1}<i_{2}<i_{3}\leq n-n_{1},1\leq j_{1}<j_{2}<j_{3}\leq n_{2}
.

Write
f_{j}=z_{i_{1},j_{1}}z_{i_{2},j_{2}}z_{i_{3},j_{3}}f_{j}^{\prime}
for
j\in J_{0}
, we set

\displaystyle\overline{f}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j\not\in J_{0}}f_{j}+\sum_{j\in J_{0}}((z_{i_{2},j_{1}}z_{i_{3},j_{2}}z_{i_{1},j_{3}}+z_{i_{3},j_{1}}z_{i_{1},j_{2}}z_{i_{2},j_{3}}-z_{i_{2},j_{1}}z_{i_{1},j_{2}}z_{i_{3},j_{3}}

(2.105)

\displaystyle\quad-z_{i_{1},j_{1}}z_{i_{3},j_{2}}z_{i_{2},j_{3}}-z_{i_{3},j_{1}}z_{i_{2},j_{2}}z_{i_{1},j_{3}})f_{j}^{\prime}).

Then we have
\overline{f}+I_{(i)}=f+I_{(i)}
and

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}(f_{j})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(i_{1},j_{1}),(i_{2},j_{2}),(i_{3},j_{3}),\mathscr{I}(f_{j}^{\prime})\}

(2.106)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\{(i_{1},j_{2}),(i_{2},j_{1}),(i_{3},j_{3}),\mathscr{I}(f_{j}^{\prime})\}=\mathscr{I}(z_{i_{1},j_{2}}z_{i_{2},j_{1}}z_{i_{3},j_{3}}f_{j}^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}(f_{j})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(i_{1},j_{1}),(i_{2},j_{2}),(i_{3},j_{3}),\mathscr{I}(f_{j}^{\prime})\}

(2.107)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\{(i_{1},j_{3}),(i_{2},j_{2}),(i_{3},j_{1}),\mathscr{I}(f_{j}^{\prime})\}=\mathscr{I}(z_{i_{1},j_{3}}z_{i_{2},j_{2}}z_{i_{3},j_{1}}f_{j}^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}(f_{j})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(i_{1},j_{1}),(i_{2},j_{2}),(i_{3},j_{3}),\mathscr{I}(f_{j}^{\prime})\}

(2.108)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\{(i_{1},j_{1}),(i_{2},j_{3}),(i_{3},j_{2}),\mathscr{I}(f_{j}^{\prime})\}=\mathscr{I}(z_{i_{1},j_{1}}z_{i_{2},j_{3}}z_{i_{3},j_{2}}f_{j}^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}(f_{j})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(i_{1},j_{1}),(i_{2},j_{2}),(i_{3},j_{3}),\mathscr{I}(f_{j}^{\prime})\}

(2.109)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\{(i_{1},j_{2}),(i_{2},j_{3}),(i_{3},j_{1}),\mathscr{I}(f_{j}^{\prime})\}=msrI(z_{i_{1},j_{2}}z_{i_{2},j_{3}}z_{i_{3},j_{1}}f_{j}^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}(f_{j})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(i_{1},j_{1}),(i_{2},j_{2}),(i_{3},j_{3}),\mathscr{I}(f_{j}^{\prime})\}

(2.110)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\{(i_{1},j_{3}),(i_{2},j_{1}),(i_{3},j_{2}),\mathscr{I}(f_{j}^{\prime})\}=\mathscr{I}(z_{i_{1},j_{3}}z_{i_{2},j_{1}}z_{i_{3},j_{2}}f_{j}^{\prime}).

Hence
\mathscr{I}(\overline{f})>\mathscr{I}(f)
.

(4) When
w_{\eta_{0}(f)}=\{(i_{0},j_{0})\}
for some
i_{0}\in\overline{1,n_{2}-n_{1}}
and
j_{0}\in\overline{n_{1}+1,n_{2}}
, we have
f_{j}\in I_{(i)}
for
j\in J_{0}
.

We set

F(f)=f-\sum_{j\in J_{0}}f_{j},

(2.111)

thus
F(f)+I_{(i)}=f+I_{(i)}
,
\mathscr{I}(F(f))\geq\mathscr{I}(f)
and
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\not\in\bigcup_{j=1}^{m_{0}}\mathscr{I}(f_{j})
for
(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in\overline{1,n_{2}-n_{1}}\times\overline{n_{1}+1,n_{2}}
.
If
\eta_{0}(f)=N
, we take
\overline{f}=F(f)
and (
2.98
) holds for
\overline{f}
.
When
\eta_{0}(f)<N
, we have
\eta_{0}(F(f))>\eta_{0}(f)
.
By induction on
\eta_{0}(f)
, there exists
\overline{F(f)}\in\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}_{i}
such that
\overline{F(f)}+I_{(i)}=F(f)+I_{(i)}
,
\mathscr{I}(\overline{F(f)})>\mathscr{I}(F(f))
or (
2.98
) holds for
\overline{F(f)}
.
We take
\overline{f}=\overline{F(f)}
, then
\overline{f}+I_{(i)}=f+I_{(i)}
,
\mathscr{I}(\overline{f})>\mathscr{I}(f)
or (
2.98
) holds for
\overline{f}
.

By inductional assumption, there exists a
\widetilde{\overline{f}}\in\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}_{i}
such that
\widetilde{\overline{f}}+I_{(i)}=\overline{f}+I_{(i)}
and

\widetilde{\overline{f}}^{T}\in\text{Span}\left\{S_{2}^{\overline{1,n_{2}},\overline{1,n-n_{1}}}(n_{1},n_{2}-n_{1}+1,i)\right\}.

(2.112)

We take
\widetilde{f}=\widetilde{\overline{f}}
, then
\widetilde{f}+I_{(i)}=f+I_{(i)}
and (
2.97
) holds.
Moreover, according to our inductive process, we can specifically determine the
\widetilde{f}
related to
f
.

Note that
\widetilde{F_{1}+F_{2}}=\widetilde{F_{1}}+\widetilde{F_{2}}
for
F_{1},F_{2}\in\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}_{i}
.
Hence we can define a linear map
\varPhi
from
\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}_{i}
to
\text{Span}\left\{S_{2}^{\overline{1,n_{2}},\overline{1,n-n_{1}}}(n_{1},n_{2}-n_{1}+1,i)\right\}
by
\varPhi(f)=\widetilde{f}^{T}
.

Suppose
f\in\ker\varPhi
, we have
f\in I_{(i)}
since
\widetilde{f}+I_{(i)}=f+I_{(i)}
. Hence
\ker\varPhi\subset I_{(i)}
.

Denote by
\mathfrak{W}_{j}
the generating element in (
2.87
) corresponding to
w_{j}
in (
2.95
) and by
\mathfrak{w}_{j}
the corresponding monomial in
\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}
for
j\in\overline{1,N}
. For example,
\mathfrak{W}_{j}=\Delta^{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}}_{j_{1},j_{2},j_{3}}
and
\mathfrak{w}_{j}=z_{i_{1},j_{1}}z_{i_{2},j_{2}}z_{i_{3},j_{3}}
if
w_{j}=\{(i_{1},j_{1}),(i_{2},j_{2}),(i_{3},j_{3})\}
.

Now we suppose
f=f^{\prime}\mathfrak{W}_{j}
where
f^{\prime}
is a monomial in
\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}
.
When
j=1
, we have
\overline{f}=f^{\prime}\overline{\mathfrak{W}_{1}}=0
, thus
\varPhi(f)=0
.
We assume that
\varPhi(f)=\varPhi(f^{\prime}\mathfrak{W}_{j})=0
for
j\in\overline{1,j_{0}-1}
. Consider
j=j_{0}
, we write
f=\sum_{l=1}^{m_{1}}f_{l}
where
f_{l}
are distinct monomials in
\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}
.
By the inductional assumption,
\varPhi(f)=\varPhi(g)
if
f-g\in\langle\mathfrak{W}_{j}|j\in\overline{1,j_{0}-1}\rangle
.
When
w_{s}\not\subset\mathscr{I}(f_{l})
for
s\in\overline{1,j_{0}-1}
and
l\in\overline{1,m_{1}}
, we have
\overline{f}=f^{\prime}\overline{\mathfrak{W}_{j_{0}}}=0
, thus
\varPhi(f)=0
.
Otherwise, we suppose that
w_{s}\subset\mathscr{I}(f_{l})
for some
s\in\overline{1,j_{0}-1}
and
l\in\overline{1,m_{1}}
. We write
f_{l}=f_{l}^{\prime}\mathfrak{w}_{s}
, then
\varPhi(f)=\varPhi(f-f_{l}^{\prime}\mathfrak{W}_{s})
and
\mathscr{I}(f-f_{l}^{\prime}\mathfrak{W}_{s})>\mathscr{I}(f)
.
Continue this process for
f-f_{l}^{\prime}\mathfrak{W}_{s}
, we can prove that there exists
g\in\langle\mathfrak{W}_{j}|j\in\overline{1,j_{0}-1}\rangle
such that
w_{s}\not\subset\mathscr{I}(h_{l})
for each
l
, where
h_{l}
are monomials in
f-g=\sum_{l}h_{l}
.
Hence
\varPhi(f)=0
.
Note that
\varPhi
is a linear map, we have
\varPhi(I_{(i)})=\{0\}
.
Therefore,
\ker\varPhi=I_{(i)}
.

Then
\varPhi
induces a linear isomorphism
\Phi
from
\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}_{i}/I_{(i)}
to
\text{Span}\left\{S_{2}^{\overline{1,n_{2}},\overline{1,n-n_{1}}}(n_{1},n_{2}-n_{1}+1,i)\right\}
by

\Phi(f+I_{(i)})=\varPhi(f).

(2.113)

Hence

\dim(\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}_{i}/I_{(i)})=\left|\left\{S_{2}^{\overline{1,n_{2}},\overline{1,n-n_{1}}}(n_{1},n_{2}-n_{1}+1,i)\right\}\right|=P_{2}(n_{2},n-n_{1},n_{1},n_{2}-n_{1}+1,i).

(2.114)

Therefore,

\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)=P_{2}(n_{2},n-n_{1},n_{1},n_{2}-n_{1}+1,k).

(2.115)

and the lemma holds.
∎

From (
2.49
), Proposition
2.1
and Proposition
2.2
, we have our main theorem in this section:

Theorem 2.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}=sl(n)
,
M={\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
.
If
n_{1}\leq n_{2}<n
,
\ell_{1}\leq 0
or
\ell_{2}\leq 0
, the Hilbert series of
\mathscr{V}(M)
is

\displaystyle\mathfrak{s}_{{}_{M}}(t)=\frac{\sum\limits_{r,s=0}\limits^{\infty}\left|\begin{matrix}\binom{n_{2}-2}{r}\binom{n-n_{2}-1}{r}&\binom{n_{2}-2}{r-1}\binom{n-n_{2}}{r}\\
\binom{n_{1}-1}{s+1}\binom{n-n_{1}-2}{s}&\binom{n_{1}-1}{s}\binom{n-n_{1}-1}{s}\end{matrix}\right|t^{r+s}}{(1-t)^{2n-3}}

(2.116)

If
n_{1}<n_{2}=n
,

\mathfrak{s}_{{}_{M}}(t)=\frac{\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{n-n_{2}-1}{r}\binom{n_{1}-1}{r}t^{r}}{\left(1-t\right)^{n-n_{2}+n_{1}-1}}.

(2.117)

This completes the proof of Theorem 1 that we stated in the introduction.

Recall that the constant term of the Hilbert polynomial
\mathfrak{p}_{M}
is the arithmetic genus of
\mathscr{V}(M)
, which we denote as
p_{a}(\mathscr{V}(M))
, then we can find
p_{a}(\mathscr{V}(M))
by the above corollary.

Corollary 2.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}=sl(n)
and
M={\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
under the conditions stated in Lemma
2.1
, the arithmetic genus of
\mathscr{V}(M)
is 1.

Proof.

When
n_{1}\leq n_{2}<n
,
\ell_{1}\leq 0
or
\ell_{2}\leq 0
, we have

\displaystyle p_{a}(\mathscr{V}(M))=\mathfrak{s}_{{}_{M}}(0)=\left|\begin{matrix}\binom{n_{2}-2}{0}\binom{n-n_{2}-1}{0}&\binom{n_{2}-2}{0-1}\binom{n-n_{2}}{0}\\
\binom{n_{1}-1}{1}\binom{n-n_{1}-2}{0}&\binom{n_{1}-1}{r}\binom{n-n_{1}-1}{0}\end{matrix}\right|=1,

(2.118)

by Proposition
2.1
and Corollary
2.1
.
When
n_{1}<n_{2}=n
, we also have
p_{a}(\mathscr{V}(M))=1
by (
2.26
).
Therefore, the arithmetic genus of
\mathscr{V}(M)
is 1.
∎

Note that the Hilbert series may be rewritten as
\mathfrak{s}_{M}(t)=\frac{Q(t)}{(1-t)^{d}}
, where
Q(t)
is a polynomial with integer coefficients, and
d
is the Krull dimension of the coordinate ring of the variety
\mathscr{V}(M)
.
Then the degree of the variety is
\deg(\mathscr{V}(M))=Q(1)=(d-1)!\cdot lc(\mathfrak{p}_{{M}})
.
Hence we have the following corollary from Proposition
2.2
.

Corollary 2.4
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}=sl(n)
and
M={\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
.

\deg(\mathscr{V}({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}))=\begin{cases}\left(\frac{n-2}{n_{1}-1}-\frac{n-2}{n_{2}-1}\right)\binom{n-3}{n_{1}-2}\binom{n-3}{n_{2}-2},\ \quad\text{if }n_{2}\neq n\text{ and }n_{1}\neq n_{2};\vskip 5.69046pt\\
\frac{1}{n_{1}-1}\binom{n-4}{n_{1}-2}\binom{n-3}{n_{1}-2},\ \quad\text{if }1<n_{1}=n_{2}<n-1;\vskip 5.69046pt\\
\binom{n+n_{1}-n_{2}-2}{n_{1}-1},\ \quad\text{if }n_{1}\neq n_{2}=n;\vskip 5.69046pt\\
1,\ \quad\text{if }1=n_{1}=n_{2}<n\text{ or }n_{1}=n_{2}=n-1.\end{cases}

(2.119)

Proof.

According to Proposition
2.1
and Corollary
2.1
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{s}_{{}_{M}}(t)=\frac{\left|\begin{matrix}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{n_{2}-2}{r}\binom{n-n_{2}-1}{r}t^{r}&\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{n_{2}-2}{r-1}\binom{n-n_{2}}{r}t^{r}\\
\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{n_{1}-1}{r+1}\binom{n-n_{1}-2}{r}t^{r}&\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{n_{1}-1}{r}\binom{n-n_{1}-1}{r}t^{r}\end{matrix}\right|}{(1-t)^{2n-3}},

(2.120)

when
n_{1}\leq n_{2}<n
,
\ell_{1}\leq 0
or
\ell_{2}\leq 0
.
Note that

\dim(I(\mathscr{V}(M)))=\begin{cases}2n-3,\ \quad\text{if }n_{2}\neq n\text{ and }n_{1}\neq n_{2};\\
2n-4,\ \quad\text{if }1<n_{1}=n_{2}<n-1;\\
n-1,\ \quad\text{if }1=n_{1}=n_{2}<n\text{ or }n_{1}=n_{2}=n-1\text{ or }n_{1}\neq n_{2}=n.\end{cases}

(2.121)

Hence, when
n_{2}\neq n
and
n_{1}\neq n_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\deg(\mathscr{V}(M))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\begin{matrix}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{n_{2}-2}{r}\binom{n-n_{2}-1}{r}&\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{n_{2}-2}{r-1}\binom{n-n_{2}}{r}\\
\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{n_{1}-1}{r+1}\binom{n-n_{1}-2}{r}&\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\binom{n_{1}-1}{r}\binom{n-n_{1}-1}{r}\end{matrix}\right|

(2.122)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\begin{matrix}\binom{n-3}{n_{2}-2}&\binom{n-2}{n_{2}-1}\vskip 5.69046pt\\
\binom{n-3}{n_{1}-2}&\binom{n-2}{n_{1}-1}\end{matrix}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\binom{n-3}{n_{1}-2}\binom{n-3}{n_{2}-2}\left(\frac{n-2}{n_{1}-1}-\frac{n-2}{n_{2}-1}\right)

by Lemma
2.4
.

When
n_{1}<n_{2}=n
, according to (
2.121
), (
2.89
) and (
2.27
), we have

\deg\mathscr{V}(M)=\binom{n+n_{1}-n_{2}-2}{n_{1}-1}.

(2.123)

When
1<n_{1}=n_{2}<n-1
, we have
\mathscr{V}(M)\cong\mathscr{V}_{3}(n-n_{1},n_{1})
, and then

\deg(\mathscr{V}(M))=\frac{1}{n_{1}-1}\binom{n-4}{n_{1}-2}\binom{n-3}{n_{1}-2}.

(2.124)

by (
2.28
) and Lemma
2.4
.

When
1=n_{1}=n_{2}<n
or
n_{1}=n_{2}=n-1
, we have
\mathscr{V}(M)\cong\mathbb{P}^{n-2}
, and then

\deg(\mathscr{V}(M))=1.

(2.125)

Therefore, (
2.119
) holds.

∎

By the same method, we can derive the following more general conclusion.

Proposition 2.3
.

For
m,n,\varrho,\xi\in\mathbb{N}
with
1\leq\varrho\leq m
and
1\leq\xi\leq n
, we have

\displaystyle lc_{r}(P_{1}(m,n,\xi,r))\cdot(\deg_{r}(P_{1}(m,n,\xi,r))!)

(2.126)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}1,\ \quad\text{if }m=1\text{ or }n<3;\vskip 5.69046pt\\
\frac{1}{n-1}\binom{m+n-4}{m-2}\binom{m+n-3}{n-2},\ \quad\text{if }\xi=1,m\geq 2\text{ and }n\geq 3;\vskip 5.69046pt\\
\frac{\xi-1}{n-1}\binom{m+n-\xi-2}{m-2}\binom{m+n-3}{n-2},\ \quad\text{else. }\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle lc_{r}(P_{2}(m,n,\varrho,\xi,r))\cdot(\deg_{r}(P_{2}(m,n,\varrho,\xi,r))!)

(2.127)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}1,\ \quad\text{if }m=1\text{ or }n=1;\vskip 5.69046pt\\
\frac{1}{n-1}\binom{m+n-4}{m-2}\binom{m+n-3}{n-2},\ \quad\text{if }m=\varrho,\xi=1\text{ and }m,n>1;\vskip 5.69046pt\\
\frac{(m-\varrho)(n-1)+(\varrho-1)(\xi-1)}{(m-1)(n-1)}\binom{m+n-\xi-2}{m-2}\binom{\varrho+n-3}{n-2},\ \quad\text{else. }\end{cases}

3
Relations between
\mathfrak{p}_{M}
and
\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}

Recall that the Hilbert polynomial
\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(k)
depends on the choice of the subspace
M_{0}
.
In this section, we study the relationship between
\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(k)
and
\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)
.
We show that the Hilbert polynomial
\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(k)
of such a completely reducible module
M
satisfies
\mathfrak{p}_{{}_{M}}(k)\leq\mathfrak{p}_{{}_{M},_{M_{0}}}(k)
for sufficiently large positive integer
k
.
In particular, when
\mathfrak{g}=sl(n)
and
M=\mathscr{H}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
, we find a necessary and sufficient condition for
\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)=\mathfrak{p}_{M,\mathbb{F}v}(k)
. Recall
M_{k}=(U_{k}(\mathfrak{g}))(M_{0})
and
M_{k}^{\prime}=(U_{k}(\mathfrak{g}))(M_{0}^{\prime})
.

Lemma 3.1
.

Suppose that
M_{0}
and
M_{0}^{\prime}
are finite-dimensional subspaces of
M
satisfying
(U(\mathfrak{g}))(M_{0})=(U(\mathfrak{g}))(M_{0}^{\prime})=M
.
If
\dim(M_{k})\leq\dim(M_{k}^{\prime})
for sufficiently large
k
, then
\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(k)\leq\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}^{\prime}}(k)
for sufficiently large
k
.
In particular, if
M_{0}\subset M_{0}^{\prime}
, we have
\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(k)\leq\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}^{\prime}}(k)
for sufficiently large
k
.

Proof.

Assume that
\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}^{\prime}}\neq\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}
.
By the definition of
\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}
, there exist
N_{1},N_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(i)=\dim(M_{i})-\dim(M_{i-1})
for
i\geq N_{1}
and
\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}^{\prime}}(i)=\dim(M_{i}^{\prime})-\dim(M_{i-1}^{\prime})
for
i\geq N_{2}
.
Define

d_{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{N_{1}}\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(i)-\dim(M_{N_{1}}),\quad d_{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{N_{2}}\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}^{\prime}}(i)-\dim(M_{N_{2}})\in\mathbb{N}.

(3.1)

Then, for sufficiently large
k
,

\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(i)=d_{1}+\dim(M_{k}),\quad\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}^{\prime}}(i)=d_{2}+\dim(M_{k}^{\prime}).

(3.2)

Since

\sum_{i=1}^{k}i^{p}=\frac{1}{p+1}k^{p+1}+\text{lower degree terms in }k,

(3.3)

for
p\in\mathbb{N}
,

\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}^{\prime}}(i)-\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(i))+d_{1}-d_{2}=\dim(M_{k}^{\prime})-\dim(M_{k})

(3.4)

is a polynomial in
k
with positive degree and takes nonnegative integers when
k
sufficiently large.
Thus the leading coefficient of
\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}^{\prime}}(i)-\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(i))
is positive.
By (
3.3
), the leading coefficient of
\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}^{\prime}}(i)-\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(i)
is also positive. Hence,
\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(k)\leq\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}^{\prime}}(k)
for sufficiently large
k
.
∎

For
i\in\mathbb{N}
, we denote by
S^{i}(\mathfrak{g})
the subspace of homogeneous polynomials with the degree
i
.
Denote

\left(\operatorname{Ann}_{S(\mathfrak{g})}(\overline{M})\right)^{i}=\operatorname{Ann}_{S(\mathfrak{g})}(\overline{M})\cap S^{i}(\mathfrak{g}),

(3.5)

and

I_{(i)}=\sqrt{\operatorname{Ann}_{S(\mathfrak{g})}(\overline{M})}\cap S^{i}(\mathfrak{g}),

(3.6)

Denote by
I(\mathscr{V}(M))=\bigoplus_{i}I_{(i)}
the homogeneous ideal of
\mathscr{V}(M)
and
R(M)=\bigoplus_{i}S^{i}(\mathfrak{g})/I_{(i)}
is the homogeneous coordinate ring of the associated variety
\mathscr{V}(M)
.

We say that
M_{0}
is radical for
M
, if

I_{(i)}=\left(\operatorname{Ann}_{S(\mathfrak{g})}(\overline{M})\right)^{i}

(3.7)

for
i
large enough.
Note that for
p\in\mathbb{N}
,
M_{p}=(U_{p}(\mathfrak{g}))(M_{0})
is radical for
M
, if
M_{0}
is radical for
M
.

Lemma 3.2
.

M
is a finitely generated
U(\mathfrak{g})
-module. We take
M_{0}=\mathbb{F}v_{0}
, where
v_{0}\in M
and
(U(\mathfrak{g}))(\mathbb{F}v_{0})=M
. Then we have

\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)\leq\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(k).

(3.8)

Moreover,
\mathbb{F}v_{0}
is radical for
M
if and only if

\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)=\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(k).

(3.9)

Proof.

For
f\in U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})
, we denote by
\overline{f}\in S^{p}(\mathfrak{g})
the image of
f
under the natural map from
U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})
to
U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})/U_{k-1}(\mathfrak{g})
.

For
p
large enough, we define the linear map
\theta_{p}:M_{p}\rightarrow S^{p}(\mathfrak{g})
by
\theta_{p}(f(v_{0}))=\overline{f}
with
f\in U_{p}(\mathfrak{g})
.

Suppose that
f\in U_{p}(\mathfrak{g})
satisfying
f(v_{0})\in M_{p-1}
.
For any
v\in M_{k}
, we may assume that
v=g(v_{0})
with
g\in U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})
.
Then

f(v)=f(g(v_{0}))\in g(f(v_{0}))+M_{k+p-1}\subset M_{k+p-1}.

(3.10)

Hence
\theta_{p}(M_{p-1})\subset I_{(p)}
, and we can define the linear map
\overline{\theta_{p}}:M_{p}/M_{p-1}\rightarrow S^{p}(\mathfrak{g})/I_{(p)}
by
\theta_{p}(f(v_{0})+M_{p-1})=\overline{f}+I_{(p)}
with
f\in U_{p}(\mathfrak{g})
for
p
large enough.
Since
\theta_{p}
is surjective,
\overline{\theta_{p}}
is surjective. This yields

\dim(M_{p}/M_{p-1})=\dim(S^{p}(\mathfrak{g})/I_{(p)})

(3.11)

for
p
large enough.
Therefore, (
3.8
) holds.

The injectivity of
\theta_{p}
for sufficiently large
p
is equivalent to (
3.9
). That is,

\ker\overline{\theta_{p}}=0+M_{p-1},

(3.12)

or equivalently, for any
f\in U_{p}(\mathfrak{g})
such that
\overline{f}\in I_{(p)}
, we have
f(v_{0})\in M_{p-1}
.

When
I_{(p)}=\left(\operatorname{Ann}_{S(\mathfrak{g})}(\overline{M})\right)^{p}
for
p
large enough, we have
\overline{f}\in\left(\operatorname{Ann}_{S(\mathfrak{g})}(\overline{M})\right)^{p}
and
f(v_{0})\in M_{p-1}
.
On the other hand, for
f\in I_{(p)}
,
f(v_{0})\in M_{p-1}
yields that
f(v)\in M_{p+k-1}
for any
v\in M_{k}
. Then
f\in\left(\operatorname{Ann}_{S(\mathfrak{g})}(\overline{M})\right)^{p}
.
∎

Corollary 3.1
.

For a finitely generated completely reducible infinite-dimensional
U(\mathfrak{g})
-module
M
with
M_{0}\in M
such that
(U(\mathfrak{g}))(M_{0})=M
, we have
\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)\leq\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(k)
.
In particular, when
M
is irreducible,
\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)\leq\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(k)
holds.

Proof.

We assume that

M=\bigoplus_{t=1}^{s}M^{(t)}

(3.13)

where
M^{(t)}
are irreducible modules for
t\in\overline{1,s}
.
Since
(U(\mathfrak{g}))(M_{0})=M
, we can also decompose
M_{0}=\bigoplus_{t=1}^{s}M^{(t)}_{0}
such that
(U(\mathfrak{g}))(M^{(t)}_{0})=M^{(t)}
for
t\in\overline{1,s}
.
Then

\overline{M}=\bigoplus_{t=1}^{s}\overline{M^{(t)}}.

(3.14)

Since the Hilbert polynomial is additive with respect to exact sequences,

\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(k)=\sum_{t=1}^{s}\mathfrak{p}_{M^{(t)},M^{(t)}_{0}}(k).

(3.15)

For
t\in\overline{1,s}
, we take
v_{t}\in M^{(t)}_{0}
with
v_{t}\neq 0
, and then
(U(\mathfrak{g}))(\mathbb{F}v_{t})=M^{(t)}
by the irreducibility of
M^{(t)}
.
According to Lemma
3.2
and Lemma
3.1
, we have

\mathfrak{p}_{M^{(t)},M^{(t)}_{0}}(k)\geq\mathfrak{p}_{M^{(t)},\mathbb{F}v_{t}}(k)\geq\mathfrak{p}_{M^{(t)}}(k).

(3.16)

According to Lemma 4.2 in
[
18
]
, we have

\mathscr{V}(M)=\bigcup_{t=1}^{s}\mathscr{V}(M^{(t)}).

(3.17)

Thus

\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)\leq\sum_{t=1}^{s}\mathfrak{p}_{M^{(t)}}(k).

(3.18)

Hence,

\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(k)\geq\sum_{t=1}^{s}\mathfrak{p}_{M^{(t)}}(k)\geq\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)

(3.19)

by (
3.15
), (
3.16
) and (
3.18
).

∎

We set

\eta=\sum_{i\in J_{1}}y_{i}\partial_{x_{i}}+\sum_{r\in J_{2}}x_{r}y_{r}+\sum_{s\in J_{3}}x_{s}\partial_{y_{s}}.

(3.20)

Then we have

\eta\tilde{\pi}(E_{i,j})=\tilde{\pi}(E_{i,j})\eta\qquad\mbox{for}\;\;i,j\in\overline{1,n}

(3.21)

and

\eta(\mathscr{H}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle})\subset\mathscr{A}_{\langle\ell_{1}+1,\ell_{2}+1\rangle}.

(3.22)

For
n_{1},n_{2},n\in\mathbb{N}
with
n_{1}\leq n_{2}\leq n
, we denote

{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}^{n_{1},n_{2},n}={\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}\qquad\tilde{\Delta}^{n_{1},n_{2},n}=\tilde{\Delta},\qquad\mathfrak{d}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}^{n_{1},n_{2},n}=\mathfrak{d},

(3.23)

and

({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}^{n_{1},n_{2},n})_{k}=U(\mathfrak{g})(V_{0}).

(3.24)

For any
g\in\mathscr{H}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}^{n_{1},n_{2},n}
and
0<m\in\mathbb{N}
,

\tilde{\Delta}(\eta^{m}(g))=m(-\ell_{1}-\ell_{2}+n_{1}-n_{2}-m+1)\eta^{m-1}(g).

(3.25)

Thus, when
-\ell_{1}-\ell_{2}+n_{1}-n_{2}+1\geq 0
,

\eta^{-\ell_{1}-\ell_{2}+n_{1}-n_{2}+1}({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}^{n_{1},n_{2},n})\subset{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle n_{1}-n_{2}-\ell_{2}+1,n_{1}-n_{2}-\ell_{1}+1\rangle}^{n_{1},n_{2},n}.

(3.26)

Furthermore, for
g\in\mathscr{H}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}^{n_{1},n_{2},n}
, we have

\mathfrak{d}_{\langle\ell_{1}+1,\ell_{2}+1\rangle}^{n_{1},n_{2},n}(\eta(g))=\mathfrak{d}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}^{n_{1},n_{2},n}(g)+2.

(3.27)

Then for
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\eta^{-\ell_{1}-\ell_{2}+n_{1}-n_{2}+1}\left(\left(\mathscr{H}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}^{n_{1},n_{2},n}\right)_{k}\right)\subset\left({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle n_{1}-n_{2}-\ell_{2}+1,n_{1}-n_{2}-\ell_{1}+1\rangle}^{n_{1},n_{2},n}\right)_{k+2(-\ell_{1}-\ell_{2}+n_{1}-n_{2}+1)},

(3.28)

when
-\ell_{1}-\ell_{2}+n_{1}-n_{2}+1\geq 0
, by (
3.21
) and (
3.27
).

Denote

L=(J_{3}\times J_{1})\cup(J_{2}\times J_{1})\cup(J_{3}\times J_{2})

(3.29)

and

L^{a}=(J_{1}\times J_{3})\cup(J_{2}\times J_{3})\cup(J_{1}\times J_{2}).

(3.30)

We define the Lie subalgebra
\mathfrak{a}=\text{Span}\{E_{i,j},E_{r,r}-E_{r+1,r+1}\mid(i,j)\notin L,1\leq r\leq n-1\}
.
We denote

\mathfrak{g}_{+}=sl(n)_{+}=\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\mathbb{F}E_{i,j}.

(3.31)

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}=sl(n)
and
M={\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}\neq 0
be an infinite-dimensional
\mathfrak{g}
-module with
n_{1}<n_{2}
,
\ell_{1}\leq 0
or
\ell_{2}\leq 0
.
M_{0}
is a finite-dimensional
U(\mathfrak{a})
-invariant subspace (i.e.
U(\mathfrak{a})(X_{0})\subset X_{0}
). If there exists
f\in M_{0}\cap V_{0}
with
f\neq 0
(cf. (
2.18
)), we have
V_{0}\subset M_{0}
.

Proof.

Denote

\displaystyle\overline{J}=\begin{cases}J_{1}&\text{if }\ell_{1}\leq 0,\\
J_{2}&\text{if }\ell_{1}>0;\end{cases}\qquad\text{ and }\quad\overline{J}^{\prime}=\begin{cases}J_{3}&\text{if }\ell_{2}\leq 0,\\
J_{2}&\text{if }\ell_{2}>0.\end{cases}

(3.32)

Write

f=\sum_{k=1}^{N}\left(a_{k}\prod_{s=1}^{|\ell_{1}|}x_{i_{k,s}}\prod_{t=1}^{|\ell_{2}|}y_{j_{k,t}}\right)

(3.33)

with
0\neq a_{k}\in\mathbb{F}
,
i_{k,s}\in\overline{J}
,
j_{k,t}\in\overline{J}^{\prime}
for
s\in\overline{1,|\ell_{1}|}
,
t\in\overline{1,|\ell_{2}|}
and
k\in\overline{1,N}
.
For convenience, we assume that the monomials
\{\prod_{s=1}^{|\ell_{1}|}x_{i_{k,s}}\prod_{t=1}^{|\ell_{2}|}y_{j_{k,t}}\}_{1\leq k\leq N}
are distinct.

Consider the generators of
V_{0}
and we set

g=\prod_{s=1}^{|\ell_{1}|}x_{i^{\prime}_{s}}\prod_{t=1}^{|\ell_{2}|}y_{j^{\prime}_{t}}

(3.34)

where
i^{\prime}_{s}\in\overline{J}
,
j^{\prime}_{t}\in\overline{J}^{\prime}
for
s\in\overline{1,|\ell_{1}|}
and
t\in\overline{1,|\ell_{2}|}
.
Denote

n_{x}=|\{i_{1,s}\mid s\in\overline{1,|\ell_{1}|}\}\cap\{i^{\prime}_{s}|1\leq s\leq|\ell_{1}|\}|,

(3.35)

n_{y}=|\{j_{1,t}\mid s\in\overline{1,|\ell_{2}|}\}\cap\{j^{\prime}_{t}|1\leq t\leq|\ell_{2}|\}|.

(3.36)
