For convenience, we assume that

\{i_{1,s}\mid s\in\overline{1,n_{x}}\}=\{i_{1,s}\mid s\in\overline{1,|\ell_{1}|}\}\cap\{i^{\prime}_{s}|1\leq s\leq|\ell_{1}|\},

(3.37)

\{j_{1,t}\mid t\in\overline{1,n_{y}}\}=\{j_{1,t}\mid t\in\overline{1,|\ell_{2}|}\}\cap\{j^{\prime}_{t}|1\leq t\leq|\ell_{2}|\},

(3.38)

and

\displaystyle n_{x}+n_{y}\leq\min\big\{\left|\{i_{k,s},j_{k,t}\mid s\in\overline{1,|\ell_{1}|},t\in\overline{1,|\ell_{2}|}\}\cap\{i^{\prime}_{s},j^{\prime}_{t}|1\leq s\leq|\ell_{1}|,1\leq t\leq|\ell_{2}|\}\right|\big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad k\in\overline{1,N}\big\}.

(3.39)

Since
X_{0}
is
U(\mathfrak{a})
-invariant, we have
E_{i,j}(X_{0})\subset X_{0}
if
(i,j)\not\in L
.

Then

\displaystyle\prod_{t=n_{y}+1}^{|\ell_{2}|}E_{j_{1,t},j^{\prime}_{t}}\prod_{s=n_{x}+1}^{|\ell_{1}|}E_{i_{1,s},i^{\prime}_{s}}(f)

(3.40)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{t=n_{y}+1}^{|\ell_{2}|}\left(y_{j^{\prime}_{t}}\partial_{y_{j_{1,t}}}\right)\prod_{s=n_{x}+1}^{|\ell_{1}|}\left(-x_{i^{\prime}_{s}}\partial_{x_{i_{1,s}}}\right)(f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{t=n_{y}+1}^{|\ell_{2}|}\left(y_{j^{\prime}_{t}}\partial_{y_{j_{1,t}}}\right)\prod_{s=n_{x}+1}^{|\ell_{1}|}\left(-x_{i^{\prime}_{s}}\partial_{x_{i_{1,s}}}\right)\left(a_{1}\prod_{s=1}^{|\ell_{1}|}x_{i_{1,s}}\prod_{t=1}^{|\ell_{2}|}y_{j_{1,t}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c\cdot g\in X_{0}

with some
0\neq c\in\mathbb{F}
.
Hence
M_{0}\subset X_{0}
and the lemma holds.
∎

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}=sl(n)
and let
M={\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
be an infinite-dimensional
\mathfrak{g}
-module under the conditions in Lemma
2.1
. If

N
is a submodule of
M
, then
N=M
or
\eta^{m}(({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1}-m,\ell_{2}-m\rangle}))\subset N
with
m=\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}+n_{2}-n_{1}-1
.

Proof.

Since
\mathscr{A}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
is nilpotent with respect to
sl(n)_{+}
, there exists
v\in N
such that
\mathfrak{g}_{+}(v)=0
.
Then
N=M
if
\dim(\{v\in M|\mathfrak{g}_{+}(v)\})=1
.
According to the Theorem 6.4.3, the Theorem 6.5.1 and the Theorem 6.6.3 in
[
33
]
, we only need to consider the follwing: (1)
n_{1}+1<n_{2}
and
\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}+n_{2}-n_{1}-1>0
; (2)
n_{1}+1<n_{2}=n
and
n_{1}-n+1-\ell_{2}<\ell_{1}\leq n_{1}-n+1
; (3)
n_{1}+1=n_{2}
,
\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}>0
and
\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\leq 0
.

(1) Case 1 :
n_{1}+1<n_{2}
and
\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}+n_{2}-n_{1}-1>0
.

When
n_{1}+1<n_{2}
and
\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}+n_{2}-n_{1}-1>0
, according to Lemma 6.4.2 in
[
33
]
, when
\mathfrak{g}_{+}(g)=0
, yields that

g\in\text{Span}\{f^{0}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle},\eta^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}+n_{2}-n_{1}-1}(f^{0}_{\langle-\ell_{2}+n_{1}+1-n_{2},-\ell_{1}+n_{1}+1-n_{2}\rangle})\},

(3.41)

where

\displaystyle f^{0}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}=\begin{cases}x_{n_{1}+1}^{\ell_{1}}y_{n_{2}}^{\ell_{2}}&\text{if }\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\geq 0;\\
x_{n_{1}+1}^{\ell_{1}}y_{n_{2}+1}^{-\ell_{2}}&\text{if }\ell_{1}\geq 0,\ell_{2}\leq 0;\\
x_{n_{1}}^{-\ell_{1}}y_{n_{2}}^{\ell_{2}}&\text{if }\ell_{1}\leq 0,\ell_{2}\geq 0;\\
x_{n_{1}}^{-\ell_{1}}y_{n_{2}+1}^{-\ell_{2}}&\text{if }\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\leq 0.\end{cases}

(3.42)

According to (
3.21
),

\eta^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}+n_{2}-n_{1}-1}(f^{0}_{\langle-\ell_{2}+n_{1}+1-n_{2},-\ell_{1}+n_{1}+1-n_{2}\rangle})\in N

(3.43)

also yields that
\eta^{m}(({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1}-m,\ell_{2}-m\rangle})_{0})\subset N
with
m=\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}+n_{2}-n_{1}-1
.

Since
\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}+n_{2}-n_{1}-1>0
, we suppose that

g=af^{0}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}+b\eta^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}+n_{2}-n_{1}-1}(f^{0}_{\langle-\ell_{2}+n_{1}+1-n_{2},-\ell_{1}+n_{1}+1-n_{2}\rangle})

(3.44)

with
a,b\neq 0
.
We assume
\ell_{1}>0
and
\ell_{2}\leq 0
, and in another case
\ell_{1}\leq 0
and
\ell_{2}>0
is symmetric.
Then

g=\begin{cases}ax_{n_{1}+1}^{\ell_{1}}y_{n_{2}+1}^{-\ell_{2}}+b\eta^{m}(x_{n_{1}+1}^{-\ell_{2}+n_{1}+1-n_{2}}y_{n_{2}+1}^{\ell_{1}-n_{1}-1+n_{2}})&\text{if }-\ell_{2}+n_{1}+1-n_{2}\geq 0,\\
ax_{n_{1}+1}^{\ell_{1}}y_{n_{2}+1}^{-\ell_{2}}+b\eta^{m}(x_{n_{1}}^{\ell_{2}-n_{1}-1+n_{2}}y_{n_{2}+1}^{\ell_{1}-n_{1}-1+n_{2}})&\text{if }-\ell_{2}+n_{1}+1-n_{2}\leq 0.\end{cases}

(3.45)

When
-\ell_{2}+n_{1}+1-n_{2}\leq 0
,

E_{n_{2},n_{1}+1}^{-\ell_{2}+n_{1}+2-n_{2}}(g)=\frac{a\ell_{1}!x_{n_{2}}^{-\ell_{2}+n_{1}+2-n_{2}}x_{n_{1}+1}^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}-n_{1}+n_{2}-2}y_{n_{2}+1}^{-\ell_{2}}}{(\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}-n_{1}+n_{2}-2)!}\in N,

(3.46)

then
l=0
and
V_{0}\subset N
by Lemma
3.3
.
When
-\ell_{2}+n_{1}+1-n_{2}\geq 0
and
n_{2}<n
, we have

\displaystyle E_{n,n_{2}+1}^{1-\ell_{2}}(g)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{b(\ell_{1}-n_{1}-1+n_{2})!}{(\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}-n_{1}+n_{2}-2)!}\eta^{m}\left(x_{n_{1}}^{\ell_{2}-n_{1}-1+n_{2}}y_{n_{2}+1}^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}-n_{1}-2+n_{2}}y_{n}^{1-\ell_{2}}\right)

(3.47)

\displaystyle\in

\displaystyle\eta^{m}(({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1}-m,\ell_{2}-m\rangle})_{0}).

Then
\eta^{m}(({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1}-m,\ell_{2}-m\rangle})_{0})\subset N
by Lemma
3.3
and (
3.21
).
Since we have shown that
U(\mathfrak{g})(V_{0})=M
in
[
34
]
, we have
M\subset N
or
\eta^{m}(({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1}-m,\ell_{2}-m\rangle}))\subset N
.

(2) Case 2:
n_{1}+1<n_{2}=n
and
n_{1}-n+1-\ell_{2}<\ell_{1}\leq n_{1}-n+1
.

When
n_{1}+1<n_{2}=n
and
n_{1}-n+1-\ell_{2}<\ell_{1}\leq n_{1}-n+1
, we have
\ell_{2}\geq 0
(otherwise
M=\{0\}
) and

g=ax_{n_{1}}^{-\ell_{1}}y_{n}^{\ell_{2}}+b\eta^{m}(x_{n_{1}}^{\ell_{2}-n_{1}-1+n}y_{n}^{-\ell_{1}+n_{1}+1-n}).

(3.48)

Then for
n_{1}>1
, we have

\displaystyle E_{n_{1},1}^{1-\ell_{1}}(g)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{b(\ell_{2}-n_{1}-1+n)!}{(\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}-n_{1}+n_{2}-2)!}\eta^{m}\left(x_{n_{1}}^{\ell_{2}-n_{1}-2+n+\ell_{1}}y_{n}^{-\ell_{1}+n_{1}+1-n}x_{1}^{1-\ell_{1}}\right)

(3.49)

\displaystyle\in

\displaystyle\eta^{m}(({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1}-m,\ell_{2}-m\rangle})_{0})

yields that
\eta^{m}(({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1}-m,\ell_{2}-m\rangle})_{0})\subset N
.
Consider
n_{1}=1
, we have

\displaystyle E_{n,2}^{-\ell_{1}+n_{1}+2-n}(g)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{b\ell_{2}!x_{n_{1}}^{-\ell_{1}}y_{n}^{\ell_{2}+\ell_{1}-n_{1}-2+n)}y_{2}^{-\ell_{1}+n_{1}+2-n}}{(\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}-n_{1}+n_{2}-2)!}\in N

(3.50)

yields that
V_{0}\subset N
by Lemma
3.3
.

(3) Case
n_{1}+1=n_{2}
,
\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}>0
and
\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\leq 0
.

According to Lemma 6.6.2 in
[
33
]
, we assume
\mathfrak{g}_{+}(g)=0
with
n_{1}+1=n_{2}
,
\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}>0
and
\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\leq 0
, and then we have

\displaystyle g\in\text{Span}\{f_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle},\eta^{m}(f_{\langle\ell_{1}-m,\ell_{2}-m\rangle}),\eta^{m_{1}+m_{2}}(x_{n_{1}}^{m_{2}}y_{n_{1}+1}^{-m_{1}}),\eta^{m_{1}+m_{2}}(y_{n_{1}+1}^{m_{2}}x_{n_{1}+1}^{-m_{1}})\}.

(3.51)

when
n_{2}<n
, and

\displaystyle g

\displaystyle\in

\displaystyle\text{Span}\{x_{n-1}^{m_{1}}y_{n}^{m_{2}},x_{n}^{m_{1}},\eta^{m_{1}+1}(x_{n-1}^{m_{1}+m_{2}+1}y_{n}^{m_{2}}),

(3.52)

\displaystyle\qquad\eta^{m_{1}+m_{2}+1}(x_{n-1}^{m_{2}+1}y_{n}^{-m_{1}-1})|m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{N}\}.

when
n_{2}=n
.

According to (6.5.60) and (6.5.62) in
[
33
]
, we have
\dim(\{v\in M|\mathfrak{g}_{+}(v)\})\leq 2
, and we can use the similar method as (1) and (2) to prove that the lemma still holds in this case.
∎

Lemma 3.5
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}=sl(n)
and
M={\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
be an infinite-dimensional
\mathfrak{g}
-module, under the conditions stated in Lemma
2.1
. Let
v\in M
and
(U(\mathfrak{g}))(\mathbb{F}v)=M
, the necessary and sufficient condition for
\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)=\mathfrak{p}_{M,\mathbb{F}v}(k)
is
\mathbb{F}v=V_{0}
.

Proof.

We suppose that
\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)=\mathfrak{p}_{M,\mathbb{F}v}(k)
.

According to Lemma
3.2
, we have
I_{(i)}=\left(\operatorname{Ann}_{S(\mathfrak{g})}(\overline{M})\right)^{i}
for
i
large enough.

(1) Consider the case of
n_{1}+1<n_{2}<n
.

(1.a) Suppose that
|J_{1}|\geq 2
and
|J_{3}|\geq 2
.

For
i,i^{\prime}\in J_{1}
with
i\neq i^{\prime}
,
E_{i,i^{\prime}}\in I_{(1)}
. Assume that
E_{i,i^{\prime}}(v)\neq 0
, then
E_{n,i^{\prime}}^{p-1}E_{i,i^{\prime}}\in I_{(p)}=\left(\operatorname{Ann}_{S(\mathfrak{g})}(\overline{M})\right)^{p}
for
p
large enough.

Denote

(\deg_{x_{n}}+\deg_{x_{i^{\prime}}}+\deg_{y_{n}}+\deg_{y_{i^{\prime}}})(v)=d_{0}

(3.53)

then

(\deg_{x_{n}}+\deg_{x_{i^{\prime}}}+\deg_{y_{n}}+\deg_{y_{i^{\prime}}})(E_{n,i^{\prime}}^{p-1}E_{i,i^{\prime}}(v))=d_{0}+2p-1.

(3.54)

On the other hand, for each
f\in(U_{p-1}(\mathfrak{g}))(\mathbb{F}v)
, we have

(\deg_{x_{n}}+\deg_{x_{i^{\prime}}}+\deg_{y_{n}}+\deg_{y_{i^{\prime}}})(f)\leq d_{0}+2p-2.

(3.55)

Hence
E_{i,i^{\prime}}(v)=0
for
i,i^{\prime}\in J_{1}
with
i\neq i^{\prime}
.
Similarly, we have
E_{j,j^{\prime}}(v)=0
for
j,j^{\prime}\in J_{3}
with
j\neq j^{\prime}
.

Assume that
E_{r,r^{\prime}}(v)\neq 0
for
r,r^{\prime}\in J_{2}
with
r\neq r^{\prime}
.
Then
E_{n,1}^{p-1}E_{r,r^{\prime}}(v)\in(U_{p-1}(\mathfrak{g}))(\mathbb{F}v)
for
p
large enough.
Write
v=\sum_{i=1}^{m}v_{i}
, where
v_{i}
are monomials in
\mathscr{A}
for
i\in{1,m}
.
For
d\in\mathbb{N}
, we set

v^{\prime}=\sum_{\{i:{\mathfrak{d}}(v_{i})=\mathfrak{d}(v)\}}v_{i}

(3.56)

Note that
\mathfrak{d}\left(E_{n,1}^{p-1}E_{r,r^{\prime}}(v^{\prime})\right)=2p-2+\mathfrak{d}(v)
, then there exist
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
f_{i}\in Z^{(p-1)}
for
i\in\overline{1,N}
such that

\phi(z_{n,1})^{p-1}E_{r,r^{\prime}}(v^{\prime})=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi(f_{i})v^{\prime}.

(3.57)

Since
\deg_{x_{r^{\prime}}}(E_{r,r^{\prime}}(v^{\prime}))=\deg_{x_{r^{\prime}}}(v^{\prime})-1
, we have

\deg_{x_{r^{\prime}}}\left(\phi(z_{n,1})^{p-1}E_{r,r^{\prime}}(v^{\prime})\right)\neq\deg_{x_{r^{\prime}}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi(f_{i})v^{\prime}\right).

(3.58)

Then
E_{r,r^{\prime}}(v^{\prime})=0
, and so
v^{\prime}=0
by (
3.57
).
Hence
E_{r,r^{\prime}}(v)=0
for
r,r^{\prime}\in J_{2}
with
r\neq r^{\prime}
.

Denote
w_{1}=x_{1}y_{2}-x_{2}y_{1}
,
w_{n}=x_{n}y_{n-1}-x_{n-1}y_{n}
and
\eta_{2}=\sum_{r\in J_{2}}x_{r}y_{r}
.
When
n_{1}+1<n_{2}
,
|J_{1}|\geq 2
and
|J_{3}|\geq 2
,
v
can be written as a rational function
g
in
\{x_{i},y_{j},w_{1},w_{n},\eta_{2},x_{n_{1}+1},y_{n_{2}}|2\leq i\leq n,1\leq j\leq n-1\}
according to Lemma 6.2.3 in
[
33
]
.
Since
E_{n_{2},n_{1}+1}(v)=0
, we have

E_{n_{2},n_{1}+1}(v)=x_{n_{2}}g_{x_{n_{1}+1}}-y_{n_{1}+1}g_{y_{n_{2}}}=0.

(3.59)

Then
g_{x_{n_{1}+1}}=g_{y_{n_{2}}}=0
.

When
|J_{1}|\geq 2
, we have

E_{1,2}(v)=(-x_{2}\partial_{x_{1}}-y_{2}\partial_{y_{1}})(v)=g_{y_{1}}=0.

(3.60)

For
i\in\overline{2,n_{1}}
,

\displaystyle E_{i,1}(v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-x_{1}\partial_{x_{i}}-y_{1}\partial_{y_{i}})(v)

(3.61)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-x_{1}(g_{x_{i}}+x_{2}^{-1}y_{2}g_{y_{i}})-x_{2}^{-1}w_{1}g_{y_{i}}=0.

Since
g_{x_{i}}+x_{2}^{-1}y_{2}g_{y_{i}}
and
x_{2}^{-1}w_{1}g_{y_{i}}
are independent of
x_{1}
, we have

g_{x_{i}}+x_{2}^{-1}y_{2}g_{y_{i}}=0
and
x_{2}^{-1}w_{1}g_{y_{i}}=0
. Thus
g_{x_{i}}=g_{y_{i}}=0
for
i\in J_{1}
when
|J_{1}|\geq 2
.

Similarly, we have
g_{x_{j}}=g_{y_{j}}=0
for
j\in J_{3}
when
|J_{3}|\geq 2
.

Assume that
E_{i_{1},i_{2}}(v)\neq 0
for some
i_{1}\in J_{1}
and
i_{2}\in J_{2}
. When
|J_{1}|\geq 2
, we denote

(\deg_{x_{n}}+\deg_{x_{i^{\prime}}}+\deg_{y_{n}}+\deg_{y_{i^{\prime}}})(v)=d^{\prime}_{0}.

(3.62)

for some
i_{1}\neq i^{\prime}\in J_{1}
.
Then

(\deg_{x_{n}}+\deg_{x_{i^{\prime}}}+\deg_{y_{n}}+\deg_{y_{i^{\prime}}})\left(E_{n,i^{\prime}}^{p-1}E_{i_{1},n_{2}}v\right)=2p+d_{0}^{\prime}-2.

(3.63)

For each
f\in(U_{p-1}(\mathfrak{g}))(\mathbb{F}v)
, we have

(\deg_{x_{n}}+\deg_{x_{i^{\prime}}}+\deg_{y_{n}}+\deg_{y_{i^{\prime}}})(f)\leq d_{0}^{\prime}+2p-2.

(3.64)

Moreover, the equality holds if and only if
(y_{n}y_{i^{\prime}}-x_{n}x_{i^{\prime}})^{p-1}|f
.
The inequality holds with equality if and only if
(y_{n}y_{i^{\prime}}-x_{n}x_{i^{\prime}})^{p-1}|f
.
Hence
E_{n,i^{\prime}}^{p-1}E_{i_{1},i_{2}}v\in(U_{p-1}(\mathfrak{g}))(\mathbb{F}v)
yields that
E_{i_{1},i_{2}}v\in\mathbb{F}v
which leads to a contradiction. Therefore
E_{i_{1},i_{2}}(v)=0
for
i_{1}\in J_{1}
and
i_{2}\in J_{2}
.

Then we have

\displaystyle(\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{n_{2}}}-y_{n_{2}}\partial_{y_{1}})(v)=g_{\eta_{2},w_{1}}y_{2}y_{n_{2}}-y_{n_{2}}x_{2}g_{w_{1}}=0.

(3.65)

Then there exists a rational function
g^{\prime}
such that
\frac{\partial(g^{\prime})}{\partial\eta_{2}}=0
and

g_{w_{1}}=e^{\frac{x_{2}}{y_{2}}\eta_{2}}g^{\prime}

(3.66)

which is not a rational function when
g^{\prime}\neq 0
.
Hence
g_{w_{1}}=0
.
Similarly,
E_{i_{3},i_{2}}(v)=0
for
i_{3}\in J_{3}
,
i_{2}\in J_{2}
and
g_{w_{n}}=0
when
|J_{3}|\geq 2
. Hence
v
only depends on
\eta_{2}
.

Since
\eta_{2}=\eta(1)
,
\mathbb{F}[\eta_{2}]=\text{Span}\{\eta^{i}(1)|i\in\mathbb{N}\}
.
Note that
1\in{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle 0,0\rangle}
and
\eta^{i}(1)\in{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle i,i\rangle}
, we have
v=\eta^{i_{0}}(1)
for some
i_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then
i_{0}=m
and
\ell_{1}-m=\ell_{2}-m=0
with
m=\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}+n_{2}-n_{1}-1
by (
3.28
). Hence
i_{0}=n_{1}+1-n_{2}\leq 0
yields that
i_{0}=0
.
Therefore, we have
\ell_{1}=\ell_{2}=0
and
v\in\mathbb{F}
.

(1.b) Suppose that
|J_{1}|=1
and
|J_{3}|\geq 2
.

In this case, we also have
E_{i,i^{\prime}}(v)=0
with
i,i^{\prime}\in J_{j}
and
i^{\prime}\neq i
for
j=2,3
. Then
v
can be written as a rational function
g
in
\{x_{1},y_{1},w_{n},\eta_{2}\}
.

Assume that
E_{1,i_{2}}(v)\neq 0
for some
i_{2}\in J_{2}
. Suppose that
\partial_{y_{1}}(v)=0
, we denote

(\deg_{y_{n}}+\deg_{y_{1}})(v)=\deg_{y_{n}}(v)=d_{0}.

(3.67)

Then

(\deg_{y_{n}}+\deg_{y_{1}})\left(E_{n,1}^{p-1}E_{1,i_{2}}(v)\right)=2p-2+d_{0}.

(3.68)

For each
f\in(U_{p-1}(\mathfrak{g}))(\mathbb{F}v)
, we have

(\deg_{y_{n}}+\deg_{y_{1}})(f)\leq d_{0}+2p-2

(3.69)

and
(\deg_{y_{n}}+\deg_{y_{1}})(f)\leq d_{0}+2p-2
if and only if
f\in\mathbb{F}E_{n,1}^{p-1}(v)
.
Hence
E_{n,1}^{p-1}E_{1,i_{2}}(v)\in\mathbb{F}E_{n,1}^{p-1}(v)
yields that
E_{1,i_{2}}(v)=0
.
Now we suppose that
\partial_{y_{1}}(v)\neq 0
, and denote

(\deg_{x_{1}}+\deg_{x_{n}})(v)=(\deg_{x_{1}}+\deg_{x_{n}})(E_{1,i_{2}}(v))=d^{\prime}_{0}.

(3.70)

Then

(\deg_{x_{1}}+\deg_{x_{n}})\left(E_{n,1}^{p-1}E_{1,i_{2}}(v)\right)=2p-2+d_{0}^{\prime},

(3.71)

which can also yields that
E_{1,i_{2}}(v)=0
.
Note that we also have
E_{i_{3},i_{2}}(v)=0
and
g_{w_{n}}=0
since
|J_{2}|\geq 2
. Then
v
only depends on
\{x_{1},y_{1},\eta_{2}\}
.
Hence
E_{1,j}(v)=0
for
j\in J_{3}
.
Therefore,
\mathbb{F}v
is a
U(\mathfrak{a})
-invariant subspaces. Note that
\mathfrak{g}_{+}(v)=0
yields that
v\in V_{0}
by Lemma 6.4.2 in
[
33
]
. So we have
V_{0}\subset\mathbb{F}v
by Lemma
3.3
and the proof of Lemma
3.4
. Hence
\dim V_{0}=1
yields
\ell_{1}\leq 0
,
\ell_{2}=0
and
\mathbb{F}v=\mathbb{F}x_{1}^{-\ell_{1}}
.

(1.c) The case of
|J_{3}|=1
and
|J_{1}|\geq 2
is symmetrical to the case (1.b), and we have
\ell_{1}=0
,
\ell_{2}\leq 0
and
\mathbb{F}v=\mathbb{F}y_{n}^{-\ell_{2}}
.

(1.d) When
|J_{1}|=|J_{3}|=1
, we have
n_{1}=1
and
n_{2}=n-1
.

Since (
3.67
)-(
3.71
) still holds, we also have
E_{1,i}(v)=E_{n,i}(v)=0
for
i\in J_{2}
.
Assume that
E_{n,1}(v)\neq 0
, then
\partial_{y_{1}}\partial_{y_{n}}(v)\neq 0
or
\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{n}}(v)\neq 0
.
When
\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{n}}(v)\neq 0
, we denote

(\deg_{y_{n}}+\deg_{y_{1}})(v)=d_{0}.

(3.72)

Then

(\deg_{y_{n}}+\deg_{y_{1}})\left(E_{n,1}^{p-1}E_{1,n}(v)\right)=2p-2+d_{0}.

(3.73)

For each
f\in(U_{p-1}(\mathfrak{g}))(\mathbb{F}v)
, we have

(\deg_{y_{n}}+\deg_{y_{1}})(f)\leq d_{0}+2p-2,

(3.74)

which yields
E_{n,1}(v)=0
.
Similarly,
E_{n,1}(v)=0
also holds when
\partial_{x_{1}}\partial_{x_{n}}(v)\neq 0
.
Then
\mathfrak{g}_{+}(v)=0
and
\mathbb{F}v
is a
U(\mathfrak{a})
-invariant subspaces.
According to Lemma 6.4.2 in
[
33
]
and Lemma
3.3
, we have
\dim V_{0}=1
which yields that
\ell_{1}\leq 0
,
\ell_{2}\leq 0
and
\mathbb{F}v=\mathbb{F}x_{1}^{-\ell_{1}}y_{n}^{-\ell_{2}}
.

(2) Consider the case of
n_{1}+1<n_{2}=n
.

(2.a) Suppose that
|J_{1}|\geq 2
, we also have
E_{i,i^{\prime}}(v)=0
for
i,i^{\prime}\in J_{j}
,
i^{\prime}\neq i
and
j=1,2
.

Assume that
E_{i_{1},i_{2}}(v)=0
for some
i_{1}\in J_{1}
and
i_{2}\in J_{2}
.
For some
i_{1}^{\prime}\in J_{1}
and
i_{2}^{\prime}\in J_{2}
with
i_{1}^{\prime}\neq i_{1}
and
i_{2}^{\prime}\neq i_{2}
, we denote

(\deg_{x_{i_{2}^{\prime}}}+\deg_{x_{i_{1}^{\prime}}})(v)=d_{0}

(3.75)

then

(\deg_{x_{i_{2}^{\prime}}}+\deg_{x_{i_{1}^{\prime}}})(E_{i_{2}^{\prime},i_{1}^{\prime}}^{p-1}E_{i_{1},i_{2}}(v))=d_{0}+2p-2.

(3.76)

On the other hand, for each
f\in(U_{p-1}(\mathfrak{g}))(\mathbb{F}v)
, we have

(\deg_{x_{i_{2}^{\prime}}}+\deg_{x_{i_{1}^{\prime}}})(f)\leq d_{0}+2p-2.

(3.77)

and
(\deg_{x_{i_{2}^{\prime}}}+\deg_{x_{i_{1}^{\prime}}})(f)\leq d_{0}+2p-2
if and only if
f\in\mathbb{F}(E_{i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}^{\prime}}^{p-1}(v))
.
Hence
E_{i_{1},i_{2}}(v)=0
for
i_{1}\in J_{1}
and
i_{2}\in J_{2}
.

(2.b) Suppose that
|J_{1}|=1
and
E_{1,i}(v)\neq 0
for some
i\in J_{2}
.

When
\partial_{y_{1}}(v)\neq 0
,
\deg_{x_{1}}(v)=\deg_{x_{1}}(E_{1,i}(v))
.
For
i^{\prime}\in J_{2}
with
i^{\prime}\neq i
, we have

\deg_{x_{1}}(E_{i^{\prime},1}^{p-1}E_{1,i}(v))=p-1+\deg_{x_{1}}(v),\,\,\deg_{x_{i^{\prime}}}(E_{i^{\prime},1}^{p-1}E_{1,i}(v))=p-1+\deg_{x_{3}}(v),\;

(3.78)

yields that
E_{1,i}(v)=0
.
When
\partial_{y_{1}}(v)=0
, we have

\deg_{y_{1}}(E_{i^{\prime},1}^{p-1}E_{1,i}(v))=p-1,\,\,\deg_{x_{i^{\prime}}}(E_{i^{\prime},1}^{p-1}E_{1,i}(v))=p-1+\deg_{x_{3}}(v),

(3.79)

which also yields that
E_{1,i}(v)=0
.
Hence
\mathbb{F}v
is a
U(\mathfrak{a})
-invariant subspaces and
\mathfrak{g}_{+}(v)=0
for the cases (2.a) and (2.b).

When
\ell_{1},\ell_{2}>0
, we have
v\in\mathbb{F}x_{n_{1}+1}^{\ell_{1}}y_{n_{2}}^{\ell_{2}}
. However,

E_{n_{2},n_{1}+1}(x_{n_{1}+1}^{\ell_{1}}y_{n_{2}}^{\ell_{2}})=\ell_{1}x_{n_{2}}x_{n_{1}+1}^{\ell_{1}-1}y_{n_{2}}^{\ell_{2}}-\ell_{2}y_{n_{1}+1}x_{n_{1}+1}^{\ell_{1}}y_{n_{2}}^{\ell_{2}-1}\neq 0.

(3.80)

Hence
\ell_{1}\leq 0
or
\ell_{2}\leq 0
, and we have
V_{0}\subset\mathbb{F}v
by Lemma
3.3
.
Then
n_{1}=1
,
\ell_{1}\leq 0
,
\ell_{2}=0
and
\mathbb{F}v=\mathbb{F}x_{1}^{-\ell_{1}}
.

(3) The case of
n_{1}+1=n_{2}
.

(3.a) Suppose that
|J_{1}|\geq 2
and
|J_{3}|\geq 2
.

Compare with the case (1.a), the main difference lies in
\eta_{2}=x_{n_{2}}y_{n_{2}}
and we do not have the expression (
3.59
). Similar to (1,a), we know that
v
can be written as a rational function
g
in
\{x_{n_{2}},y_{n_{2}}\}
.
Since
\ell_{1}\leq 0
or
\ell_{2}\leq 0
,
\deg_{x_{n_{2}}}(v)
and
\deg_{y_{n_{2}}}(v)
cannot both be positive, so we have
v\in\mathbb{F}[x_{n_{2}}]
or
v\in\mathbb{F}[y_{n_{2}}]
.
There are only three possible cases: (3.a.1)
\mathbb{F}v=\mathbb{F}x_{n_{2}}^{\ell_{1}}
with
\ell_{1}>0
,
\ell_{2}=0
; (3.a.2)
\mathbb{F}v=\mathbb{F}y_{n_{2}}^{\ell_{2}}
with
\ell_{2}>0
,
\ell_{1}=0
; (3.a.3)
v\in\mathbb{F}
with
\ell_{1}=\ell_{2}=0
.

(3.b) Suppose that
|J_{1}|=1
and
|J_{3}|\geq 2
, then
n_{1}=1
and
n_{2}=2
.

Similar to (1.b), (
3.67
)-(
3.71
) still holds. Hence
E_{1,2}(v)=0
and
v
can be written as a rational function
g
in
\{x_{1},y_{1},x_{2},y_{2}\}
.
Then
\mathfrak{g}_{+}(v)=0
and
\mathbb{F}v
is a
U(\mathfrak{a})
-invariant subspaces.
According to the Theorem 6.5.1 in
[
33
]
and Lemma
3.3
and the proof of Lemma
3.4
,
V_{0}=\mathbb{F}v
and
\dim(V_{0})=1
. There are only two possible cases: (3.b.1)
\mathbb{F}v=\mathbb{F}x_{1}^{-\ell_{1}}y_{2}^{\ell_{2}}
with
\ell_{1}\leq 0
,
\ell_{2}\geq 0
; (3.b.2)
\mathbb{F}v=\mathbb{F}x_{2}^{\ell_{1}}
and
\ell_{1}>0
,
\ell_{2}=0
.

(3.c) The case of
|J_{3}|=1
and
|J_{1}|\geq 2
is symmetrical to the case (3.b), and we have two possible cases: (3.c.1)
\mathbb{F}v=\mathbb{F}x_{n-1}^{\ell_{1}}y_{n}^{-\ell_{2}}
with
\ell_{1}\geq 0
,
\ell_{2}\leq 0
; (3.c.2)
\mathbb{F}v=\mathbb{F}y_{n-1}^{\ell_{2}}
and
\ell_{1}=0
,
\ell_{2}>0
.

(3.d) When
|J_{1}|=|J_{2}|=1
, we have
n_{1}=1
,
n_{2}=2
and
n=3
.

Note that

V_{0}=\begin{cases}\mathbb{F}x_{2}^{\ell_{1}}y_{3}^{-\ell_{2}}&\text{if }\ell_{1}\geq 0,\ell_{2}\leq 0;\\
\mathbb{F}x_{1}^{-\ell_{1}}y_{2}^{\ell_{2}}&\text{if }\ell_{1}\leq 0,\ell_{2}\geq 0;\\
\mathbb{F}x_{1}^{-\ell_{1}}y_{3}^{-\ell_{2}}&\text{if }\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\leq 0.\end{cases}

(3.81)

Similar to (1.d), we also have
\mathfrak{g}_{+}(v)=0
and
V_{0}=\mathbb{F}v
.

(3.e) Suppose that
2<n_{1}+1=n_{2}=n
.

For
i,i^{\prime}\in J_{1}
with
i\neq i
, we have
E_{n,i^{\prime}}^{p-1}E_{i,}(v)\in U_{p-1}(v)
for
p
large enough, we have
E_{i,n}(v)=0
by considering
(\deg_{x_{i^{\prime}}}+\deg_{x_{n}})(E_{n,i^{\prime}}^{p-1}E_{i,}(v))
.
Hence
v
can be written as a rational function
g
in
\{x_{n},y_{n}\}
. When
\ell_{1},\ell_{2}>0
,
v\in\mathbb{F}[x_{n_{2}},y_{n_{2}}]
is not possible. Thus, we only need to consider the cases where
\ell_{1}\leq 0
or
\ell_{2}\leq 0
. There are only two possible cases: (3.e.1)
\mathbb{F}v=\mathbb{F}x_{n}^{\ell_{1}}
with
\ell_{1}\geq 0
,
\ell_{2}=0
; (3.e.2)
\mathbb{F}v=\mathbb{F}y_{n}^{\ell_{2}}
with
\ell_{1}=0
,
\ell_{2}\geq 0
.

(3.f) Suppose that
n_{1}=1
,
n_{2}=n=2
.

Since
E_{2,1}^{p-1}E_{1,2}(v)\in U_{p-1}(v)
for
p
large enough, we have
E_{1,2}(v)=0
by considering
(\deg_{x_{1}}+\deg_{x_{2}})(E_{2,1}^{p-1}E_{1,2}(v))
.
Thus we have

\mathbb{F}v=\begin{cases}\mathbb{F}x_{1}^{-\ell_{1}}y_{2}^{\ell_{2}}&\text{if }\ell_{1}\leq 0,\ell_{2}\geq 0;\\
\mathbb{F}x_{1}^{\ell_{1}}&\text{if }\ell_{1}\geq 0,\ell_{2}=0;\\
\mathbb{F}\eta^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}}(x_{1}^{\ell_{2}}y_{2}^{-\ell_{1}})&\text{if }\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\geq 0.\end{cases}

(3.82)

Note that

\displaystyle\eta^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}}(x_{n-1}^{\ell_{2}}y_{n}^{-\ell_{1}})

(3.83)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}y_{i}\partial_{x_{i}}+x_{n}y_{n}\right)^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}}(x_{n-1}^{\ell_{2}}y_{n}^{-\ell_{1}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}y_{i}\partial_{x_{i}}\right)^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}-j}(x_{n}y_{n})^{j}(x_{n-1}^{\ell_{2}}y_{n}^{-\ell_{1}})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left(y_{n-1}\partial_{x_{n-1}}\right)^{\ell_{2}}(x_{n}y_{n})^{\ell_{1}}(x_{n-1}^{\ell_{2}}y_{n}^{-\ell_{1}})\quad(\mbox{mod }x_{n}y_{n})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\ell_{2}!x_{n}^{\ell_{1}}y_{n-1}^{\ell_{2}}\quad(\mbox{mod }x_{n}y_{n}),

when
n_{1}+1=n_{2}=n
and
\ell_{1},\ell_{2}>0
, then
we have

\mathbb{F}\eta^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}}(x_{n-1}^{\ell_{2}}y_{n}^{-\ell_{1}})=\mathbb{F}T\left(x_{n}^{\ell_{1}}y_{n-1}^{\ell_{2}}\right)\subset V_{0}.

(3.84)

Therefore,
\dim(V_{0})=1
when
n_{1}+1=n_{2}=n
and
\ell_{1},\ell_{2}>0
if and only if
n_{1}=1
and
V_{0}=\mathbb{F}\eta^{\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}}(x_{1}^{\ell_{2}}y_{2}^{-\ell_{1}})
.

(4) When
n_{1}=n_{2}
.

Using the above method along with Lemma 6.6.2 and the Theorem 6.64 in
[
33
]
, we can also conclude that: (4.1)
v\in\mathbb{F}
and
\ell_{1}=\ell_{2}=0
; (4.2)
\mathbb{F}v=\mathbb{F}(x_{1}y_{2}-x_{2}y_{1})^{\ell_{2}}
and
\ell_{2}=-\ell_{1}>0
when
n_{1}=2<n
; (4.3)
\mathbb{F}v=\mathbb{F}(x_{n}y_{n-1}-x_{n-1}y_{n})^{\ell_{1}}
and
\ell_{1}=-\ell_{2}>0
when
n_{1}=n-2
; (4.4)
\mathbb{F}v=\mathbb{F}x_{1}^{-\ell_{1}}y_{2}^{-\ell_{2}}
and
\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\leq 0
when
n_{1}=1
and
n=2
.

In conclusion,
\mathbb{F}v=V_{0}
if
I_{(i)}=\left(\operatorname{Ann}_{S(\mathfrak{g})}(\overline{M})\right)^{i}
.
Furthermore, it is easy to check that
I_{(i)}=\left(\operatorname{Ann}_{S(\mathfrak{g})}(\overline{M})\right)^{i}
when
\mathbb{F}v=V_{0}
from Lemma 4.1, Lemma 4.2, Lemma 4.3 and Lemma 4.5 in
[
34
]
.

∎

Now we have our main theorem in this section:

Theorem 3.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}=sl(n)
and
M={\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
be an infinite-dimensional
\mathfrak{g}
-module under the conditions stated in Lemma
2.1
.
M_{0}
is a subspace of
M
satisfying
(U(\mathfrak{g}))(M_{0})=M
, then
\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)\leq\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(k)
. The necessary and sufficient condition for
\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)=\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(k)
is
M_{0}=V_{0}
and
\dim(V_{0})=1
.
Specifically,
M_{0}
corresponds to one of the following cases:

(1)
M_{0}=\mathbb{F}
when
\ell_{1}=\ell_{2}=0
;

(2)
M_{0}=\mathbb{F}x_{1}^{-\ell_{1}}
when
n_{1}=1
,
\ell_{1}\leq 0
and
\ell_{2}=0
;

(3)
M_{0}=\mathbb{F}y_{n}^{-\ell_{2}}
when
n_{2}=n-1
,
\ell_{1}=0
and
\ell_{2}\leq 0
;

(4)
M_{0}=\mathbb{F}x_{1}^{-\ell_{1}}y_{n}^{-\ell_{2}}
when
n_{1}=1
,
n_{2}=n-1
,
\ell_{1}\leq 0
and
\ell_{2}\leq 0
;

(5)
M_{0}=\mathbb{F}y_{n}^{\ell_{2}}
when
n_{2}=n
,
\ell_{1}=0
and
\ell_{2}\geq 0
;

(6)
M_{0}=\mathbb{F}x_{n_{2}}^{\ell_{1}}
when
n_{1}+1=n_{2}
,
\ell_{1}\geq 0
and
\ell_{2}=0
;

(7)
M_{0}=\mathbb{F}y_{n_{2}}^{\ell_{2}}
when
n_{1}+1=n_{2}
,
\ell_{1}=0
and
\ell_{2}\geq 0
;

(8)
M_{0}=\mathbb{F}x_{1}^{-\ell_{1}}y_{n_{2}}^{\ell_{2}}
when
n_{1}+1=n_{2}=2
,
\ell_{1}\leq 0
and
\ell_{2}\geq 0
;

(9)
M_{0}=\mathbb{F}x_{n_{2}}^{\ell_{1}}y_{n}^{-\ell_{2}}
when
n_{1}+1=n_{2}=n-1
,
\ell_{1}\geq 0
and
\ell_{2}\leq 0
;

(10)
M_{0}=\mathbb{F}(x_{1}y_{2}-x_{2}y_{1})^{\ell_{2}}
when
n_{1}=n_{2}=2<n
,
\ell_{2}=-\ell_{1}>0
;

(11)
M_{0}=\mathbb{F}(x_{n}y_{n-1}-x_{n-1}y_{n})^{\ell_{1}}
when
n_{1}=n_{2}=n-2
,
\ell_{1}=-\ell_{2}>0
;

(12)
M_{0}=\mathbb{F}T\left(x_{2}^{\ell_{1}}y_{1}^{\ell_{2}}\right)
when
n_{1}=1
,
n_{2}=n=2
and
\ell_{1},\ell_{2}>0
.

Proof.

For
u\in M_{0}
, we set
M_{\{u\}}=U(\mathfrak{g})(\mathbb{F}u)
, which is a
U(\mathfrak{g})
-submodule of
M
.
According to Lemma
3.4
,
M\subset M_{\{u\}}
or
\eta^{m}({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1}-m,\ell_{2}-m\rangle})\subset M_{\{u\}}
with
m=\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}+n_{2}-n_{1}-1
.

Assume that there exists
u_{0}\in M_{0}
such that
M_{\{u_{0}\}}=M
,
then we have

\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(k)\geq\mathfrak{p}_{M_{\{u_{0}\}},\mathbb{F}u_{0}}(k)\geq\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)

(3.85)

according to Lemma
3.1
and Lemma
3.2
.

Suppose that
\eta^{m}({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1}-m,\ell_{2}-m\rangle})\subset M_{\{u_{0}\}}
with
u_{0}\in M_{0}
,
we define a linear map
\zeta
from
{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1}-m,\ell_{2}-m\rangle}
to
M_{\{u_{0}\}}
by
\zeta(f)=\eta^{m}(f)
.
Thus,
\zeta
is injective.
There exists
u_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1}-m,\ell_{2}-m\rangle}
such that
f_{0}(\lambda u_{0})=\eta^{m}(u_{0}^{\prime})
for some
f_{0}\in U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})
and
\lambda\in\mathbb{F}
, and we have
\zeta\left(U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})(\mathbb{F}u_{0}^{\prime})\right)\subset U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})(\mathbb{F}f_{0}(\lambda u_{0}))
.
Hence

\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(k)\geq\mathfrak{p}_{M_{\{u_{0}\}},\mathbb{F}u_{0}}(k)\geq\mathfrak{p}_{\eta^{m}({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1}-m,\ell_{2}-m\rangle}),\mathbb{F}f_{0}(\lambda u_{0})}(k)\geq\mathfrak{p}_{{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1}-m,\ell_{2}-m\rangle},\mathbb{F}u_{0}^{\prime}}(k)

(3.86)

by Lemma
3.1
.
According to the main theorem in
[
34
]
, we have
\mathscr{V}({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1}-m,\ell_{2}-m\rangle})=\mathscr{V}({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle})
.
Therefore,

\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(k)\geq\mathfrak{p}_{{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1}-m,\ell_{2}-m\rangle}}(k)=\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k).

(3.87)

Now we suppose that
\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)=\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(k)
, then all the inequalities mentioned above must hold as equalities.
When
M_{\{u_{0}\}}\neq M
, the linear map from
M_{\{u_{0}\}}
to
M
satisfying
U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})(\mathbb{F}u_{0})\mapsto U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})(\mathbb{F}u_{0})\subsetneq U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})M_{0}
for sufficiently large
k
, thus

\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(k)>\mathfrak{p}_{M_{\{u_{0}\}},\mathbb{F}u_{0}}(k).

(3.88)

Then
\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(k)>\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)
holds.

Hence we suppose that
M_{\{u_{0}\}}=M
, using the conclusion in Lemma
3.5
, we have
\mathbb{F}u_{0}=V_{0}
and
\dim(V_{0})=1
.
Moreover,
\dim(U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})(M_{0}))=\dim(U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})(\mathbb{F}u_{0}))
for sufficiently large
k
.

Assume that there exists
v\in M_{0}\setminus\mathbb{F}u_{0}
, then
U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})(\mathbb{F}v)\subset U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})(\mathbb{F}u_{0})
for sufficiently large
k
.
Denote

m_{0}=\min\{k\in\mathbb{F}|v\in U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})(\mathbb{F}u_{0})\}>0

(3.89)

and
v=f(\lambda^{\prime}u_{0})
, where
f\in U_{m_{0}}(\mathfrak{g})
and
\lambda^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}
.
Note that
I=I(\mathscr{V}(M_{\{u_{0}\}}))=\operatorname{Ann}_{S(\mathfrak{g})}(\overline{M_{\{u_{0}\}}})
is a prime ideal in this case, since
\mathbb{F}u_{0}=V_{0}
.
We denote by
\overline{g}\in S^{p}(\mathfrak{g})
the image of
g
under the natural map from
U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})
to
U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})/U_{k-1}(\mathfrak{g})
.
For sufficiently large
k
and
g\in U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})
such that
\overline{g}\not\in I
, we have

(gf)(\lambda^{\prime}u_{0})=g(v)\in U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})(\mathbb{F}v)\subset U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})(\mathbb{F}u_{0})\subset U_{k+m_{0}-1}(\mathfrak{g})(\mathbb{F}u_{0}).

(3.90)

Then
\overline{gf}\in I
yields that
\overline{f}\in I
.
Thus
v=f(\lambda^{\prime}u_{0})\in U_{m_{0}-1}(\mathbb{F}u_{0})
, which contradicts (
3.89
).
Therefore,
M_{0}=\mathbb{F}u_{0}=V_{0}
.

∎

This completes the proof of Theorem 2 that we stated in the introduction.

4
Leading Coefficient of
\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}

First, we will show that the leading coefficient of
\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}
is independent of the choice of
M_{0}
.
For
\mathfrak{g}=sl(n)
,
M=\mathscr{H}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
, we compute
lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}})
and find the relationship between
lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}})
and
lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M})
.
For the case where
n_{1}<n_{2}<n
and
\ell_{1}\leq 0
or
\ell_{2}\leq 0
, we identify a subspace that is isomorphic to
\overline{M_{k}}
and find a corresponding basis. To construct this basis, we introduce the concept of “3-chain”, which is a natural extension of the 3-chain notion introduced in
[
34
]
.
Moreover, we define a map from the basis of
\overline{M_{k}}
to the basis of
\overline{(\mathscr{H}_{\langle 0,0\rangle})_{k}}

to compute the leading coefficient.
Another case which is relatively more complicated is
n_{1}=n_{2}<n
and
\ell_{1}\ell_{2}<0
. The main difficulty lying in the structure of
M_{0}
. We introduce the concepts of
\omega
-2-chain and
\omega
-3-chain, from which we construct a basis and give a formula of
\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}}
.

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
M_{0},M_{0}^{\prime}
be finite-dimensional subspaces of
M
satisfying
(U(\mathfrak{g}))(M_{0})=(U(\mathfrak{g}))(M_{0}^{\prime})=M
, then we have

lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}})=lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}^{\prime}}).

(4.1)

Proof.

Note that the filtration generated by
M_{0}
is equivalent to the filtration generated by
M_{0}^{\prime}
, that is
M_{k}\subset M_{k+d}^{\prime}
and
M_{k}^{\prime}\subset M_{k+d}
for some
d\in\mathbb{N}
and each
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
According to (
3.1
), we have

\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}}(k)\leq\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{d}^{\prime}}(k),\,\,\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}^{\prime}}(k)\leq p_{M,M_{d}}(k)

(4.2)

for
k
large enough.
Then
lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}})\leq lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{d}^{\prime}})\leq lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{2d}})
and (
4.1
) holds.

∎

Hence we can denote
lc(M)=lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}})
.

In this section, we will compute
lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}})
when
\mathfrak{g}=sl(n)
and
M={\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
.
Note that
\deg(\mathfrak{p}_{M,M_{0}})=\deg(\mathfrak{p}_{M})
and
\deg(\mathscr{V}(M))=lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M})\cdot(\deg(\mathfrak{p}_{M}))!
, but we will see that
lc(M)=lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M})
and
lc(M)\cdot(\deg(\mathfrak{p}_{M}))!=\deg(\mathscr{V}(M))
does not always hold true.

In the rest of this section, we assume that
\mathfrak{g}=sl(n)
,
M=\mathscr{H}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
under the conditions stated in Lemma
2.1
,
M_{0}=V_{0}
(cf. (
2.18
)), and
M_{k}=U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})V_{0}
for
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Then
lc(M)=lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}})
by the proposition
4.1
.

4.1
Case:
n_{1}<n_{2}=n

Since
M=0
if
\ell_{2}>0
and
n_{1}<n_{2}=n
, we assume that
\ell_{2}\geq 0
in this subsection.
Recall that when
\ell_{1},\ell_{2}>0
and
n_{1}<n_{2}=n
, we have

V_{0}=\text{Span}\left\{TN\begin{pmatrix}0&\ell_{1}&0\\
k_{21}&k_{22}&0\end{pmatrix}\Big|k_{21}+k_{22}=\ell_{2}\right\}.

(4.3)

and

\displaystyle M_{k}=U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})(V_{0})=\text{Span}\left\{TN\begin{pmatrix}t&\ell_{1}+t&0\\
k_{21}&k_{22}&0\end{pmatrix}\Big|0\leq t\leq k,k_{21}+k_{22}=\ell_{2}\right\}

(4.4)

according to Lemma 4.4 in
[
34
]
.

When
n_{1}+1<n_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}}(k)

(4.5)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\left\{N\begin{pmatrix}k&\ell_{1}+k&0\\
k_{21}&k_{22}&0\end{pmatrix}\Big|k_{21}+k_{22}=\ell_{2}\right\}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\binom{n+\ell_{2}-1}{\ell_{2}}\binom{n_{1}+k-1}{k}\binom{n-n_{1}-1+\ell_{1}+k-1}{\ell_{1}+k}

\displaystyle+\binom{n-1+\ell_{2}-1}{\ell_{2}}\binom{n_{1}+k-1}{k}\binom{n-n_{1}+\ell_{1}+k-1}{\ell_{1}+k}

\displaystyle-\binom{n-1+\ell_{2}-1}{\ell_{2}}\binom{n_{1}+k-1}{k}\binom{n-n_{1}-1+\ell_{1}+k-1}{\ell_{1}+k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{\ell_{1}}{n-1}+\frac{\ell_{1}+k}{n-n_{1}-1}\right)\binom{n+\ell_{2}-2}{\ell_{2}}\binom{n_{1}+k-1}{k}\binom{n-n_{1}+\ell_{1}+k-2}{\ell_{1}+k}\qquad\quad

Then

\displaystyle lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}})=\frac{1}{(n_{1}-1)!(n-n_{1}-1)!}\binom{n+\ell_{2}-2}{\ell_{2}}.

(4.6)

When
n_{1}+1=n_{2}=n
, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}}(k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\left\{x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}\in\mathscr{A}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}\Big|\sum_{t=1}^{n-1}\alpha_{t}=k,\sum_{s=1}^{n-1}\beta_{s}=\ell_{2}\right\}\right|

(4.7)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\binom{n+k-2}{k}\binom{n+\ell_{2}-2}{\ell_{2}},

and

lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M})=\frac{1}{(n-2)!}\binom{n+\ell_{2}-2}{\ell_{2}}.

(4.8)

Note that
\mathscr{V}(M)\cong\mathscr{V}_{2}(\overline{n_{1}+1,n},\overline{1,n_{1}})
, then

lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M})=\frac{\deg(\mathscr{V}(M))}{(\deg(\mathfrak{p}_{M}))!}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{(n_{1}-1)!(n-n_{1}-1)!}&\text{if }n_{1}>1\text{ and }n_{1}+1<n;\\
\frac{1}{(n-2)!}&\text{if }n_{1}=1\text{ or }n_{1}+1=n.\end{cases}

(4.9)

by (
2.26
).

Similarly, when
n_{1}<n_{2}=n_{2}
,
\ell_{1}\leq 0
and
\ell_{2}\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle M_{k}=U_{k}(\mathfrak{g})(V_{0})=\text{Span}\left\{TN\begin{pmatrix}-\ell_{1}+t&t&0\\
k_{21}&k_{22}&0\end{pmatrix}\Big|0\leq t\leq k,k_{21}+k_{22}=\ell_{2}\right\}

(4.10)

and

\displaystyle lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}})=\frac{1}{(n_{1}-1)!(n-n_{1}-1)!}\binom{n+\ell_{2}-2}{\ell_{2}}.

(4.11)

Therefore, we draw the following conclusion:

Lemma 4.1
.

For
\mathfrak{g}=sl(n)
and infinite-dimensional
\mathfrak{g}
-module
M={\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
, if
n_{1}<n_{2}=n
, we have

\frac{lc(M)}{lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M})}=\binom{n+\ell_{2}-2}{\ell_{2}}.

(4.12)

4.2
Case:
n_{1}=n_{2}<n
and
\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\leq 0

Recall that

V_{0}=\mbox{Span}\Big\{\prod_{i\in J_{1},j\in J_{3}}x_{i}^{\alpha_{i}}y_{j}^{\beta_{j}}|\sum_{i\in J_{1}}\alpha_{i}=-\ell_{1},\sum_{j\in J_{3}}\beta_{j}=-\ell_{2}\Big\},

(4.13)

and according to (4.157) in
[
34
]
, we have

M_{k}=\mbox{Span}\Big\{\prod_{i\in J_{1},j\in J_{3}}x_{i}^{\alpha_{i}}y_{j}^{\beta_{j}}\phi(h)\mid\sum_{i\in J_{1}}\alpha_{i}=-\ell_{1},\sum_{j\in J_{3}}\beta_{j}=-\ell_{2},h\in Z^{(r)},r\in\overline{0,k}\Big\}.

(4.14)

Denote the set

\displaystyle S_{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\{f=\prod_{i\in J_{1},j\in J_{3}}x_{i}^{\alpha_{i}}y_{j}^{\beta_{j}}h\big|h\in Z^{(k)},\sum_{i\in J_{1}}\alpha_{i}=-\ell_{1},\sum_{j\in J_{3}}\beta_{j}=-\ell_{2},

(4.15)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\text{ no 3-chains in }\mathscr{I}(f)\Big\}.

Then
\phi(S_{k})
is linearly independent and
T(\phi(S_{k}))\oplus M_{k-1}=M_{k}
by Lemma 3.3 in
[
34
]
.

Suppose that
n_{1}>1
and
n-n_{1}>1
.
For
f\in S_{k}
, we denote
i_{1}(f)=\max\{i\in J_{1}|\alpha_{i}>0\})
and
j_{1}(f)=\min(\{j\in J_{3}|\beta_{j}>0\}
.
For
d,i_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
, we denote

\displaystyle G_{0}(d,i_{1},\ell_{1},\ell_{2})

(4.16)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &\binom{d-\ell_{2}-2}{-\ell_{2}-1}\binom{i_{1}-\ell_{1}-2}{-\ell_{1}-1},&\text{ if }&\ell_{1},\ell_{2}<0;\\
&\binom{d-\ell_{2}-2}{-\ell_{2}-1},&\text{ if }&\ell_{1}=0,\ell_{2}<0;\\
&\binom{i_{1}-\ell_{1}-2}{-\ell_{1}-1},&\text{ if }&\ell_{1}<0,\ell_{2}=0;\\
&1,&\text{ if }&\ell_{1}=\ell_{2}=0.\end{aligned}\right.

Then for
i_{1}\in J_{1}
and
j_{1}\in J_{3}
, we have

\displaystyle|\{g=x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}|\max\{i\in J_{1}|\alpha_{i}>0\}=i_{1},\min(\{j\in J_{3}|\beta_{j}>0\})=j_{1}\}|

(4.17)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle G_{0}(n-j_{1}+1,i_{1},\ell_{1},\ell_{2})

and

\displaystyle|\{h\in Z^{(k)}|f=gh\in S_{k}\text{ for some }g=x^{\alpha}y^{\beta},i_{1}(f)=i_{1},j_{1}(f)=j_{1}\}|

(4.18)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{2}(n-n_{1},n_{1},j_{1}-n_{1},i_{1},k).

Hence

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}}(k)

(4.19)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\dim(M_{k}/M_{k-1})=|S_{k}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}\limits^{n_{1}}\sum\limits_{j_{1}=n_{1}+1}\limits^{n}G_{0}(n-j_{1}+1,i_{1},\ell_{1},\ell_{2})P_{2}(n-n_{1},n_{1},j_{1}-n_{1},i_{1},k)\text{ if }\ell_{1},\ell_{2}<0;\vskip 5.69046pt\\
\sum\limits_{j_{1}=n_{1}+1}\limits^{n}G_{0}(n-j_{1}+1,1,\ell_{1},\ell_{2})P_{1}(n_{1},n-n_{1},n-j_{1}+1,k)\text{ if }\ell_{1}=0,\ell_{2}<0;\vskip 5.69046pt\\
\sum\limits_{i_{1}=1}\limits^{n_{1}}G_{0}(n,i_{1},\ell_{1},\ell_{2})P_{1}(n-n_{1},n_{1},i_{1},k)\text{ if }\ell_{1}<0,\ell_{2}=0;\vskip 5.69046pt\\
h_{3}(n-n_{1},n_{1},k)\text{ if }\ell_{1}=0,\ell_{2}=0.\end{cases}

According to the corollary
2.1
and the corollary
2.2
, when
1<n_{1}<n-1
,

\displaystyle\deg_{k}\left(P_{2}(n-n_{1},n_{1},j_{1}-n_{1},i_{1},k)\right)=\begin{cases}2n-5&\text{ if }i_{1}=1,j_{1}=n;\\
n+j_{1}-i_{1}-3&\text{ else}.\end{cases}

(4.20)

and

\displaystyle\deg_{k}\left(P_{1}(n-n_{1},n_{1},i_{1},k)\right)=\begin{cases}2n-5&\text{ if }i_{1}=1;\\
2n-i_{1}-3&\text{ else}.\end{cases}

(4.21)

Hence when
\ell_{1},\ell_{2}<0
and
|J_{1}|,|J_{3}|>1
,

\displaystyle lc(M)=lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}})

(4.22)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle lc\left(\sum_{(i_{1},j_{1})\in\{(1,n),(1,n-1),(2,n)\}}G_{0}(n-j_{1}+1,i_{1},\ell_{1},\ell_{2})P_{2}(n-n_{1},n_{1},j_{1}-n_{1},i_{1},k)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\ell_{1}-\ell_{2})lc_{k}(P_{2}(n-n_{1},n_{1},n-n_{1},1,k))=(1-\ell_{1}-\ell_{2})lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M}).

Similarly, we also have
lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}})=(1-\ell_{1})lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M})
when
\ell_{2}=0
and
lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}})=(1-\ell_{2})lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M})
when
\ell_{1}=0
from (
4.16
),(
4.19
) and (
4.21
).

When
n_{1}=1
, we have
\deg(\mathscr{V}(M))=1
and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}}(k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\left\{x_{1}^{-\ell_{1}}y^{\beta}h\Big|\beta_{1}=0,\sum_{j\in J_{3}}\beta_{j}=-\ell_{2},h\in Z^{(k)}\right\}\right|

(4.23)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\binom{n+k-2}{k}\binom{n-\ell_{2}-2}{-\ell_{2}}.

Hence,

lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}}(k))=\frac{1}{(n-2)!}\binom{n-\ell_{2}-2}{-\ell_{2}}.

(4.24)

Similarly, when
n-n_{2}=1
,

lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}}(k))=\frac{1}{(n-2)!}\binom{n-\ell_{1}-2}{-\ell_{1}}.

(4.25)

Therefore, we draw the following conclusion.

Lemma 4.2
.

If
\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\leq 0
and
n_{1}=n_{2}<n
, we have

\frac{lc(M)}{lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M})}=\begin{cases}1-\ell_{1}-\ell_{2}&\text{ if }1<n_{1}<n-1;\\
\binom{n-\ell_{2}-2}{-\ell_{2}}&\text{ if }n_{1}=1;\\
\binom{n-\ell_{1}-2}{-\ell_{1}}&\text{ if }n_{1}=n-1.\end{cases}

(4.26)

4.3
Case:
n_{1}<n_{2}<n
and
\ell_{1}\leq 0
or
\ell_{2}\leq 0

For
n_{1},n_{2},n\in\mathbb{N}
with
n_{1}<n_{2}\leq n
, we define an associative algebra isomorphism
\Psi:\mathscr{A}\rightarrow\mathscr{A}
by

\displaystyle\Psi(x_{i})=y_{n-i+1},\;\;\Psi(y_{i})=x_{n-i+1},\Psi(x_{j})=y_{n-j+1},\;\;\Psi(y_{j})=x_{n-j+1},\qquad j\in J_{3},i\in J_{1};

\displaystyle\Psi(x_{n_{1}+s})=y_{n-n_{2}+s},\;\;\Psi(y_{n_{1}+s})=x_{n-n_{2}+s},\qquad s\in\overline{1,n_{2}-n_{1}}.

(4.27)

According to (
1.8
), for
f\in{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}^{n_{1},n_{2},n}
, we have
\Psi(f)\in{\mathscr{A}}_{\langle\ell_{2},\ell_{1}\rangle}^{n-n_{2},n-n_{1},n}
,
and

\displaystyle\tilde{\Delta}^{n-n_{2},n-n_{1},n}(\Psi(f))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n-n_{2}}x_{i}\partial_{y_{i}}(\Psi(f))-\sum_{r=n-n_{2}+1}^{n-n_{1}}\partial_{x_{r}}\partial_{y_{r}}(\Psi(f))+\sum_{s=n-n_{1}+1}^{n}y_{s}\partial_{x_{s}}(\Psi(f))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n-n_{2}}\Psi(y_{n-i+1}\partial_{x_{n-i+1}}(f))-\sum_{r=n_{1}+1}^{n_{2}}\Psi(\partial_{x_{r}}\partial_{y_{r}}(f))+\sum_{s=n-n_{1}+1}^{n}\Psi(x_{n-s}\partial_{y_{n-s}}(f))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Psi(\tilde{\Delta}^{n_{1},n_{2},n}(f))=0.

(4.28)

Thus
\Psi:{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}^{n_{1},n_{2},n}\rightarrow{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{2},\ell_{1}\rangle}^{n-n_{2},n-n_{1},n}
is a linear space isomorphism.
Moreover, according to (
2.18
),(
2
) and (
3.24
), we have

\dim\left(({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}^{n_{1},n_{2},n})_{k}\right)=\dim\left(\Psi\left({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}^{n_{1},n_{2},n})_{k}\right)\right)=\dim\left(({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{2},\ell_{1}\rangle}^{n-n_{2},n-n_{1},n})_{k}\right)

(4.29)

for
k\geq 0
.

Therefore, in the rest of this section, we suppose that
\ell_{2}\leq 0
.

Let
\{(j_{1},i_{1}),(j_{2},i_{2}),...,(j_{r},i_{r})\}\subset\mathbb{Z}^{2}
.
For
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}\in\mathbb{N}
,
\{s_{1,t_{1}}\in J_{1}|1\leq t_{1}\leq k_{1}\}
,
\{s_{2,t_{2}}\in J_{3}|1\leq t_{2}\leq k_{2}\}
,
\{s_{3,t_{3}}\in J_{3}|1\leq t_{3}\leq k_{3}\}
,
\{(j_{t_{4}},i_{t_{4}})\in J_{3}\times J_{1}|1\leq t_{4}\leq k_{4}\}
and
g\in\mathbb{F}[x_{i},y_{j}|i\in J_{2},j\in J_{1}\cup J_{2}]
,

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}\left[\left(\prod_{t_{1}=1}^{k_{1}}x_{s_{1,t_{1}}}\right)\left(\prod_{t_{2}=1}^{k_{2}}y_{s_{2,t_{2}}}\right)\left(\prod_{t_{3}=1}^{k_{3}}x_{s_{3,t_{3}}}\right)\left(\prod_{t_{4}=1}^{k_{4}}z_{j_{t_{4}},i_{t_{4}}}\right)g\right]

(4.30)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(n+1,s_{1,t_{1}}),(s_{2,t_{2}},0),(s_{3,t_{3}},-1),(j_{t_{4}},i_{t_{4}})|1\leq t_{1}\leq k_{1},

\displaystyle 1\leq t_{2}\leq k_{2},1\leq t_{3}\leq k_{3},1\leq t_{4}\leq k_{4}\},\qquad

We set

\displaystyle S_{k}=S^{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}_{k}=\Big\{\;gh

\displaystyle|

\displaystyle g=\prod_{i\in\overline{1,n}}\prod_{j\in\overline{n_{1}+1,n}}x_{i}^{\alpha_{i}}y_{j}^{\beta_{j}}\in N(k-r),h\in Z^{(r)};

(4.31)

\displaystyle\text{no 3-chains in $\mathscr{I}(gh)$};r=0,1,\cdots k\Big\},

and

\displaystyle W_{k}=\text{Span}\{\phi(S_{k})\}.

(4.32)

Then
T(W_{k})
is a subspace of
M_{k}
.

Before proving
T(W_{k})
is actually a basis of
M_{k}/M_{k-1}
, we need some preparation, which have been proved in
[
34
]
.

(1) The Lemma 3.1 in
[
34
]
is shown below..

Lemma 4.3
.

Fix
k,k_{13},k_{21}\in\mathbb{N}
. For each
j
,
i_{1,j}\in J_{1}
,
i_{3,j}\in J_{3}
,
v_{0}\in\mathbb{F}\left[X_{J_{1}},X_{J_{2}\setminus\{n_{1}+1\}},Y_{J_{3}}\right]\\
\cup\mathbb{F}\left[X_{J_{1}},Y_{J_{2}\setminus\{n_{1}+1\}},Y_{J_{3}}\right]
, we have

\displaystyle\left(-1\right)^{k}\frac{k!}{\left(k-k_{13}\right)!}\ T\left(v_{0}\cdot\left(\prod_{j=1}^{k}x_{i_{1,j}}\right)\left(\prod_{j=1}^{k_{13}}x_{i_{3,j}}\right)\cdot x_{n_{1}+1}^{k-k_{13}}\right)

(4.33)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{i_{3,k_{13}},n_{1}+1}\cdots E_{i_{3,1},n_{1}+1}E_{n_{1}+1,i_{1,1}}\cdots E_{n_{1}+1,i_{1,k}}\left(v_{0}\right)

when
k\geq k_{13}
,
and

\displaystyle\left(-1\right)^{k}\frac{k!}{\left(k-k_{21}\right)!}\ T\left(v_{0}\cdot\left(\prod_{j=1}^{k_{21}}y_{i_{1,j}}\right)\left(\prod_{j=1}^{k}y_{i_{3,j}}\right)\cdot y_{n_{1}+1}^{k-k_{21}}\right)

(4.34)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{n_{1}+1,i_{1,1}}\cdots E_{n_{1}+1,i_{1,k_{21}}}E_{i_{3,1},n_{1}+1}\cdots E_{i_{3,k},n_{1}+1}\left(v_{0}\right)

if
k\geq k_{21}
.

(2) The Corollary 2.1 in
[
34
]
is shown below..

Lemma 4.4
.

Fix
k,\alpha_{n_{1}+1},\beta_{n_{1}+1}\in\mathbb{N}
.
For
j
,
i_{1,j}\in J_{1}
,
i_{3,j}\in J_{3}
,
v_{1}\in\mathbb{F}[X_{J_{1}},X_{J_{2}\setminus\{n_{1}+1\}},\\
Y_{J_{3}}]\cup\mathbb{F}\left[X_{J_{1}},Y_{J_{2}\setminus\{n_{1}+1\}},Y_{J_{3}}\right]
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{k!}{\alpha_{n_{1}+1}!}\ T\left(v_{1}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{k-\alpha_{n_{1}+1}}x_{i_{3,j}}\right)\cdot x_{n_{1}+1}^{\alpha_{n_{1}+1}}\right)=E_{i_{3,k-\alpha_{n_{1}+1}},n_{1}+1}\cdots E_{i_{3,1},n_{1}+1}\left(v_{1}x_{n_{1}+1}^{k}\right),\end{split}

(4.35)

when
k\geq\alpha_{n_{1}+1}
,
and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{k!}{\beta_{n_{1}+1}!}\ T\left(v_{1}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{k-\beta_{n_{1}+1}}y_{i_{1,j}}\right)\cdot y_{n_{1}+1}^{\beta_{n_{1}+1}}\right)=E_{n_{1}+1,i_{1,1}}\cdots E_{n_{1}+1,i_{1,k-\beta_{n_{1}+1}}}\left(v_{1}y_{n_{1}+1}^{k}\right),\end{split}

(4.36)

where
k\geq\beta_{n_{1}+1}
.

(3)
For
f\in M_{k}
(cf. (
2
)), we define the P-
order
of
f
in
M_{k}
as

\displaystyle\mbox{ord}_{k}\left(f\right)=\min\left\{s\in\mathbb{N}|f\in\mbox{Span}\{TN_{k},TN(k-r)P^{r}|r=0,1,2,\cdots,s\}\right\}.

(4.37)

In particular,
\mbox{ord}_{k}\left(f\right)=0\iff f\in TN_{k}
.

The Lemma 4.1 in
[
34
]
is shown below.

Lemma 4.5
.

For
f\in M_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}\left(f\right)\leq k+\mbox{ord}_{k}\left(f\right).

(4.38)

Moreover,
{\mathfrak{d}}\left(f\right)=k+\mbox{ord}_{k}\left(f\right)
if and only if
f\notin TN_{k-1}
.

(4)
Denote

J_{3}^{\prime}=\overline{n_{2}+1,n+1},\quad J^{\prime}_{1}=\overline{-1,n_{1}}

(4.39)

Set

z_{n+1,0}=0,\;z_{n+1,i}=x_{i},\;z_{j,0}=y_{j}\qquad\mbox{for}\;\;i\in J_{1},\;j\in J_{3}.

(4.40)

Then

\mathscr{C}=\mathbb{F}[z_{j,i}\mid j\in\overline{n_{2}+1,n+1},\;i\in\overline{0,n_{1}},\;(j,i)\neq(n+1,0)]

(4.41)

and

\mathscr{R}_{3}=\left\langle\left|\begin{array}[]{ccc}z_{j_{1},i_{1}}&z_{j_{1},i_{2}}&z_{j_{1},i_{3}}\\
z_{j_{2},i_{1}}&z_{j_{2},i_{2}}&z_{j_{2},i_{3}}\\
z_{j_{3},i_{1}}&z_{j_{3},i_{2}}&z_{j_{3},i_{3}}\end{array}\right|\;\mid\;i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}\in J_{1}^{\prime},\;j_{1},j_{2},j_{3}\in J_{3}^{\prime}\right\rangle

(4.42)

be an ideal of
\mathscr{C}
.

Then Lemma 3.3 in
[
34
]
is shown below.

Lemma 4.6
.

We have
\ker\phi|_{\mathscr{C}}=\mathscr{R}_{3}
.

(5) The following lemma is actually a corollary of Lemma 3.1 in
[
34
]
.

Lemma 4.7
.

For
c_{1},c_{2},n_{1},n\in\mathbb{N}
,
c_{1},c_{2}>0
and
1<n_{1}<n
, we define an associative algebra homomorphism

\varphi_{x,y}:{\mathscr{A}}_{c_{1},c_{2}}\rightarrow\mathscr{A}
by

\varphi_{x,y}(x_{i})=y_{i},\;\varphi_{x,y}(x_{i^{\prime}})=x_{i^{\prime}},\;\varphi_{x,y}(y_{j})=y_{j},\;\;\;\mbox{for}\;i\in\overline{1,n_{1}},i^{\prime}\in\overline{1},j\in\overline{1,n}.

(4.43)

Then

\ker\varphi_{x,y}=\left\langle x_{i}y_{j}-x_{j}y_{i}|i,j\in\overline{1,n}\right\rangle\cap{\mathscr{A}}_{c_{1},c_{2}}.

(4.44)

Proof.

Suppose that
f=f(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n},y_{1},\cdots,y_{n})\in\ker\varphi_{x,y}
.
Write

f=\sum_{i=1}^{m}f_{i},\;\;f_{i}=g_{i}h_{i}f^{\prime}_{i}

(4.45)

where
f_{i}
are monomials in
\mathscr{A}
,
g_{i}
are monomials in
\mathbb{F}[x_{1},\cdots,x_{n_{1}}]
,
h_{i}
are monomials in
\mathbb{F}[y_{1},\cdots,y_{n_{1}}]
and
f^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathbb{F}[x_{j},y_{j}|n_{1}<j\leq n]
.
Denote the distinct elements in
\{f_{i}^{\prime}|1\leq i\leq m\}
by
\{F_{1},\cdots,F_{m^{\prime}}\}
.
Then

f=\sum_{j=1}^{m^{\prime}}\sum_{\{i:f^{\prime}_{i}=F_{j}\}}f_{i}

(4.46)

Since
\varphi_{x,y}(f)=0
, we have

\sum_{\{i:f^{\prime}_{i}=F_{j}\}}\varphi_{x,y}(f_{i})=\varphi_{x,y}\left(\sum_{\{i:f^{\prime}_{i}=F_{j}\}}g_{i}h_{i}\right)F_{j}=0

(4.47)

for
j\in\overline{1,m^{\prime}}
.
Hence we may assume that
f_{i}^{\prime}=1
for
i\in\overline{1,n}
.

Denote
U=\{u_{1,1},\cdots,u_{1,n_{1}},u_{2,1},\cdots,u_{2,n_{1}}\}
as a set of
2n_{1}
variables and
\{v_{1},v_{2}\}
as a set of
2
variables.
Define associative algebra homomorphisms
\varphi_{u}:\mathbb{F}[U]\rightarrow\mathbb{F}[v_{1},v_{2},y_{i}|i\in\overline{1,n_{1}}]
by

\varphi_{u}(u_{1,j})=v_{1}y_{j},\;\varphi_{u}(u_{2,j})=v_{2}y_{j}\qquad\mbox{for}\;\;j\in\overline{1,n_{1}}.

(4.48)

Then we have

\displaystyle v_{1}^{c_{1}}v_{2}^{c_{2}}\varphi_{x,y}(f(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n_{1}},y_{1},\cdots,y_{n_{1}}))

(4.49)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle v_{1}^{c_{1}}v_{2}^{c_{2}}\varphi_{x,y}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}g(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n_{1}})h(y_{1},\cdots,y_{n_{1}})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle v_{1}^{c_{1}}v_{2}^{c_{2}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}g(y_{1},\cdots,y_{n_{1}})h(y_{1},\cdots,y_{n_{1}})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}g(v_{1}y_{1},\cdots,v_{1}y_{n_{1}})h(v_{2}y_{1},\cdots,v_{2}y_{n_{1}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varphi_{u}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}g(u_{1,1},\cdots,u_{1,{n_{1}}})h(u_{2,1},\cdots,u_{2,{n_{1}}})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle f(u_{1,1},\cdots,u_{1,{n_{1}}},u_{2,1},\cdots,u_{2,{n_{1}}})

According to Lemma 3.1 in
[
34
]
,

f\in\left\langle x_{i}y_{j}-x_{j}y_{i}|i,j\in\overline{1,n}\right\rangle\cap{\mathscr{A}}_{c_{1},c_{2}}.

(4.50)

∎

Proposition 4.2
.

For
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have
T(W_{k})\oplus M_{k-1}=M_{k}
and
\phi(S_{k})
is a linearly independent set.

Proof.

Denote

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}z_{n_{2}+1,n_{1}}&\cdots&z_{n,n_{1}}&z_{n+1,n_{1}}\\
z_{n_{2}+1,n_{1}-1}&\cdots&z_{n,n_{1}-1}&z_{n+1,n_{1}-1}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots\\
z_{n_{2}+1,1}&\cdots&z_{n,1}&z_{n+1,1}\\
z_{n_{2}+1,0}&\cdots&z_{n,0}&z_{n+1,0}\\
x_{n_{2}+1}&\cdots&x_{n}&-1\end{pmatrix}

(4.51)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}z_{n_{2}+1,n_{1}}&\cdots&z_{n,n_{1}}&x_{n_{1}}\\
z_{n_{2}+1,n_{1}-1}&\cdots&z_{n,n_{1}-1}&x_{n_{1}-1}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots\\
z_{n_{2}+1,1}&\cdots&z_{n,1}&x_{1}\\
y_{n_{2}+1}&\cdots&y_{n}&0\\
x_{n_{2}+1}&\cdots&x_{n}&-1\end{pmatrix}.

It is easy to verify that the 3-minors of
\phi(\mathbf{A})
are all zero. Using the method in the proof of Proposition
2.2
(or the proof of Lemma 3.3 in
[
34
]
), we can show that
T(W_{k})+M_{k-1}=M_{k}
.

Next, we will prove that
M_{k-1}\cap T(W_{k})=\emptyset
and
\phi(S_{k})
is a basis of
W_{k}
.

We assume that exists
f\in\text{Span}\{S_{k}\}
and
T(\phi(f))\in M_{k-1}
.
Write

\displaystyle f=\sum_{r=0}^{r_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{r}}a_{i,r}g_{i,r}h_{i,r}

(4.52)

where
g_{i,r}h_{i,r}\in S_{k}
,
g_{i,r}=\prod_{i\in\overline{1,n}}\prod_{j\in\overline{n_{1}+1,n}}x_{i}^{\alpha_{i}}y_{j}^{\beta_{j}}\in N(k-r)
,
h_{i,r}\in Z^{(r)}
and there are no 3-chains in
\mathscr{I}(g_{i,r}h_{i,r})
, for
i\in\overline{1,N_{r}}
,
r\in\overline{0,r_{0}}
,
r_{0}=\mbox{ord}_{k}(T\phi(f))\in\overline{0,k}
.
For convenience, we assume that
g_{i,r}h_{i,r}
are distinct for different
(i,r)
and
a_{i,r}\neq 0
for each
(i,r)
.

We may assme that
g_{i,r}\in\mathbb{F}[x_{i},y_{j}|i,j\in\overline{1,n}\setminus J_{2}]
. Otherwise, we set

\displaystyle g_{i,r}=u_{i,r}v_{i,r}

(4.53)

where
u_{i,r}\in\mathbb{F}[x_{i},y_{j}|i,j\in\overline{1,n}\setminus J_{2}]
and
v_{i,r}\in\mathbb{F}[x_{i},y_{j}|i,j\in J_{2}]
.
Since
T\phi(f)\in M_{k-1}
, we have

\displaystyle\phi(f)=\sum_{r=0}^{r^{\prime}_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N^{\prime}_{r}}a^{\prime}_{i,r}g^{\prime}_{i,r}\phi(h^{\prime}_{i,r})=\sum_{r=0}^{r^{\prime}_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N^{\prime}_{r}}a^{\prime}_{i,r}u^{\prime}_{i,r}v^{\prime}_{i,r}\phi(h^{\prime}_{i,r})

(4.54)

where
g^{\prime}_{i,r}\in N(k-1-r)
,
h^{\prime}_{i,r}\in Z^{(r)}
,
u^{\prime}_{i,r}\in\mathbb{F}[x_{i},y_{j}|i,j\in\overline{1,n}\setminus J_{2}]
and
v^{\prime}_{i,r}\in\mathbb{F}[x_{i},y_{j}|i,j\in J_{2}]
for
r^{\prime}_{0}\in\overline{1,k-1}
.

Set
\mathscr{T}=\{(i,r)|v_{i,r}=v_{1,r_{0}}\}
and
\mathscr{T}^{\prime}=\{(i,r)|v^{\prime}_{i,r}=v_{1,r_{0}}\}
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{(i,r)\in\mathscr{T}^{\prime}}a^{\prime}_{i,r}u^{\prime}_{i,r}\phi(h^{\prime}_{i,r})=\sum_{(i,r)\in\mathscr{T}}a_{i,r}u_{i,r}\phi(h_{i,r})

(4.55)

For
j=1,2,3
and
x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}\in\mathscr{A}
, we denote

\deg_{x_{{}_{J_{j}}}}(x^{\alpha}y^{\beta})=\sum_{i\in J_{j}}\alpha_{i},\qquad\deg_{y_{{}_{J_{j}}}}(x^{\alpha}y^{\beta})=\sum_{i\in J_{j}}\beta_{i}\;.

(4.56)

Denote
\deg_{x_{{}_{J_{2}}}}(v_{1,r_{0}})=k_{12}
,
\deg_{y_{{}_{J_{2}}}}(v_{1,r_{0}})=k_{22}
, and we set

\displaystyle\overline{f}=\sum_{(i,r)\in\mathscr{T}}a_{i,r}u_{i,r}h_{i,r}.

(4.57)

Then
T(\phi(\overline{f}))\in\mathscr{H}_{\langle\ell_{1}-k_{12},\ell_{2}-k_{22}\rangle}
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}_{\langle\ell_{1}-k_{12},\ell_{2}-k_{22}\rangle}^{n_{1},n_{2},n}(u_{i,r})=\mathfrak{d}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}^{n_{1},n_{2},n}(u_{i,r})-k_{12}-k_{22}

(4.58)

for
(i,r)\in\mathscr{T}
.

Moreover,

\displaystyle T\left(\sum_{(i,r)\in\mathscr{T}^{\prime}}a^{\prime}_{i,r}u^{\prime}_{i,r}\phi(h^{\prime}_{i,r})\right)\in(\mathscr{H}_{\langle\ell_{1}-k_{12},\ell_{2}-k_{22}\rangle})_{k-1-k_{12}-k_{22}}.

(4.59)

Note that
g_{i,r}h_{i,r}=g_{i^{\prime},r^{\prime}}h_{i^{\prime},r^{\prime}}
can be deduced from
u_{i,r}h_{i,r}=u_{i^{\prime},r^{\prime}}h_{i^{\prime},r^{\prime}}
.
By induction on
k
, we have
\overline{f}=0
and
a_{i,r}=0
for each
(i,r)\in\mathscr{T}
if
k_{12}+k_{22}>0
.

Since
\overline{f}=0
is independent of the selection of
v_{1,r_{0}}
, we have
a_{i,r}=0
for
i\in\overline{1,N_{r}}
and
r\in\overline{0,r_{0}}
. In other words, we can prove that
f\in M_{k-1}+T(W_{k})
and
M_{k-1}+T(W_{k})=M_{k}
.

Hence, we assme that
g_{i,r}\in\mathbb{F}[x_{i},y_{j}|i,j\in\overline{1,n}\setminus J_{2}]
in the following.

(a) Suppose that
\ell_{1}\leq 0
.

We set

g_{i,r}=\left(\prod_{j=1}^{k_{13}}x_{s_{i,r,j}}x_{t_{i,r,j}}\right)v_{i,r}

(4.60)

and

h_{i,r}=\prod_{j=1}^{r}z_{p_{i,r,j},q_{i,r,j}},

(4.61)

where
s_{i,r,j}\in J_{3}
,
t_{i,r,j}\in J_{1}
,
(p_{i,r,j},q_{i,r,j})\in J_{3}\times J_{1}
,
v_{i,r}\in M_{0}\cap\mathbb{F}[x_{i},y_{j}|i\in J_{1},j\in J_{3}]
and
2k_{13}+r=k
.

According to Lemma
4.3
, we have

\displaystyle T(\phi(f))

(4.62)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T\left(\sum_{r=0}^{r_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{r}}a_{i,r}g_{i,r}\phi(h_{i,r})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T\left(\sum_{r=0}^{r_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{r}}\left(\prod_{j^{\prime}=1}^{r}\phi\left(z_{p_{i,r,j^{\prime}},q_{i,r,j^{\prime}}}\right)\prod_{j=1}^{k_{13}}x_{s_{i,r,j}}x_{t_{i,r,j}}\right)a_{i,r}v_{i,r}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{r=0}^{r_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{r}}\left(\frac{(-1)^{k_{13}}}{k_{13}!}\prod_{j^{\prime}=1}^{r}E_{p_{i,r,j^{\prime}},q_{i,r,j^{\prime}}}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{k_{13}}E_{s_{i,r,j},n_{1}+1}\right)\left(\prod_{j=1}^{k_{13}}E_{n_{1}+1,t_{i,r,j}}\right)a_{i,r}v_{i,r}\right)

\displaystyle\in

\displaystyle M_{k-1}

We set

H=\sum_{r=0}^{r_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{r}}\left(\phi\left(\prod_{j^{\prime}=1}^{r}z_{p_{i,r,j^{\prime}},q_{i,r,j^{\prime}}}\right)\left(\prod_{j=1}^{k_{13}}y_{s_{i,r,j}}y_{n_{1}+1}x_{t_{i,r,j}}x_{n_{1}+1}\right)a_{i,r}v_{i,r}\right)

(4.63)

Suppose that the set of distinct elements of
\{r|\ \text{exists }i\text{ such that }a_{i,r}\neq 0\}
is
\{\jmath_{1},\dots,\jmath_{N}\}
, and
\jmath_{1}<\jmath_{2}<\cdots<\jmath_{N}=r_{0}
. For
\iota\in\overline{1,N}
, we set

H_{\iota}=\sum_{i=1}^{N_{\jmath_{\iota}}}\left(\phi\left(\prod_{j^{\prime}=1}^{\jmath_{\iota}}z_{p_{i,\jmath_{\iota},j^{\prime}},q_{i,\jmath_{\iota},j^{\prime}}}\right)\left(\prod_{j=1}^{k_{13}}y_{s_{i,\jmath_{\iota},j}}y_{n_{1}+1}x_{t_{i,\jmath_{\iota},j}}x_{n_{1}+1}\right)a_{i,\jmath_{\iota}}v_{i,\jmath_{\iota}}\right)

(4.64)

For
\iota^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
and
F=\sum_{i=1}^{m^{\prime}}F_{i}\in\mathscr{A}
, where
F_{i}
are distinct monomials in
\mathscr{A}
, we define

{\mathfrak{r}}(F,\iota^{\prime})=\min\{\deg_{x_{{}_{J_{3}}}}(F_{i})+\deg_{y_{{}_{J_{1}}}}(F_{i})\mid\mathfrak{d}(F_{i})=\iota^{\prime},i\in\overline{1,m^{\prime}}\}.

(4.65)

Then we have

{\mathfrak{r}}(T(\phi(f)),k+\jmath_{\iota})\leq{\mathfrak{r}}\left(H_{\iota},\mathfrak{d}(H_{\iota})\right)=\jmath_{\iota}.

(4.66)

Since
T(\phi(f))\in M_{k-1}
and
T(\phi(f))\notin TN_{k-1}
, according to Lemma
4.5
, we have

{\mathfrak{d}}\left(T(\phi(f))\right)=\mbox{ord}_{k-1}\left(T(\phi(f))\right)+k-1\Rightarrow\mbox{ord}_{k-1}\left(T(\phi(f))\right)\geq 1+\jmath_{\iota}.

(4.67)

For
r=\mbox{ord}_{k-1}\left(T(\phi(f))\right)\geq 1+\jmath_{\iota}
in this case, we can write

\displaystyle T(\phi(f))=\sum_{s_{r}=1}^{l_{r}}G_{r,s_{r}}G^{\prime}_{r,s_{r}}+\sum_{s_{r-1}=1}^{l_{r-1}}G_{r-1,s_{r-1}}G^{\prime}_{r-1,s_{r-1}}+\cdots+G_{0},

(4.68)

where
G_{i,s_{i}}\in P^{\left(i\right)}
,
G^{\prime}_{i,s_{i}}\in TN(k-1-i)
for
s_{i}\in\overline{1,l_{i}}
, and
G_{0}\in TN_{k-1}
.
Note that for
s_{i}\in\overline{1,l_{i}}
and
i\in\overline{1,r}
,

{\mathfrak{d}}(G_{i,s_{i}}G^{\prime}_{i,s_{i}})=k-1-i+2i=k-1+i

(4.69)

Since
\overline{h}_{\iota}\neq 0
and
{\mathfrak{d}}(\overline{h}_{\iota})=p+\jmath_{\iota}
, we have

\sum_{s_{\jmath_{\iota}+1}=1}^{l_{\jmath_{\iota}+1}}G_{\jmath_{\iota}+1,s_{\jmath_{\iota}+1}}G^{\prime}_{\jmath_{\iota}+1,s_{\jmath_{\iota}+1}}\neq 0.

(4.70)

Therefore,

{\mathfrak{r}}(T(\phi(f)),k+\jmath_{\iota})={\mathfrak{r}}\left(\sum_{s_{\jmath_{\iota}+1}=1}^{l_{\jmath_{\iota}+1}}G_{\jmath_{\iota}+1,s_{\jmath_{\iota}+1}}G^{\prime}_{\jmath_{\iota}+1,s_{\jmath_{\iota}+1}},k+\jmath_{\iota}\right)\geq\jmath_{\iota}+1,

(4.71)

which contradicts (
4.66
).
Hence
H_{\iota}=0
for
w\in\overline{1,N}
.

In particular,
H_{N}=0
.
Then

\displaystyle 0=\sum_{i=1}^{N_{r_{0}}}\left(\phi\left(\prod_{j^{\prime}=1}^{r_{0}}z_{p_{i,r_{0},j^{\prime}},q_{i,r_{0},j^{\prime}}}\right)\left(\prod_{j=1}^{k_{13}}y_{s_{i,r_{0},j}}x_{t_{i,r_{0},j}}\right)a_{i,r_{0}}v_{i,r_{0}}\right)

(4.72)

Define an associative algebra homomorphism

\psi:\mathscr{C}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
by

\displaystyle\psi(z_{j,i})=z_{j,i},\;\;\psi(x_{t})=x_{t},\;\psi(x_{j})=y_{j},\;\psi(y_{r})=y_{r},

\displaystyle\quad i\in J_{1},\;j\in J_{3},t\in\overline{1,n_{2}},r\in\overline{1,n}.

(4.73)

For
i\in\overline{1,N_{r_{0}}}
, we set

\displaystyle\overline{f_{i,r_{0}}}=\left(\prod_{j^{\prime}=1}^{r_{0}}z_{p_{i,r_{0},j^{\prime}},q_{i,r_{0},j^{\prime}}}\right)\left(\prod_{j=1}^{k_{13}}y_{s_{i,r_{0},j}}x_{t_{i,r_{0},j}}\right)a_{i,r_{0}}v_{i,r_{0}}

(4.74)

Then

\phi\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N_{r_{0}}}\overline{f_{i,r_{0}}}\right)=0

(4.75)

and

\psi\left(a_{i,r_{0}}g_{i,r_{0}}h_{i,r_{0}}\right)=\overline{f_{i,r_{0}}}.

(4.76)

Since there are no 3-chains in
\mathscr{I}(g_{i,r_{0}}h_{i,r_{0}})
for each
i\in\overline{1,N_{r_{0}}}
, there are no 3-chains in
\mathscr{I}(\overline{f_{i,r_{0}}})
for each
i\in\overline{1,N_{r_{0}}}
.
Therefore,

\sum_{i=1}^{N_{r_{0}}}\overline{f_{i,r_{0}}}=0

(4.77)

and

\sum_{i=1}^{N_{r_{0}}}a_{i,r_{0}}g_{i,r_{0}}h_{i,r_{0}}\in\ker\psi.

(4.78)

For a monomial
x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}\in\mathscr{A}
, we denote the degree in
\{x_{i_{j}}\mid i_{j}\in J_{j}\}
as
\deg_{x_{{}_{J_{j}}}}\left(x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}\right)
for
j=1,2,3
.
Define
\mathfrak{w}(x^{\alpha}y^{\beta})=\deg_{x_{J_{3}}}(x^{\alpha}y^{\beta})+\deg_{y_{J_{3}}}(x^{\alpha}y^{\beta})
.
For
F\in\ker\psi
, we can decompose
F
by different
\mathfrak{w}
:

F=\sum_{\{s:\mathfrak{w}(F_{s,t})=w_{s}\}}\sum_{t}c_{s,t}F_{s,t},

(4.79)

where
F_{s,t}
are monomials with
\mathfrak{w}(F_{s,t})=w_{s}
and
c_{s,t}\in\mathbb{F}
for each
s,t
.

Thus
\psi(F_{s,t})
are also monomials and
\mathfrak{w}\left(\psi(F_{s,t})\right)=w_{s}
for each
s,t
. Therefore, for a given
s
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t}c_{s,t}F_{s,t}\in\langle x_{t}y_{t^{\prime}}-x_{t^{\prime}}y_{t}|\;t,t^{\prime}\in J_{3}\rangle

(4.80)

by Lemma
4.7
.
Hence,

\sum_{i=1}^{N_{r_{0}}}a_{i,r_{0}}g_{i,r_{0}}h_{i,r_{0}}\in\langle x_{t}y_{t^{\prime}}-x_{t^{\prime}}y_{t}|\;t,t^{\prime}\in J_{3}\rangle.

(4.81)

There do not exist
i_{3},j_{3}\in J_{3}
such that
i_{3}<j_{3}
and
x_{i_{3}}y_{j_{3}}\mid g_{i,r}
.
Otherwise, there exists
i_{1}\in J_{1}
such that
x_{i_{1}}\mid g_{i,r}
because of
\ell_{1}\leq 0
. Then

(i_{3},-1)\prec(j_{3},0)\prec(n+1,i_{1})
is a 3-chain in
\mathscr{I}(g_{i,r}h_{i,r})
, which contradicts (
4.31
).
Then
g_{i,r}\equiv g^{\prime}\quad(\mbox{mod}\;\langle x_{t}y_{t^{\prime}}-x_{t^{\prime}}y_{t}|\;t\neq t^{\prime}\in J_{3}\rangle)
forces
g^{\prime}=g_{i,r}
.
According to (
4.81
),
\sum_{i=1}^{N_{r_{0}}}a_{i,r_{0}}g_{i,r_{0}}h_{i,r_{0}}=0
, whcih contradicts
a_{i,r}\neq 0
. Therefore
f=0
.

Since elements in
S_{k}
are monomials,
\phi(S_{k})
is a linearly independent set.

(b) Consider
\ell_{1}>0
.

We set

g_{i,r}=\left(\prod_{j=1}^{k_{13}}x_{s_{i,r,j}}\right)v_{i,r}

(4.82)

and

h_{i,r}=\prod_{j=1}^{r}z_{p_{i,r,j},q_{i,r,j}},

(4.83)

where
s_{i,r,j}\in J_{3}
,
(p_{i,r,j},q_{i,r,j})\in J_{3}\times J_{1}
,
v_{i,r}\in\mathbb{F}[x_{i},y_{j}|i\in J_{1},j\in J_{3}]
.
Moreover, we have
k_{13}\geq\ell_{1}
,
\deg_{x_{J_{1}}}(v_{i,r})=k_{13}-\ell_{1}
,
k-r=2k_{13}-\ell_{1}
,
k\geq\ell_{1}
,
r\leq r_{0}\leq k-\ell_{1}
.

According to Lemma
4.4
, we have

\displaystyle T(\phi(f))

(4.84)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T\left(\sum_{r=0}^{r_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{r}}a_{i,r}g_{i,r}\phi(h_{i,r})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T\left(\sum_{r=0}^{r_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{r}}\left(\prod_{j^{\prime}=1}^{r}\phi\left(z_{p_{i,r,j^{\prime}},q_{i,r,j^{\prime}}}\right)\prod_{j=1}^{k_{13}}x_{s_{i,r,j}}\right)a_{i,r}v_{i,r}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{r=0}^{r_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{r}}\left(\frac{a_{i,r}}{k_{13}!}\prod_{j^{\prime}=1}^{r}E_{p_{i,r,j^{\prime}},q_{i,r,j^{\prime}}}\left(\prod_{j=1}^{k_{13}}E_{s_{i,r,j},n_{1}+1}\right)(v_{i,r}x_{n_{1}+1}^{k_{13}})\right)

\displaystyle\in

\displaystyle M_{k-1}

with
v_{i,r}x_{n_{1}+1}^{k_{13}}\in M_{k_{13}-\ell_{1}}
.

By Lemma
4.7
, for
r\in\overline{0,r_{0}}
, we also have

\sum_{i=1}^{N_{r}}a_{i,r}g_{i,r}h_{i,r_{0}}\in\langle x_{t}y_{t^{\prime}}-x_{t^{\prime}}y_{t}|\;t,t^{\prime}\in J_{3}\rangle.

(4.85)

We may assume that

\sum_{i=1}^{N_{r}}a_{i,r}g_{i,r}h_{i,r}=v_{r}+v^{\prime}_{r},

(4.86)

where
\deg_{x_{J_{1}}}(v_{r})=0
and each monomial in
v^{\prime}_{r}
has a factor in
\{x_{i}|i\in J_{1}\}
.
According to (
4.85
), we may assume that

\sum_{i=1}^{N_{r}}a_{i,r}g_{i,r}h_{i,r}=\sum_{i}(x_{t_{i}}y_{t^{\prime}_{i}}-x_{t^{\prime}_{i}}y_{t_{i}})u_{i}+\sum_{j}(x_{s_{j}}y_{s^{\prime}_{j}}-x_{s^{\prime}_{j}}y_{s_{j}})u^{\prime}_{j}

(4.87)

where
u_{i},u^{\prime}_{j}\in\mathscr{C}
are monomials such that
\deg_{x_{J_{1}}}(u_{i})=0
and
\deg_{x_{J_{1}}}(u^{\prime}_{j})>0
for each
i,j
.

Then

v_{r}=\sum_{i}(x_{t_{i}}y_{t^{\prime}_{i}}-x_{t^{\prime}_{i}}y_{t_{i}})u_{i},\;v^{\prime}_{r}=\sum_{j}(x_{s_{j}}y_{s^{\prime}_{j}}-x_{s^{\prime}_{j}}y_{s_{j}})u^{\prime}_{j}\in\ker\psi.

(4.88)

Since there are no 3-chain in
\mathscr{I}(v^{\prime}_{0})
, we also have
v^{\prime}_{r}=0
just like what we discussed in the case of
\ell_{1}\geq 0
. Then we have
f=\sum_{r=0}^{r_{0}}v_{r}
. In other words,
g_{i,r}\in\mathbb{F}[x_{i},y_{i}|i\in J_{3}]
for
i\in\overline{1,N_{r}}
,
r\in\overline{0,r_{0}}
.

According to Lemma
4.5
, we have

\mbox{ord}_{k-1}(T(\phi(f)))=\mathfrak{d}(T\phi(f))-(k-1)=\mbox{ord}_{k}(T(\phi(f)))+1=r_{0}+1.

(4.89)

Thus we may assume that

\displaystyle T(\phi(f))=\sum_{s_{r_{0}+1}=1}^{l_{r_{0}+1}}c_{r_{0}+1,s_{r_{0}+1}}G_{r_{0}+1,s_{r_{0}+1}}H_{r_{0}+1,s_{r_{0}+1}}+\sum_{s_{r_{0}}=1}^{l_{r_{0}}}c_{r_{0},s_{r_{0}}}G_{r_{0},s_{r_{0}}}H_{r_{0},s_{r_{0}}}+\cdots+G_{0}

(4.90)

with
c_{r,s_{r}}\in\mathbb{F}
,
G_{r,s_{r}}\in TN(k-1-r)
and
H_{r,s_{r}}\in\phi(Z^{(r)})
for
r\in\overline{1,r_{0}+1}
,
s_{r}\in\overline{1,l_{r}}
and
G_{0}\in N_{k-1}
.
Note that

\xi=\sum_{s_{r_{0}+1}=1}^{l_{r_{0}+1}}c_{r_{0}+1,s_{r_{0}+1}}G_{r_{0}+1,s_{r_{0}+1}}H_{r_{0}+1,s_{r_{0}+1}}\neq 0,

(4.91)

otherwise
\mbox{ord}_{k-1}(T(\phi(f)))<r_{0}+1
.

For a polynomial
f\in\mathscr{A}
, we denote

\mathfrak{m}(f)=\deg_{x_{J_{1}}}(f)+\deg_{y_{J_{1}}}(f).

(4.92)

Then
\mathfrak{m}\left(H_{r_{0}+1,s_{r_{0}+1}}\right)=r_{0}+1
for
s_{r_{0}+1}\in\overline{1,l_{r_{0}+1}}
.
For each monomial
u\in\mathscr{A}
in
\xi
, we have
\mathfrak{m}(u)=r_{0}+1
.
Hence we have

\mathfrak{m}\left(T\phi(f)\right)\geq\mathfrak{m}\left(\xi\right)=r_{0}+1.

(4.93)

On the other hand, since
g_{i,r}\in\mathbb{F}[x_{i},y_{i}|i\in J_{3}]
for
i\in\overline{1,N_{r}}
,
r\in\overline{0,r_{0}}
, we have

\mathfrak{m}\left(T(g_{i,r})\right)=0.

(4.94)

Hence

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}\left(T\phi(f)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}\left(\sum_{r=0}^{r_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{r}}a_{i,r}T(g_{i,r})\phi(h_{i,r})\right)

(4.95)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{0\leq r\leq r_{0},1\leq i\leq N_{r}}\left\{\mathfrak{m}\left(T(g_{i,r})\phi(h_{i,r})\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{0\leq r\leq r_{0},1\leq i\leq N_{r}}\left\{r\right\}\leq r_{0},

which contradicts (
4.93
).
Therefore,
f=0
.
∎

Corollary 4.1
.

When
n_{1}<n_{2}<n
,
\ell_{2}\leq 0
, we have
\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}}(k)=|S_{k}|
.

Note that
\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}}(k)=\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)
when
\ell_{1}=\ell_{2}=0
according to Proposition
3.1
.

For
f=gh\in S^{\langle 0,0\rangle}_{k}
,
g=x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}\in N(k-r)
with
\beta_{i}=0
for
i\in J_{1}
,
h\in Z^{(r)}
, we write

g=\left(\prod_{s=1}^{k_{12}+k_{13}}x_{i_{1,s}}\right)\left(\prod_{s=1}^{k_{12}}x_{i_{2,s}}\right)\left(\prod_{s=1}^{k_{13}}x_{i_{3,s}}\right)\left(\prod_{t=1}^{k_{22}}y_{j_{2,t}}\right)\left(\prod_{t=1}^{k_{22}}y_{j_{3,t}}\right)

(4.96)

with
n_{1}\geq i_{1,1}\geq i_{1,2}\geq\cdots\geq i_{1,k_{12}+k_{13}}\geq 1
,
n_{2}\geq i_{2,1}\geq i_{2,2}\geq\cdots\geq i_{2,k_{12}}\geq n_{1}+1
,
n_{2}+1\leq i_{3,1}\leq i_{3,2}\leq\cdots\leq i_{3,k_{13}}\leq n
,
n_{2}\geq j_{2,1}\geq j_{2,2}\geq\cdots\geq j_{2,k_{22}}\geq n_{1}+1
,
n_{2}+1\leq j_{3,1}\leq j_{3,2}\leq\cdots\leq j_{3,k_{23}}\leq n
and
2k_{13}+k_{22}+k_{12}=k-r
.
We define the following map
\Upsilon
from
S^{\langle 0,0\rangle}_{k}
to
S_{2}^{\overline{n_{2}+1,n+n_{2}-n_{1}},\overline{n_{1}-n_{2}+1,n_{1}}}(n,1,k)
by

\Upsilon(f)=\left(\prod_{s=1}^{k_{12}}z_{n+1+n_{2}-i_{2,s},i_{1,s}}\right)\left(\prod_{t=1}^{k_{22}}z_{j_{3,t},j_{2,t}}\right)\left(\prod_{s=1}^{k_{13}}z_{n+n_{2}-n_{1},i_{1,s+k_{12}}}z_{i_{3,s},n_{1}-n_{2}+1}\right)h.

(4.97)

For
f\in S_{2}^{\overline{n_{2}+1,n+n_{2}-n_{1}},\overline{n_{1}-n_{2}+1,n_{1}}}(n,1,k)
, we write

f=\left(\prod_{q=1}^{k_{3}}z_{p_{3,q},p_{1,q}}\right)\left(\prod_{s=1}^{k_{1}}z_{i_{2,s},i_{1,s}}\right)\left(\prod_{t=1}^{k_{2}}z_{j_{3,t},j_{2,t}}\right).

(4.98)

with
p_{3,q}\in J_{3}
,
p_{1,q}\in J_{1}
,
n+1\leq i_{2,k_{2}}\leq\cdots\leq i_{2,2}\leq i_{2,1}\leq n+n_{2}-n_{1}
,
i_{1,s}\in J_{1}
,
j_{3,t}\in J_{3}
,
n_{1}-n_{2}+1\leq j_{2,1}\leq j_{2,2}\cdots\leq j_{2,k_{2}}\leq 0
and
k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}=k
for
q\in\overline{1,k_{3}}
,
s\in\overline{1,k_{1}}
,
t\in\overline{1,k_{2}}
.

We define
\Upsilon^{-1}
from
S_{2}^{\overline{n_{2}+1,n+n_{2}-n_{1}},\overline{n_{1}-n_{2}+1,n_{1}}}(n,1,k)
to
S^{\langle 0,0\rangle}_{k}
by

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{-1}(f)

(4.99)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\prod_{q=1}^{k_{3}}z_{p_{3,q},p_{1,q}}\right)\left(\prod_{s=k_{0}+1}^{k_{1}}x_{n+n_{2}+1-i_{2,s}}x_{i_{1,s}}\right)\left(\prod_{t=k_{0}+1}^{k_{2}}y_{j_{3,t}}y_{j_{2,t}+n_{2}}\right)\left(\prod_{s=1}^{k_{0}}x_{j_{3,s}}x_{i_{1,s}}\right)\qquad\quad

where

\displaystyle k_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min\{\min\{s|i_{2,s}<n+n_{2}-n_{1}\},\max\{t|j_{2,t}>n_{1}-n_{2}+1\}\}-1

(4.100)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min\left\{\sum_{i\in J_{1}}\deg_{z_{n+n_{2}-n_{1},i}}(f),\sum_{j\in J_{3}}\deg_{z_{j,n_{1}-n_{2}+1}}(f)\right\}.

Then we have
\Upsilon(\Upsilon^{-1}(f))=f
and
\Upsilon^{-1}(\Upsilon(f^{\prime}))=f^{\prime}
for
f\in S_{2}^{\overline{n_{2}+1,n+n_{2}-n_{1}},\overline{n_{1}-n_{2}+1,n_{1}}}(n,1,k)
and
f^{\prime}\in S^{\langle 0,0\rangle}_{k}
.

Hence
\Upsilon
is one-to-one, which yields that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)=|S^{\langle 0,0\rangle}_{k}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|S_{2}^{\overline{n_{2}+1,n+n_{2}-n_{1}},\overline{n_{1}-n_{2}+1,n_{1}}}(n,1,k)|

(4.101)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{2}(n-n_{1},n_{2},n-n_{2},n_{2}-n_{1}+1,k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{2}(n_{2},n-n_{1},n_{1},n_{2}-n_{1}+1,k)

by (
2.47
).

Therefore,
\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}}(k)=\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)=P_{2}(n_{2},n-n_{1},n_{1},n_{2}-n_{1}+1,k)
, which is another proof of the Proposition
2.2
.

Corollary 4.2
.

If
\ell_{1}\leq 0
or
\ell_{2}\leq 0
and
n_{1}<n_{2}<n
, we have

lc(M)=lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M})

(4.102)

Proof.

When
\ell_{1}<0
and
\ell_{2}\leq 0
, we define the map
\mathfrak{t}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
from
S^{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}_{k}
to
S^{\langle 0,0\rangle}_{k-\ell_{1}-\ell_{2}}
by

\mathfrak{t}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}(f)=x_{1}^{-\ell_{1}}y_{n}^{-\ell_{2}}f.

(4.103)

Note that
\mathfrak{t}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
is injective, hence

|S^{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}_{k}|\leq|S^{\langle 0,0\rangle}_{k-\ell_{1}-\ell_{2}}|=\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k-\ell_{1}-\ell_{2})

(4.104)

for sufficiently large
k
.
According to Proposition
3.1
, Corollary
4.1
and (
4.104
),

\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)\leq\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}}(k)\leq\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k-\ell_{1}-\ell_{2})

(4.105)

for sufficiently large
k
.
Therefore,

lc(M)=lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M}).

(4.106)

When
\ell_{1}>0
and
\ell_{2}\leq 0
, for
f=gh\in S^{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}_{k}
with
g=x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}\in N(k-r)
and
h\in Z^{(r)}
, we set

j_{0}(f)=\min\{j\in J_{3}|\alpha_{j}+\beta_{j}>0\},

(4.107)

Denote

Q^{j}_{k}=\{f\in S^{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}_{k}|\deg_{x_{{}_{J_{1}}}}(f)=0,j_{0}(f)=j\}

(4.108)

for
j\in J_{3}
, and

Q^{0}_{k}=\bigcup_{j\in J_{3}}Q^{j}_{k}=\{f\in S^{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}_{k}|\deg_{x_{{}_{J_{1}}}}(f)=0,\deg_{x_{J_{3}}}(f)+\deg_{y_{J_{3}}}(f)>0\}.

(4.109)

For
f\in Q^{j_{0}}_{k}
, we write
f=gh
,
g=x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}
and
h\in Z^{(r)}
.
Then

|\{h\in Z^{(r)}|gh\in Q^{j_{0}}_{k}\text{ for some }g=x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}\}|=P_{1}(n_{1},n-n_{2},n-j_{0}+1,r).

(4.110)

by the definition of
P_{1}
(cf. (
2
)).
According to Corollary
2.1
,

\deg_{r}(|\{h\in Z^{(r)}|gh\in Q^{j_{0}}_{k}\text{ for some }g=x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}\}|)\leq n+2n_{1}-2n_{2}+j_{0}-4.

(4.111)

Moreover,

\displaystyle\deg_{k-r}(|\{g=x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}|gh\in Q^{j_{0}}_{k}\text{ for some }h\in Z^{(r)}\}|)

(4.112)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\deg_{k-r}\left(\sum_{k_{13}=0}^{\ell_{1}}\sum_{k_{23}=0}^{k-r-k_{13}}k_{13}^{n_{2}-n_{1}-2}k_{23}^{n_{2}-n_{1}-2}(k_{13}+k_{23})\binom{n-j_{0}+k_{13}}{k_{13}}\binom{n-j_{0}+k_{23}}{k_{23}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\deg_{k-r}\left(\sum_{k_{13}=0}^{\ell_{1}}\sum_{k_{23}=0}^{k-r-k_{13}}k_{13}^{n_{2}-n_{1}-2}k_{23}^{n_{2}-n_{1}-1}k_{13}^{n-j_{0}}k_{23}^{n-j_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n+n_{2}-n_{1}-j_{0}-1.

Thus

\displaystyle\deg_{k}(|Q^{j_{0}}_{k}|)

(4.113)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(n+2n_{1}-2n_{2}+j_{0}-4)+(n+n_{2}-n_{1}-j_{0}-1)+1

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2n-4+(n_{1}-n_{2})<2n-4,

yields that

\deg_{k}(|Q^{0}_{k}|)<2n-4

(4.114)

Note that

\deg_{k}(\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}})=\deg_{k}(\mathfrak{p}_{M})=\deg_{k}(P_{2}(n_{2},n-n_{1},n_{1},n_{2}-n_{1}+1,k))=2n-4

(4.115)

by the Proposition
2.2
and Corollary
2.2
.
Hence

lc_{k}(\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}})=lc_{k}(|S^{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}_{k}|)=lc_{k}(|S^{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}_{k}\setminus Q^{0}_{k}|).

(4.116)

Define the map
\mathfrak{t}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
from
S^{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}_{k}\setminus Q^{0}_{k}
to
S^{\langle 0,0\rangle}_{k+\ell_{1}-\ell_{2}}
by

\mathfrak{t}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}(f)=x_{1}^{\ell_{1}}y_{n}^{-\ell_{2}}f.

(4.117)

Note that
\mathfrak{t}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
is still injective and we also have

lc(M)=lc_{k}(|S^{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}_{k}\setminus Q^{0}_{k}|)=lc_{k}(|S^{\langle 0,0\rangle}_{k}|)=lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M})

(4.118)

by Proposition
3.1
, Corollary
4.1
and (
4.116
).

According to (
4.29
), (
4.102
) still holds when
\ell_{1}\leq 0
and
\ell_{2}>0
.
∎

4.4
Case:
1<n_{1}=n_{2}<n
,
\ell_{1}\leq 0
and
\ell_{2}\geq 0
.

We set
\ell_{1}=-m_{1}-m_{2}
and
\ell_{2}=m_{2}
with
m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
. In this case,

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle-m_{1}-m_{2},m_{2}\rangle}

(4.119)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mbox{Span}\Big\{[\prod_{r=1}^{n_{1}}x_{r}^{l_{r}}][\prod_{1\leq p<q\leq n_{1}}(x_{p}y_{q}-x_{q}y_{p})^{k_{p,q}}][\prod_{r=1}^{n_{1}}\prod_{s=n_{1}+1}^{n}(x_{r}x_{s}-y_{r}y_{s})^{l_{r,s}}]

\displaystyle\qquad\;\;\mid l_{r},k_{p,q},l_{r,s}\in\mathbb{N};\sum_{r=1}^{n_{1}}l_{r}=m_{1};\sum_{1\leq p<q\leq n_{1}}k_{p,q}=m_{2}\Big\}

by (6.6.53) in
[
33
]
.
Recall that we take

\displaystyle M_{0}=V_{0}=

\displaystyle\mbox{Span}\Big\{\left.\left[\prod_{1\leq p<q\leq n_{1}}(x_{p}y_{q}-x_{q}y_{p})^{k_{p,q}}\right]\prod_{r=1}^{n_{1}}x_{r}^{\alpha_{r}}\right|

(4.120)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad k_{p,q}\in\mathbb{N},\sum_{1\leq p<q\leq n_{1}}k_{p,q}=m_{2},\sum_{1\leq i\leq n_{1}}\alpha_{i}=m_{1}\Big\}.

According to (4.163) in
[
34
]
, we have

M_{k}=\mbox{Span}\{M_{0}\phi(Z^{(i)})\mid i\leq k\}.

(4.121)

In this case, in order to ensure
\mathfrak{d}^{n_{1},n_{1},n}_{\langle-m_{1}-m_{2},m_{2}\rangle}(M_{0})=0
, we have

{\mathfrak{d}}_{\langle-m_{1}-m_{2},m_{2}\rangle}\left(x^{\alpha}y^{\beta}\right)=2\sum_{i\in J_{3}}\alpha_{i}+2\sum_{i\in J_{1}}\beta_{i}.

(4.122)

Set

W^{\prime}_{k}=\mbox{Span}\{M_{0}\phi(Z^{(k)})\}.

(4.123)

For
f\in W_{k}
, we assume that
f=\sum_{i}f_{i}
, where
f_{i}
are monomials in
\mathscr{A}
. Then we have

\deg_{x_{J_{1}}}(f_{i})+\deg_{y_{J_{1}}}(f_{i})=k.

(4.124)

Hence

W^{\prime}_{k}\oplus M_{k-1}=M_{k},

(4.125)

for
k\geq 1
.

Set

O=\{(j,i)\in J_{1}\times J_{1}|j>i\}

(4.126)

and

\Omega=\{\omega_{j,i}\mid(j,i)\in O\}

(4.127)

as a set of
\frac{n_{1}(n_{1}-1)}{2}
variables.
Set

\mathscr{E}=\mathbb{F}[\Omega]

(4.128)

the polynomial algebra in
\Omega
. Denote by
\mathscr{E}_{m}
the subspace of its homogeneous polynomials with degree
m
and by
\Omega^{(m)}
the set of monomials with degree
m
.

Let

\mathscr{F}=\mathbb{F}[z_{j,i},\Omega\mid j\in J_{3}^{\prime},\;i\in\overline{0,n_{1}},\;(j,i)\neq(n+1,0)]

(4.129)

(cf.(
4.40
)) be the algebra of polynomials in the variables
\{z_{j,i},x_{i},y_{j},\omega_{j^{\prime},i^{\prime}}\mid j\in J_{3},\;i\in J_{1},\;(j,i)\neq(n+1,0),(j^{\prime},i^{\prime})\in O\}
.

Define associative algebra homomorphisms
\tilde{\phi}:\mathscr{F}\rightarrow\mathscr{A}
by

\tilde{\phi}(f)=\phi(f),\;\;\tilde{\phi}(\omega_{j,i})=x_{j}y_{i}-x_{j}y_{j}\qquad\mbox{for}\;\;f\in\mathscr{C},\;i<j\in J_{1}.

(4.130)

Then
V_{0}=\text{Span}\Big\{\tilde{\phi}(g)\prod_{i=1}^{n_{1}}x_{i}^{\alpha_{i}}|g\in\Omega^{(m_{2})},\sum_{1\leq i\leq n_{1}}\alpha_{i}=m_{1}\}
and

W^{\prime}_{k}=\mbox{Span}\left\{\tilde{\phi}(gh)\prod_{i=1}^{n_{1}}x_{i}^{\alpha_{r}}\Big|\sum_{1\leq i\leq n_{1}}\alpha_{i}=m_{1},\;g\in\Omega^{(m_{2})},\;h\in Z^{(k)}\right\}.

(4.131)

For
g\in\mathscr{C}
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
1\leq i_{t}<j_{t}\leq n_{1}
for
1\leq t\leq N
, we define

\tilde{\mathscr{I}}\left(g\prod_{t=1}^{N}\omega_{i_{t},j_{t}}\right)=\mathscr{I}(g)\cup\{(n+n_{1}-j_{t}+2,i_{t})|1\leq t\leq N\}

(4.132)

If there exist three distinct
1\leq k_{1},k_{2}\leq r
such that

i_{k_{1}}<i_{k_{2}}
,
j_{k_{1}}<j_{k_{2}}
and
j_{k_{2}}>n+1
, we say that
\{(j_{1},i_{1}),(j_{2},i_{2}),...,(j_{r},i_{r})\}
contains a
\omega
-2-chain
\left(j_{k_{1}},i_{k_{1}}\right)\prec\left(j_{k_{2}},i_{k_{2}}\right)
.

If there exist three distinct
1\leq k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\leq r
such that

i_{k_{3}}\leq i_{k_{1}}<i_{k_{2}}
,
j_{k_{1}}<j_{k_{2}}\leq n+1<j_{k_{3}}<n+n_{1}+2-i_{k_{2}}
, we say that
\{(j_{1},i_{1}),(j_{2},i_{2}),...,(j_{r},i_{r})\}
contains a
\omega
-3-chain
\{\left(j_{k_{1}},i_{k_{1}}\right),\left(j_{k_{2}},i_{k_{2}}\right),(j_{k_{3}},i_{k_{3}})\}
and
\left(j_{k_{1}},i_{k_{1}}\right)\prec\left(j_{k_{2}},i_{k_{2}}\right)\prec(n+n_{1}+2-i_{k_{3}},n+n_{1}+2-j_{k_{3}})
.

We set

\displaystyle S^{\prime}_{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\{gh\prod_{i=1}^{n_{1}}x_{i}^{\alpha_{r}}\Big|\;g\in\Omega^{(m_{2})},\;h\in Z^{(k)};\text{no 3-chains in }\mathscr{I}(h\prod_{i=1}^{n_{1}}x_{i}^{\alpha_{r}}),

(4.133)

\displaystyle\qquad\text{no $\omega$-2-chains or $\omega$-3-chains in }\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(gh\prod_{i=1}^{n_{1}}x_{i}^{\alpha_{r}}),\sum_{i=1}^{n_{1}}\alpha_{i}=m_{1}\Big\}.

Lemma 4.8
.

For
k\geq 0
,
\text{Span}\{\tilde{\phi}(S^{\prime}_{k})\}=W_{k}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

For
f=gh\prod_{i=1}^{n_{1}}x_{i}^{\alpha_{i}}\in W_{k}^{\prime}
and
\tilde{\phi}(f)=\tilde{\phi}(gh)\prod_{i=1}^{n_{1}}x_{i}^{\alpha_{i}}\in W_{k}^{\prime}
with
\sum_{1\leq i\leq n_{1}}\alpha_{i}=m_{1}
,
g\in\Omega^{(m_{2})}
and
h\in Z^{(k)}
, if
\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f)
does not contain
\omega
-2-chains or
\omega
-3-chains, we have proved that
f\in W_{k}^{\prime}
from Lemma 3.4 (The Lemma 3.3 in
[
34
]
). Hence we suppose that
\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f)
contains
\omega
-2-chains or
\omega
-3-chains.
We will discuss the following four cases separately and make a
\tilde{\phi}
-invariant transformation on
f
in each case.

(1) Suppose the
\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f)
contains a
\omega
-2-chain
(s,t)\prec(n+n_{1}+2-j,i)
with
1\leq i<j\leq n_{1}
,
s\in J_{3}^{\prime}
and
1\leq t<i
.
Write
f=z_{s,t}\omega_{j,i}f^{\prime}
, then

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}(f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\phi(z_{s,t})(x_{j}y_{i}-x_{i}y_{j})\tilde{\phi}(f^{\prime})

(4.134)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}(f^{\prime})\phi(z_{s,i})(x_{j}y_{t}-x_{t}y_{j})-\tilde{\phi}(f^{\prime})\phi(z_{s,j})(x_{i}y_{t}-x_{t}y_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}(f^{\prime}z_{s,i}\omega_{j,t})-\tilde{\phi}(f^{\prime}z_{s,j}\omega_{i,t}).

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(s,t),(n+n_{1}-j+2,i),\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f^{\prime})\}

(4.135)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\{(s,i),(n+n_{1}-j+2,t),\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f^{\prime})\}=\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f^{\prime}z_{s,i}\omega_{j,t}).

and

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(s,t),(n+n_{1}-j+2,i),\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f^{\prime})\}

(4.136)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\{(s,i),(n+n_{1}-j+2,t),\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f^{\prime})\}=\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f^{\prime}z_{s,i}\omega_{j,t}).

(2) Similar to the Case (1), we suppose the
\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f)
contains a
\omega
-2-chain
(n+n_{1}+2-s,t)\prec(n+n_{1}+2-j,i)
with
1\leq i<j\leq n_{1}
,
1\leq t<s\leq n_{1}
.
Write
f=\omega_{s,t}\omega_{j,i}f^{\prime}
, then

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}(f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}(f^{\prime})(x_{s}y_{i}-x_{s}y_{i})(x_{j}y_{t}-x_{t}y_{j})-\tilde{\phi}(f^{\prime})(x_{s}y_{j}-x_{s}y_{j})(x_{i}y_{t}-x_{t}y_{i})

(4.137)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}(f^{\prime}\omega_{s,i}\omega_{j,t})-\tilde{\phi}(f^{\prime}\omega_{s,j}\omega_{i,t}).

And we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(n+n_{1}+2-s,t),(n+n_{1}-j+2,i),\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f^{\prime})\}

(4.138)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\{(n+n_{1}+2-s,i),(n+n_{1}-j+2,t),\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f^{\prime})\}=\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f^{\prime}z_{s,i}\omega_{j,t}).

and

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(n+n_{1}+2-s,t),(n+n_{1}-j+2,i),\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f^{\prime})\}

(4.139)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\{(n+n_{1}+2-s,i),(n+n_{1}-j+2,t),\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f^{\prime})\}=\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f^{\prime}z_{s,i}\omega_{j,t}).

(3) Suppose the
\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f)
contains a
\omega
-3-chain
\{\left(s_{1},t_{1}\right),\left(s_{2},t_{2}\right),(n+n_{1}+2-t_{3},t_{1})\}
with
1\leq t_{1}<t_{2}<t_{3}\leq n_{1}
,
s_{1}<s_{2}\in J_{3}^{\prime}
.
Write
f=z_{s_{1},t_{1}}z_{s_{2},t_{2}}\omega_{t_{3},t_{1}}f^{\prime}
, then

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}(f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}(z_{s_{1},t_{1}}z_{s_{2},t_{3}}\omega_{t_{2},t_{1}}f^{\prime})+\tilde{\phi}(z_{s_{1},t_{2}}z_{s_{2},t_{1}}\omega_{t_{3},t_{1}}f^{\prime})-\tilde{\phi}(z_{s_{1},t_{3}}z_{s_{2},t_{1}}\omega_{t_{2},t_{1}}f^{\prime}).

(4.140)

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(s_{1},t_{1}),(s_{2},t_{2}),(n+n_{1}+2-t_{3},t_{1}),\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f^{\prime})\}

(4.141)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\{(s_{1},t_{1}),(s_{2},t_{3}),(n+n_{1}+2-t_{2},t_{1}),\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f^{\prime})\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(z_{s_{1},t_{1}}z_{s_{2},t_{3}}\omega_{t_{2},t_{1}}f^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(s_{1},t_{1}),(s_{2},t_{2}),(n+n_{1}+2-t_{3},t_{1}),\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f^{\prime})\}

(4.142)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\{(s_{1},t_{2}),(s_{2},t_{1}),(n+n_{1}+2-t_{3},t_{1}),\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f^{\prime})\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(z_{s_{1},t_{2}}z_{s_{2},t_{1}}\omega_{t_{3},t_{1}}f^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{(s_{1},t_{1}),(s_{2},t_{2}),(n+n_{1}+2-t_{3},t_{1}),\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f^{\prime})\}

(4.143)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\{(s_{1},t_{3}),(s_{2},t_{1}),(n+n_{1}+2-t_{2},t_{1}),\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f^{\prime})\}

\displaystyle=
