\displaystyle\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(z_{s_{1},t_{3}}z_{s_{2},t_{1}}\omega_{t_{2},t_{1}}f^{\prime}).

(4) Suppose the
\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f)
contains a
\omega
-3-chain
\{\left(s_{1},t_{1}\right),\left(s_{2},t_{2}\right),(n+n_{1}+2-t_{3},t_{0})\}
with
1\leq t_{0}<t_{1}<t_{2}<t_{3}\leq n_{1}
,
s_{1}<s_{2}\in J_{3}^{\prime}
.
Write
f=z_{s_{1},t_{1}}z_{s_{2},t_{2}}\omega_{t_{3},t_{0}}f^{\prime}
, then

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}(f)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}(z_{s_{1},t_{3}}z_{s_{2},t_{2}}\omega_{t_{1},t_{0}}f^{\prime})+\tilde{\phi}(z_{s_{1},t_{1}}z_{s_{2},t_{3}}\omega_{t_{2},t_{0}}f^{\prime})+\tilde{\phi}(z_{s_{1},t_{2}}z_{s_{2},t_{1}}\omega_{t_{3},t_{0}}f^{\prime})

(4.144)

\displaystyle-\tilde{\phi}(z_{s_{1},t_{2}}z_{s_{2},t_{3}}\omega_{t_{1},t_{0}}f^{\prime})-\tilde{\phi}(z_{s_{1},t_{3}}z_{s_{2},t_{1}}\omega_{t_{2},t_{0}}f^{\prime}).

Moreover, we also have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(z_{s_{1},t_{3}}z_{s_{2},t_{2}}\omega_{t_{1},t_{0}}f^{\prime}),\;\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f)<\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(z_{s_{1},t_{1}}z_{s_{2},t_{3}}\omega_{t_{2},t_{0}}f^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(z_{s_{1},t_{2}}z_{s_{2},t_{1}}\omega_{t_{3},t_{0}}f^{\prime}),\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f)<\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(z_{s_{1},t_{2}}z_{s_{2},t_{3}}\omega_{t_{1},t_{0}}f^{\prime}),

(4.145)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(f)<\tilde{\mathscr{I}}(z_{s_{1},t_{3}}z_{s_{2},t_{1}}\omega_{t_{2},t_{0}}f^{\prime}).

Continue this process for
f
, until there are no
\omega
-2-chains or
\omega
-3-chains. Then there exists
g\in\text{Span}\{S_{k}^{\prime}\}
such that
\tilde{\phi}(f)=\tilde{\phi}(g)
. Therefore,
W_{k}^{\prime}=\text{Span}\{\tilde{\phi}(S_{k}^{\prime})\}
.
∎

For
0<m\in\mathbb{N}
, we set

\widehat{J}_{1}^{m}=\{\{i_{1},i_{2},...,i_{m}\}\mid i_{s}\in J_{1}\},\quad\widehat{J}_{3}^{m}=\{\{j_{1},j_{2},...,j_{m}\}\mid i_{t}\in J_{1}\}.

(4.146)

For

f=\left(\prod_{t_{1}=1}^{k_{1}}x_{s_{1,t_{1}}}\right)\left(\prod_{t_{2}=1}^{k_{2}}y_{s_{2,t_{2}}}\right)\left(\prod_{t_{3}=1}^{k_{3}}z_{j_{t_{3}},i_{t_{3}}}\right)\left(\prod_{t_{4}=1}^{k_{4}}\omega_{p_{t_{4}},q_{t_{4}}}\right)g\in\mathscr{E}

(4.147)

with
g\in\mathbb{F}\left[x_{i},y_{j}|i\in J_{2}\cup J_{3},j\in J_{1}\cup J_{2}\right]
;
s_{1,t_{1}},i_{t_{3}},p_{t_{4}},q_{t_{4}}\in J_{1}
;
s_{2,t_{2}},j_{t_{3}}\in J_{3}
and
k_{1}
,
k_{2}
,
k_{3}
,
k_{4}\in\mathbb{N}
, we define

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}_{1}\left(f\right)=\{s_{1,1},...,s_{1,k_{1}},i_{1},...,i_{k_{3}},p_{1},...,p_{k_{4}},q_{1},...,q_{k_{4}}\}\in\widehat{J}_{1}^{k_{1}+k_{3}+2k_{4}},

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}_{3}\left(f\right)=\{s_{2,1},...,s_{2,k_{2}},j_{1},...,j_{k_{3}}\}\in\widehat{J}_{3}^{k_{2}+k_{3}}.

(4.148)

Set

\widehat{J}_{1}^{k_{1}+k_{3},k_{4}}=\{s\in\widehat{J}_{1}^{k_{1}+k_{3},k_{4}}|\text{there exists $g\in\Omega^{(k_{4})}$ such that }\mathscr{I}_{1}(g)\subset s\}.

(4.149)

In the following, we use the lexical order
\leq
on
\mathbb{N}^{2}
.
Fix
{\cal I}_{1}\in\widehat{J}_{1}^{k_{1}+k_{3},k_{4}}
and
{\cal I}_{3}\in\widehat{J}_{3}^{k_{2}+k_{3}}
, we define

\displaystyle G_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}}^{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\{f\,=\,gh\left(\prod_{t_{1}=1}^{k_{1}}x_{s_{1,t_{1}}}\right)\left(\prod_{t_{2}=1}^{k_{2}}y_{s_{2,t_{2}}}\right)\Big|\;g\in\Omega^{(k_{4})},h\in Z^{(k_{3})},\mathscr{I}_{1}(f)={\cal I}_{1},\;

(4.150)

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}_{3}(f)={\cal I}_{3},\;\mbox{ no 3-chains in }\;\mathscr{I}(f),\;\mbox{no $\omega$-2-chains or $\omega$-3-chains in }\;\mathscr{I}(f);\;

\displaystyle 1\leq s_{1,1}\leq\cdots\leq s_{1,k_{1}}\leq n_{1},\;n_{2}+1\leq s_{2,1}\leq\cdots\leq s_{1,k_{2}}\leq n.\Big\}

Set

V_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}^{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3}}=\text{Span}\{G_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}^{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3}}\}.

(4.151)

Note that
k_{2}k_{4}=0
in (
4.4
), otherwise there exists
\omega_{p,q}\mid g
for some
(p,q)\in O
and
\mathscr{I}(f)
contains a
\omega
-2-chain
(s_{k_{2}},0)\prec(n+n_{1}+2+p_{k_{4}},q_{k_{4}})
.

Lemma 4.9
.

For
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}\in\mathbb{N}
,
{\cal I}_{1}\in\widehat{J}_{1}^{k_{1}+k_{3},k_{4}}
and
{\cal I}_{3}\in\widehat{J}_{3}^{k_{2}+k_{3}}
,

\tilde{\phi}\left(G_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}}^{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3}}\right)
is a linearly independent set in
\mathscr{A}
.

Proof.

According to Lemma 3.2 in
[
34
]
, we have kown that
\phi(G_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},0}^{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3}})
is a linearly independent set in
\mathscr{A}
. Hence we suppose that
k_{2}=0
and
k_{4}\geq 0
in the following.

For convenience, we make the following convention:

\omega_{s,t}=-\omega_{t,s}\qquad\mbox{for}\;\;s,t\in J_{1};t\geq s.

(4.152)

In particular,
\omega_{s,s}=0
.
For
i_{1},i_{2}\in J_{1}
with
i_{1}\neq i_{2}
, we define an associative algebra endomorphism
\Gamma_{i_{1},i_{2}}
of
\mathscr{F}
(cf.(
4.129
)) by

\displaystyle\Gamma_{i_{1},i_{2}}(z_{j,i_{1}})=z_{j,i_{2}},\;\Gamma_{i_{1},i_{2}}(z_{j,i^{\prime}})=z_{j,i^{\prime}},\;\Gamma_{i_{1},i_{2}}(\omega_{i_{1},t})=\omega_{i_{2},t},\;

\displaystyle\Gamma_{i_{1},i_{2}}(\omega_{s,i_{1}})=\omega_{s,i_{2}},\;\Gamma_{i_{1},i_{2}}(\omega_{s^{\prime},t^{\prime}})=\omega_{s^{\prime},t^{\prime}}\quad\mbox{for}\;\;j\in J_{3}^{\prime},\;

\displaystyle\;i_{1}\neq i^{\prime}\in\overline{0,n_{1}},\;1\leq t<i_{1}<s\leq n_{1},\;i_{1}\not\in\{s^{\prime},t^{\prime}\}.

(4.153)

For any
{\cal I}\in(\widehat{J}_{1}^{m})
, we denote by

\mathfrak{c}({\cal I})
the number of distinct elements in
{\cal I}
. We want to prove that
\phi(G_{k_{1},0,k_{3},k_{4}}^{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3}})
is linearly dependent by induction on
\mathfrak{c}({\cal I}_{1})+k_{4}
.

First
\mathfrak{c}({\cal I}_{1})+k_{4}\geq 1
by definition.
When
\mathfrak{c}({\cal I}_{1})=1
,
k_{4}=0
. We have kown that
\phi(G_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},0}^{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3}})
is a linearly independent set in
\mathscr{A}
, according to Lemma 3.2 in
[
34
]
. Hence the lemma holds.

Next, we assume the lemma holds for
\mathfrak{c}({\cal I}_{1})+k_{4}<l\in\mathbb{N}
. Consider
\mathfrak{c}({\cal I}_{1})+k_{4}=l
and
k_{4}>0
.

Suppose that
\phi(G_{k_{1},0,k_{3},k_{4}}^{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3}})
is linearly dependent. Then there exists
0\neq f\in V_{k_{1},0,k_{3},k_{4}}^{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3}}
such that
\phi(f)=0
.
Write

f=\sum_{i=1}^{N}a_{i}f_{i}\in V_{k_{1},0,k_{3},k_{4}}^{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3}}\cap\ker\tilde{\phi}

(4.154)

where
f_{i}\in G_{k_{1},0,k_{3},k_{4}}^{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3}}
and
0\neq a_{i}\in\mathbb{F}
for
i\in\overline{1,N}
.
For convenience, we assume that
f_{1},\cdots,f_{N}
are distinct.

Denote

i_{1}=\min\{{\cal I}_{1}\}

(4.155)

and

i_{2}=\min\{i\in{\cal I}_{1}|i>i_{1}\}.

(4.156)

According to (
4.4
), we have

\tilde{\phi}(\Gamma_{i_{1},i_{2}}(f))=\tilde{\phi}(f)|_{x_{i_{1}}=x_{i_{2}},y_{i_{1}}=y_{i_{2}}}=0.

(4.157)

Suppose that
\mathscr{I}\left(\Gamma_{i_{1},i_{2}}(f)\right)
contains a 3-chain
(s_{1},i_{2})\prec(s_{2},t_{2})\prec(s_{3},t_{3})
with
s_{3}\leq n+1
. Then
(s_{1},i_{1})\prec(s_{2},t_{2})\prec(s_{3},t_{3})
is a 3-chain in
\mathscr{I}\left(f\right)
. Hence
\mathscr{I}\left(\Gamma_{i_{1},i_{2}}(f)\right)
does not contain 3-chains.
Suppose that
\mathscr{I}\left(\Gamma_{i_{1},i_{2}}(f)\right)
contains a
\omega
-2-chain
(s_{1},i_{2})\prec(n+n_{1}+2-s_{2},t_{2})
with
s_{2}>t_{2}
. Then
(s_{1},i_{1})\prec(n+n_{1}+2-s_{2},t_{2})
is a
\omega
-2-chain in
\mathscr{I}\left(f\right)
. Hence
\mathscr{I}\left(\Gamma_{i_{1},i_{2}}(f)\right)
does not contain
\omega
-2-chains.

Suppose that
\mathscr{I}\left(\Gamma_{i_{1},i_{2}}(f)\right)
contains a
\omega
-3-chain
\{(s_{1},t_{1}),(s_{2},t_{2}),(n+n_{1}+2-s_{3},t_{3})\}
with
s_{2}>t_{2}
.
There are two cases. First,
t_{1}=i_{2}
, then
t_{3}=i_{2}
. Since
\mathscr{I}\left(f\right)
does not contain
\omega
-2-chains,
\{(s_{1},i_{1}),(n+n_{1}+2-s_{3},i_{2})\}\not\subset\mathscr{I}\left(f\right)
. Then
(n+n_{1}+2-s_{3},i_{1})\in\mathscr{I}\left(f\right)
, otherwise
\{(s_{1},i_{2}),(s_{2},t_{2}),(n+n_{1}+2-s_{3},i_{2})\}
is a
\omega
-3-chain in
\mathscr{I}\left(f\right)
. However, whether it is
\{(s_{1},i_{2}),(s_{2},t_{2}),(n+n_{1}+2-s_{3},i_{1})\}
or
\{(s_{1},i_{1}),(s_{2},t_{2}),(n+n_{1}+2-s_{3},i_{1})\}
in
\mathscr{I}\left(f\right)
, the are both
\omega
-3-chains. Hence
\mathscr{I}\left(\Gamma_{i_{1},i_{2}}(f)\right)
does not contain
\omega
-3-chains.

Furthermore, we assume that
{\cal I}_{1}={\cal I}^{\prime}_{1}\cup\{i_{1}\cdots,i_{1}\}
with
i_{1}\not\in{\cal I}^{\prime}_{1}
. Denote
{\cal I}_{1}^{\sharp}=\mathscr{I}_{1}(\Gamma_{i_{1},i_{2}}(f_{i}))
, then we have
{\cal I}_{1}^{\sharp}={\cal I}^{\prime}_{1}\cup\{i_{2},\cdots,i_{2}\}
and
\mathscr{I}_{3}(\Gamma_{i_{1},i_{2}}(f_{i}))={\cal I}_{3}
for
i\in\overline{1,N}
.

Therefore,
\Gamma_{i_{1},i_{2}}(f)\in V_{k_{1},0,k_{3},k_{4}}^{{\cal I}_{1}^{\sharp},{\cal I}_{3}}
.

Note that

\mathfrak{c}({\cal I}_{1}^{\sharp})=\mathfrak{c}({\cal I}_{1})-1.

(4.158)

and

\mathfrak{c}({\cal I}_{1}^{\sharp})+\deg_{\Omega}(\Gamma_{i_{1},i_{2}}(f))=\mathfrak{c}({\cal I}_{1})+k_{4}-1<l.

(4.159)

By inductional assumption,
\phi(G_{k_{1},0,k_{3},k_{4}}^{{\cal I}_{1}^{\sharp},{\cal I}_{3}})
is linearly independent.
This yields

\Gamma_{i_{1},i_{2}}(f)=0.

(4.160)

Write

f_{i}=f_{i,i_{1}}f_{i,i_{2}}f_{i}^{\prime},

(4.161)

where
f_{i,i_{1}}
are monomials in
\mathbb{F}[z_{j,i_{1}},\omega_{j^{\prime},i_{1}}|n_{2}+1\leq j\leq n+1,i_{1}<j^{\prime}\leq n_{1}]
,
f_{i,i_{2}}
are monomials in
\mathbb{F}[z_{j,i_{2}},\omega_{j^{\prime},i_{2}}|n_{2}+1\leq j\leq n+1,i_{2}<j^{\prime}\leq n_{1}]
and
i_{1},i_{2}\not\in\mathscr{I}_{1}(f_{i}^{\prime})
.

Denote

I_{z,z}=\langle z_{s,i_{1}}z_{s^{\prime},i_{2}}-z_{s^{\prime},i_{1}}z_{s,i_{2}}|s<s^{\prime}\in J_{3}^{\prime}\rangle,

(4.162)

I_{\omega,\omega}=\langle\omega_{j,i_{1}}\omega_{j^{\prime},i_{2}}-\omega_{j^{\prime},i_{1}}\omega_{j,i_{2}}|i_{2}<j<j^{\prime}\leq n_{1}\rangle,

(4.163)

I_{\omega,z}=\langle\omega_{j,i_{1}}z_{s,i_{2}}-\omega_{j,i_{2}}z_{s,i_{1}}|s\in J_{3}^{\prime},i_{2}<j\leq n_{1}\rangle

(4.164)

are ideals of
\mathscr{F}
.

Since there exits
c_{1},c_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
c_{1}=\deg(f_{i,i_{1}})
and
c_{2}=\deg(f_{i,i_{2}})
for
i\in\overline{1,N}
, we have

f\in I_{z,z}+I_{\omega,\omega}+I_{\omega,z}+\langle\omega_{i_{2},i_{1}}\rangle

(4.165)

by Lemma
4.7
.

Write

f=F_{1}+F_{2}+F_{3}+F_{4},\;\;F_{1}\in I_{z,z},\;F_{2}\in I_{\omega,\omega},\;F_{3}\in I_{\omega,z},F_{4}\in\langle\omega_{i_{2},i_{1}}\rangle.

(4.166)

For
i=1,2,3,4
, we write

F_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{m_{i}}F_{i,j}

(4.167)

where
F_{i,j}
are monomials in
\mathscr{F}
, and
\omega_{i_{2},i_{1}}\not|F_{i^{\prime},j}
for
i^{\prime}=1,2,3
,
j\in\overline{1,m_{i^{\prime}}}
.

Suppose that
F_{1}\neq 0
, then there exist
j\in\overline{1,m_{i^{\prime}}}
,
s_{1}<s_{2}\in J_{3}
such that
z_{s_{1},i_{1}}z_{s_{2},i_{2}}|F_{1,j}
. Note that
k_{4}>0
and
\omega_{i_{2},i_{1}}\not|F_{1,j}
, there exists
t_{0}\in\overline{i_{2}+1,n_{1}}
, such that
\omega_{t_{0},i_{1}}|F_{1,j}
.
Hence
\mathscr{I}(F_{j,1})
contains a
\omega
-3-chain
\{(s_{1},i_{1}),(s_{2},i_{2}),(n+n_{1}+2-t_{0},i_{1})\}
, which leads to a contradiction. Therefore,
F_{1}=0
.

Similarly, suppose that
F_{2}\neq 0
or
F_{3}\neq 0
, it can be deduced that
\mathscr{I}(F_{2,j})
or
\mathscr{I}(F_{3,j})
has a
\omega
-2-chain for some
j
, respectively. Then
F_{2}=F_{3}=0
.

Hence
\omega_{i_{2},i_{1}}|f_{i}
for each
i\in\overline{1,N}
and

\frac{f}{w_{i_{2},i_{1}}}\in\;V_{k_{1},0,k_{3},k_{4}-1}^{{\cal I}_{1}\setminus\{i_{1},i_{2}\},{\cal I}_{3}}\cap\ker\tilde{\phi}.

(4.168)

By the inductional assumption,
\frac{f}{w_{i_{2},i_{1}}}=0
. Then
f=0
.
Therefore,
\phi(G_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}}^{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3}})
is linearly independent.

∎

Proposition 4.3
.

For
k\geq 1
,
|S_{k}^{\prime}|=\dim(M_{k}/M_{k-1})
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.8
,
\text{Span}\{\tilde{\phi}(S^{\prime}_{k})\}=W_{k}^{\prime}
for
k\geq 0
.

We suppose that
f\in\text{Span}\{S_{k}^{\prime}\}\cap\ker\tilde{\phi}
. Since

S_{k}^{\prime}=\bigcup_{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3}}G_{m_{1},0,k,m_{2}}^{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3}},

(4.169)

we may assume that

f=\sum_{{\cal I}_{1}\in\widehat{J}_{1}^{k_{1}+k_{3},k_{4}},{\cal I}_{3}\in\widehat{J}_{3}^{k_{2}+k_{3}}}f_{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3}}\;,\;\;\tilde{\phi}(f_{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3}})=\sum_{j}f^{\prime}_{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3},j},

(4.170)

where
f_{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3}}\in V_{m_{1},0,k,m_{2}}^{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3}}
and
f^{\prime}_{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3},j}
are monomials in
\mathscr{A}
.
Since
\mathscr{I}_{1}(f^{\prime}_{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3},j})={\cal I}_{1}
and
\mathscr{I}_{3}(f^{\prime}_{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3},j})={\cal I}_{3}
for each
j
, we have
f_{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3}}\in\ker\tilde{\phi}
for each
{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3}
in (
4.170
).

According to Lemma
4.9
,
f_{{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3}}=0
for each
{\cal I}_{1},{\cal I}_{3}
in (
4.170
). Then
f=0
. That is,
\text{Span}\{S_{k}^{\prime}\}\cap\ker\tilde{\phi}=\{0\}
.
Hence
\tilde{\phi}(S^{\prime}_{k})
is a basis of
W_{k}^{\prime}
.

We have known that
W^{\prime}_{k}\oplus M_{k-1}=M_{k}
(cf. (
4.125
)).
Then
|S_{k}^{\prime}|=\dim(M_{k}/M_{k-1})
, for
k\geq 1
.
∎

For
p,q\in\mathbb{N}
and
p\geq q>0
, we denote

O_{p,q}=\{(s,t)|1\leq s\leq p-t,1\leq t\leq q\}

(4.171)

as a ladder in
\{(s,t)|1\leq s\leq p,1\leq t\leq q\}
.
Denote

P_{3}(p,q,r)=\left|\left\{\prod_{j=1}^{r}\omega_{s_{j},t_{j}}\,\Big|\,(s_{j},t_{j})\in O_{p,q}\;\mbox{for}\;j\in\overline{1,r}\right\}\right|.

(4.172)

Then
P_{3}
is the Hilbert polynomial of the ladder-determinantal ideal, which can be calculated using formulas in
[
24
,
11
,
23
]
.
In particular,
P_{3}(p,1,r)=\binom{p+r-2}{r}
for
p>1
.

For
f=ghf^{\prime}\in S_{k}^{\prime}
with
g\in\Omega^{(m_{2})}
,
h\in Z^{(k)}
,
f^{\prime}=\prod_{i=1}^{n_{1}}x_{i}^{\alpha_{r}}
.
Since
\mathscr{I}(g)
does not contain
\omega
-2-chains, we may assume that

g=\prod_{i=1}^{m_{2}}\omega_{s_{i},t_{i}}

(4.173)

with
1\leq s_{m_{2}}<\cdots<s_{1}\leq n_{1}
and
1\leq t_{m_{2}}<\cdots<t_{1}\leq n_{1}
.
Then the number of
g
satisfying

s_{1}=\max\{s\in J_{1}\big|\omega_{s,t}|g\;\text{for some }t<s\},\;t_{1}=\max\{t\in J_{1}\big|\omega_{s,t}|g\;\text{for some }s>t\}

(4.174)

is

P_{3}(s_{1},t_{1},m_{2}-1).

(4.175)

(1) Suppose that
\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=0
, or equivalently,
m_{1}=0
.
Then
f^{\prime}=1
.

For
i\in\overline{1,t_{1}-1}
, we have
i\not\in\mathscr{I}_{1}(h)
. Otherwise, there exists
j\in J_{3}
, such that
z_{j,i}|h
. Then
(j,i)\prec(n+n_{1}+2-s_{1},t_{1})
is a
\omega
-2-chain in
\mathscr{I}(f)
.

We write

h=\prod_{(j,i)\in J_{3}\times J_{1}}z_{j,i}^{\gamma_{j,i}}.

(4.176)

For
h
in (
4.176
) and
(i^{\prime},i)\in O
, we denote

h|_{i,i^{\prime}}=\prod_{(j,i)\in J_{3}\times\overline{i,i^{\prime}}}z_{j,i}^{\gamma_{j,i}}

(4.177)

Suppose that
\mathscr{I}\left(h|_{t_{1},s_{1}-1}\right)
contains a 2-chain
(j_{1},i_{1})\prec(j_{2},i_{2})
.
Then
\mathscr{I}(f)
contains a
\omega
-3-chain
\{(j_{1},i_{1}),(j_{2},i_{2}),(n+n_{1}+2-s_{1},t_{1})\}
since
s_{1}>i_{2}
. Hence
\mathscr{I}\left(h|_{t_{1},s_{1}-1}\right)
does not contain 2-chains.

At the same time, for
g\in\Omega^{(m_{2})}
with (
4.174
) holding and no 2-chains in
\mathscr{I}(g)
, if
h\in Z^{k}
satisfying
\overline{1,t_{1}-1}\cap\mathscr{I}_{1}(h)=\emptyset
, no 2-chains in
\mathscr{I}\left(h|_{t_{1},s_{1}-1}\right)
and no 3-chains in
\mathscr{I}(h)
. Thus
hg\in S_{k}^{\prime}
.

Therefore, for
g\in\Omega^{(m_{2})}
with (
4.174
) holding, the number of
h\in Z^{k}
is

P_{1}(n-n_{1},n_{1}-t_{1}+1,s_{1}-t_{1}+1,k).

(4.178)

Then

\displaystyle|S_{k}|=\sum_{(s_{1},t_{1})\in O}P_{1}(n-n_{1},n_{1}-t_{1}+1,s_{1}-t_{1}+1,k)P_{3}(s_{1},t_{1},m_{2}-1).

(4.179)

(2) Suppose that
\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}<0
, then
m_{1}>0
.
Denote

i_{1}=\max\{i\in J_{1}|\alpha_{i}>0\}\in J_{1}.

(4.180)

and

\displaystyle i_{2}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\max\{i\in\overline{t_{1},s_{1}-1}|\alpha_{i}>0\},&\text{ if }\{i\in\overline{t_{1},s_{1}-1}|\alpha_{i}>0\}\neq\emptyset;\\
&t_{1}-1,&\text{ if }\{i\in\overline{t_{1},s_{1}-1}|\alpha_{i}>0\}=\emptyset.\end{aligned}\right.

(4.181)

For
i^{\prime}\in\mathscr{I}_{1}(f^{\prime})
, we have
i^{\prime}\geq t_{1}
, otherwise
\mathscr{I}(f)
cantains a
\omega
-2-chain
(n+1,i^{\prime})\prec(n+n_{1}+2-s_{1},t_{1})
.

Set the function

\displaystyle L(i_{1},i_{2},s_{1},t_{1})=\begin{cases}\binom{i_{1}+i_{2}-s_{1}-t_{1}+m_{1}-1}{m_{1}-2}&\text{ if }i_{1}\geq s_{1}\text{ and }t_{1}\leq i_{2},\\
\binom{i_{1}-s_{1}+m_{1}-1}{m_{1}-1}&\text{ if }i_{1}\geq s_{1}\text{ and }i_{2}-t_{1}+1=0,\\
\binom{i_{2}-t_{1}+m_{1}-1}{m_{1}-1}&\text{ if }i_{1}=i_{2}\text{ and }t_{1}\leq i_{2},\\
0&\text{ else.}\end{cases}

(4.182)

Then the number of
f^{\prime}
satisfying (
4.180
) adn (
4.181
) is

\displaystyle L(i_{1},i_{2},s_{1},t_{1})

(4.183)

For
s_{1},t_{1},i_{1},i_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
, we denote

p_{0}=\max\{i_{2},t_{1}\},\quad q_{0}=\max\{i_{1},s_{1}\}\geq p_{0}.

(4.184)

We suppose that
\overline{1,t_{1}-1}\cap\mathscr{I}_{1}(h)\neq\emptyset
and
(j,i^{\prime})\in J_{3}\times\overline{1,t_{1}-1}\cap\mathscr{I}(h)
. Then
\mathscr{I}(f)
cantains a
\omega
-2-chain
(j,i^{\prime})\prec(n+n_{1}+2-s_{1},t_{1})
.
We suppose that
i_{2}>t_{1}

\overline{1,i_{2}-1}\cap\mathscr{I}_{1}(h)\neq\emptyset
and
(j,i^{\prime})\in J_{3}\times\overline{1,i_{2}-1}\cap\mathscr{I}(h)
. Then
\mathscr{I}(f)
cantains a
\omega
-3-chain
\{(j,i^{\prime}),(n+1,i_{2}),(n+n_{1}+2-s_{1},t_{1})\}
. Hence
\overline{1,p_{0}-1}\cap\mathscr{I}_{1}(h)=\emptyset
.

Suppose that
\mathscr{I}\left(h|_{p_{0},i_{1}-1}\right)
contains a 2-chain
(j,i)\prec(j^{\prime},i^{\prime})
. Then
\mathscr{I}(f)
contains a 3-chain
(j,i)\prec(j^{\prime},i^{\prime})\prec(n+1,i_{1})
.
Suppose that
\mathscr{I}\left(h|_{p_{0},s_{1}-1}\right)
contains a 2-chain
(j,i)\prec(j^{\prime},i^{\prime})
. Then
\mathscr{I}(f)
contains a
\omega
-3-chain
(j,i)\prec(j^{\prime},i^{\prime})\prec(n+n_{1}+2-s_{1},t_{1})
.
Hence
\mathscr{I}\left(h|_{p_{0},q_{0}-1}\right)
does not cotain 2-chains.

On the contrary, as long as
h
satisfies
\overline{1,p_{0}-1}\cap\mathscr{I}_{1}(h)=\emptyset
, no 2-chains in
\mathscr{I}\left(h|_{p_{0},q_{0}-1}\right)
and no 3-chains in
\mathscr{I}(h)
,
ghf^{\prime}\in S_{k}^{\prime}
holds.

Therefore, for
g\in\Omega^{(m_{2})}
with (
4.174
) holding and
f^{\prime}
satisfying (
4.180
) and (
4.181
), the number of
h\in Z^{(k)}
is

P_{1}(n-n_{1},n_{1}-p_{0}+1,q_{0}-p_{0}+1,k).

(4.185)

Therefore

\displaystyle|S_{k}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{(s_{1},t_{1})\in O}\sum_{i_{1}=t_{1}}^{n_{1}}\sum_{i_{2}=t_{1}-1}^{\min\{i_{1},s_{1}-1\}}L(i_{1},i_{2},s_{1},t_{1})P_{3}(s_{1},t_{1},m_{2}-1)

(4.186)

\displaystyle\,P_{1}(n-n_{1},n_{1}-\max\{i_{2},t_{1}\}+1,\max\{i_{1},s_{1}\}-\max\{i_{2},t_{1}\}+1,k).

In summary, we have

Lemma 4.10
.

When
1<n_{1}=n_{2}<n
,
\ell_{1}=-m_{1}-m_{2}
and
\ell_{2}=m_{2}
with
m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}}(k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{(s_{1},t_{1})\in O}\sum_{i_{1}=t_{1}}^{n_{1}}\sum_{i_{2}=t_{1}-1}^{\min\{i_{1},s_{1}-1\}}L(i_{1},i_{2},s_{1},t_{1})P_{3}(s_{1},t_{1},m_{2}-1)

(4.187)

\displaystyle P_{1}(n-n_{1},n_{1}-\max\{i_{2},t_{1}\}+1,\max\{i_{1},s_{1}\}-\max\{i_{2},t_{1}\}+1,k).\qquad\;

when
m_{1}>0
.

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}}(k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{(s_{1},t_{1})\in O}P_{1}(n-n_{1},n_{1}-t_{1}+1,s_{1}-t_{1}+1,k)P_{3}(s_{1},t_{1},m_{2}-1).\qquad

(4.188)

when
m_{1}=0
.

Corollary 4.3
.

When
1<n_{1}=n_{2}<n
,
\ell_{1}<0
and
\ell_{2}>0
, we have

\displaystyle lc(M)=\begin{cases}1-\ell_{1}-\ell_{2}&\text{ if }n_{1}<n-1,\\
\binom{n+\ell_{2}-3}{\ell_{2}}&\text{ if }\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=0\text{ and }n_{1}=n-1,\\
\binom{n-\ell_{1}-2}{-\ell_{1}}&\text{ if }\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}>0\text{ and }n_{1}=n-1.\end{cases}

(4.189)

Proof.

(1) When
|J_{3}|>1
and
m_{1}=0
, we have
n_{1}-t_{1}+1>1
for
(s_{1},t_{1})\in 0
, and then

\displaystyle\deg_{k}(P_{1}(n-n_{1},n_{1}-t_{1}+1,s_{1}-t_{1}+1,k))\leq 2n-s_{1}-t_{1}-2\leq 2n-5

(4.190)

by Corollary
2.1
.
Moreover,

\deg_{k}(P_{1}(n-n_{1},n_{1}-t_{1}+1,s_{1}-t_{1}+1,k))=2n-5

(4.191)

if and only if
s_{1}=2
and
t_{1}=1
.
Hence

\displaystyle lc_{k}(\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}}(k))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{3}(2,1,m_{2}-1)lc_{k}(P_{1}(n-n_{1},n_{1},2,k))

(4.192)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle lc_{k}(h_{3}(n-n_{1},n_{1},k))=lc_{k}(\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)).

according to (
4.188
).

(2) When
|J_{3}|>1
and
m_{1}>0
, we have
n_{1}-\max\{i_{2},t_{1}\}+1>1
since
t_{1}<n_{1}
and
i_{2}<n_{1}
.
According to Corollary
2.1
,

\displaystyle\deg_{k}(P_{1}(n-n_{1},n_{1}-\max\{i_{2},t_{1}\}+1,\max\{i_{1},s_{1}\}-\max\{i_{2},t_{1}\}+1,k))

(4.193)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2n-\max\{i_{2},t_{1}\}-\max\{i_{1},s_{1}\}-2\leq 2n-5

Moreover,

\deg_{k}(P_{1}(n-n_{1},n_{1}-\max\{i_{2},t_{1}\}+1,\max\{i_{1},s_{1}\}-\max\{i_{2},t_{1}\}+1,k))=2n-5

(4.194)

if and only if
s_{1}=2
,
t_{1}=1
,
i_{1}\in\{1,2\}
and
i_{2}\in\{0,1\}
.
Note that
P_{3}(2,1,m_{2}-1)=1
, then

\displaystyle lc_{k}(\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}}(k))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{2}\sum_{i_{2}=0}^{1}L(i_{1},i_{2},2,1)

(4.195)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+m_{1}

by (
4.187
).

(3) When
|J_{3}|=1
and
m_{1}=0
, we have
n_{1}=n-1
and

P_{1}(n-n_{1},n_{1}-t_{1}+1,s_{1}-t_{1}+1,k)=\binom{n-t_{1}+k-1}{k}.

(4.196)

Hence

\displaystyle lc_{k}(\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}}(k))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n-2)!}\sum_{s_{1}=2}^{n-1}P_{3}(s_{1},1,m_{2}-1)

(4.197)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n-2)!}\binom{n+m_{2}-3}{m_{2}}=\binom{n+m_{2}-3}{m_{2}}lc_{k}(\mathfrak{p}_{M}(k)).

(4) When
|J_{3}|=1
and
m_{1}>0
, we have
n_{1}=n-1
and

\displaystyle P_{1}(n-n_{1},n_{1}-\max\{i_{2},t_{1}\}+1,\max\{i_{1},s_{1}\}-\max\{i_{2},t_{1}\}+1,k)

(4.198)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\binom{n-\max\{i_{2},t_{1}\}+k-1}{k}.

Hence

\displaystyle lc_{k}(\mathfrak{p}_{M,V_{0}}(k))

(4.199)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n-2)!}\sum_{s_{1}=2}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n-1}\sum_{i_{2}=0}^{1}L(i_{1},i_{2},s_{1},1)P_{3}(s_{1},1,m_{2}-1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n-2)!}\sum_{s_{1}=2}^{n}\left(1+\sum_{i_{1}=s_{1}}^{n-1}\left(L(i_{1},0,s_{1},1)+L(i_{1},1,s_{1},1)\right)\right)\binom{s_{1}+m_{2}-3}{m_{2}-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n-2)!}\sum_{s_{1}=2}^{n}\left(1+\sum_{i_{1}=s_{1}}^{n-1}\binom{i_{1}-s_{1}+m_{1}}{m_{1}-1}\right)\binom{s_{1}+m_{2}-3}{m_{2}-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n-2)!}\sum_{s_{1}=2}^{n}\binom{s_{1}+m_{2}-3}{m_{2}-1}\binom{n-s_{1}+m_{1}}{m_{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(n-2)!}\binom{n+m_{1}+m_{2}-2}{m_{1}+m_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\binom{n-\ell_{1}-2}{-\ell_{1}}lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M})

by (
4.182
) and Lemma
2.4
-(5).

∎

In summary, we have the following theorem:

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}=sl(n)
and
M={\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}
be an infinite-dimensional
\mathfrak{g}
-module, under the conditions stated in Lemma
2.1
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\frac{lc(M)}{lc(\mathfrak{p}_{M})}=\begin{cases}1&\text{ if }n_{1}<n_{2}<n\text{ or }\ell_{1}=\ell_{2}=0;\\
1-\ell_{1}-\ell_{2}&\text{ if }1<n_{1}=n_{2}<n-1;\\
\binom{n+\ell_{2}-2}{\ell_{2}}&\text{ if }n_{1}<n_{2}=n;\\
\binom{n-\ell_{2}-2}{-\ell_{2}}&\text{ if }1=n_{1}=n_{2}<n,\ell_{2}\leq 0\text{ and }\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}<0;\\
\binom{n-\ell_{1}-2}{-\ell_{1}}&\text{ if }1<n_{1}=n_{2}=n-1,\ell_{1}\leq 0\text{ and }\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}<0;\\
\binom{n+\ell_{1}-3}{\ell_{1}}&\text{ if }1=n_{1}=n_{2}<n-1\text{ and }\ell_{2}=-\ell_{1}<0;\\
\binom{n+\ell_{2}-3}{\ell_{2}}&\text{ if }1<n_{1}=n_{2}=n-1\text{ and }\ell_{1}=-\ell_{2}<0.\end{cases}

(4.200)

Proof.

When
n_{1}=n_{2}
, similar to (
4.3
), we define an associative algebra isomorphism
\Psi^{\prime}:\mathscr{A}\rightarrow\mathscr{A}
by

\displaystyle\Psi^{\prime}(x_{i})=y_{n-i+1},\;\;\Psi^{\prime}(y_{i})=x_{n-i+1},\qquad i\in\overline{1,n}.

(4.201)

Similar to (
4.3
)-(
4.29
),
\Psi^{\prime}:{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}^{n_{1},n_{1},n}\rightarrow{\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{2},\ell_{1}\rangle}^{n-n_{1},n-n_{1},n}
is also a linear space isomorphism. And we have

\dim\left(({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}^{n_{1},n_{1},n})_{k}\right)=\dim\left(({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{2},\ell_{1}\rangle}^{n-n_{1},n-n_{1},n})_{k}\right)

(4.202)

for
k\geq 0
.
Then
lc\left({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\rangle}^{n_{1},n_{1},n}\right)=lc\left({\mathscr{H}}_{\langle\ell_{2},\ell_{1}\rangle}^{n-n_{1},n-n_{1},n}\right)
.
When
n_{1}=n_{2}<n-1
,
\ell_{1}>0
and
\ell_{2}<0
, we have

\displaystyle lc(M)=\begin{cases}1-\ell_{1}-\ell_{2}&\text{ if }n_{1}>1,\\
\binom{n+\ell_{1}-3}{\ell_{1}}&\text{ if }\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}=0\text{ and }n_{1}=1,\\
\binom{n-\ell_{2}-2}{-\ell_{2}}&\text{ if }\ell_{1}+\ell_{2}>0\text{ and }n_{1}=1.\end{cases}

(4.203)

according to Corollary
4.3
.

For other cases, Lemma
4.1
, Lemma
4.2
, Corollary
4.2
and Corollary
4.3
tell us that (
4.200
) holds.
∎

This completes the proof of Theorem 3 that we stated in the introduction.
